### [1] [Symmetry analysis and exact solutions of multi-layer quasi-geostrophic problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26959)
*Serhii D. Koval,Alex Bihlo,Roman O. Popovych*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs an extended symmetry analysis of the multi-layer quasi-geostrophic (QG) model, deriving its conservation laws, Hamiltonian structure, Lie invariance algebra, and full point-symmetry pseudogroup. Through systematic Lie reduction, it uncovers linear invariant submodels (e.g., Helmholtz, Klein-Gordon, Bessel equations), whose solvability hinges on spectral properties of the vertical coupling matrix. It constructs broad families of exact solutions—including Rossby waves, hetons, and dipolar vortices—and validates them against real oceanographic data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically uncover exact solutions and structural properties (symmetries, conservation laws, Hamiltonian formulation) of the general multi-layer quasi-geostrophic model—a fundamental PDE system for geophysical fluid dynamics—whose complexity has hindered analytical progress.

Method: Extended Lie symmetry analysis: computation of maximal Lie invariance algebra and complete point-symmetry pseudogroup; classification of low-dimensional subalgebras; codimension-one to -three Lie reductions; spectral analysis of the vertical coupling matrix; derivation of conservation laws and Hamiltonian structure via geometric and variational methods.

Result: First complete description of conservation laws and Hamiltonian structure for arbitrary-layer QG; explicit maximal Lie algebra and full pseudogroup; reduction to linear PDEs (Helmholtz, Klein-Gordon, Bessel, etc.); exact solution families for Rossby waves, hetons, baroclinic eddies, and dipolar vortices; physical validation using three-layer ocean data.

Conclusion: The multi-layer QG system admits rich symmetry structure enabling exact solvability in physically relevant regimes; spectral properties of vertical coupling critically govern integrability and solution types; the derived exact solutions provide rigorous benchmarks and interpretive frameworks for observed geophysical phenomena.

Abstract: We carry out an extended symmetry analysis of the multi-layer quasi-geostrophic problem. This model is given by a system of an arbitrary number of coupled barotropic vorticity equations. Conservation laws and a Hamiltonian structure for the general case of the model are correctly described for the first time. Using original methods, we compute the maximal Lie invariance algebra and the complete point-symmetry pseudogroup of the model. After classifying one- and two-dimensional subalgebras of the Lie invariance algebra, we exhaustively study codimension-one, -two and -three Lie reductions. Notably, among invariant submodels of the original nonlinear model, we obtain uncoupled systems of well-known linear equations, including the Helmholtz, modified Helmholtz, Laplace, Klein-Gordon, Whittaker, Bessel and linearized Benjamin-Bona-Mahony equations. Integration of these systems significantly depends on spectral properties of the model's vertical coupling matrix, which we also revisit in detail. As a result, we construct wide families of exact solutions, including rediscovered representations of stationary and travelling baroclinic Rossby waves, coherent baroclinic eddies, hetons and localized dipolar vortices. We illustrate the physical relevance of obtained solutions using real-world geophysical data for a three-layer ocean model.

</details>


### [2] [Semiclassical shape resonances for magnetic Stark Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27196)
*Kentaro Kameoka,Naoya Yoshida*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a one-to-one correspondence between shape resonances of 2D magnetic Stark Hamiltonians and eigenvalues of a reference operator in the semiclassical limit, under constant magnetic field and perturbed linear potential with wells; it establishes Weyl law and asymptotics for resonance real parts near well bottoms, using complex distortion via complex translation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously analyze shape resonances in two-dimensional magnetic Stark systems in the semiclassical regime, where a constant magnetic field couples with a scalar potential that is a perturbation of a linear potential—particularly when potential wells exist, enabling resonance quantization and spectral asymptotics.

Method: Uses complex scaling (complex translation outside a compact set) to define a complex-distorted Hamiltonian whose complex eigenvalues correspond to shape resonances; constructs and analyzes a reference operator (e.g., harmonic oscillator-type) associated with the potential well; applies semiclassical microlocal analysis and spectral theory to establish bijection and derive asymptotics.

Result: Proves a bijective correspondence between shape resonances and discrete eigenvalues of a reference operator; derives the Weyl law for resonance counting; obtains leading-order asymptotics for the real parts of resonances near the bottom of a potential well.

Conclusion: Shape resonances of the magnetic Stark Hamiltonian are semiclassically quantized by the eigenvalues of an effective well Hamiltonian, confirming their regular distribution and providing precise asymptotic localization in the complex energy plane.

Abstract: We study shape resonances of two-dimensional magnetic Stark Hamiltonians in the semiclassical limit. The magnetic field is assumed to be constant and the scalar potential is a perturbation of a linear potential. Under the assumption that the scalar potential has potential wells, the existence of a one-to-one correspondence between shape resonances of the Hamiltonian and discrete eigenvalues of a certain reference operator is proved. This implies the Weyl law for the number of resonances and the asymptotic behavior of the real parts of resonances near the bottom of a potential well. Resonances are studied as complex eigenvalues of complex distorted Hamiltonians, which is defined by the complex translation outside a compact set.

</details>


### [3] [Quantum Hall States response to toroidal geometry deformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27319)
*Bruno Mera,José M. Mourão,João P. Nunes,Carolina Paiva*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies geometric quantization and complex-time Hamiltonian evolution to study how integer and fractional quantum Hall states (specifically Laughlin states) respond to toroidal geometry deformations—both flat (non-periodic quadratic Hamiltonians) and non-flat Kähler (bi-periodic, τ-preserving Mabuchi geodesics ending at curvature singularities); it introduces generalized coherent state transforms (gCST) as a novel tool for tracking state evolution, especially under S¹-invariant deformations where analytic solutions up to singularity are obtained.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric response of quantum Hall states—particularly Laughlin states—to controlled deformations of the underlying toroidal background geometry, bridging quantum Hall physics with Kähler geometry and geometric quantization; flat deformations serve as a benchmark, while non-flat Kähler deformations probe deeper connections to Mabuchi geodesics and metric singularities.

Method: Uses complex-time Hamiltonian evolution to induce geometric deformations on the torus, combined with generalized coherent state transforms (gCST) to track the evolution of Laughlin wavefunctions; distinguishes two deformation classes: (1) flat toroidal deformations governed by non-periodic, quadratic translation-generator Hamiltonians; (2) non-flat Kähler deformations generated by global, bi-periodic Hamiltonians inducing τ-preserving Mabuchi geodesics in the space of Kähler metrics; restricts to S¹-invariant cases for explicit analytic solutions.

Result: Derives explicit analytic expressions for both toroidal geometry evolution and Laughlin state evolution under S¹-invariant non-flat Kähler deformations, all the way to a finite-time curvature singularity; confirms consistency of gCST approach for flat deformations and establishes its utility as a geometric quantization tool for quantum Hall systems on deformed tori.

Conclusion: Geometric quantization via gCST and complex-time evolution provides a powerful and novel framework to analyze quantum Hall responses to geometric deformations; non-flat Kähler deformations reveal deep links to Mabuchi geometry and singularity formation, suggesting that quantum Hall states encode fine-grained information about the Kähler moduli space and its boundary structure.

Abstract: In this paper, we apply techniques of geometric quantization to study the response of the integer and fractional quantum Hall effects to toroidal geometry deformation. The main method is that of using complex time Hamiltonian evolution to induce the geometry change and then the associated generalized coherent state transforms (gCST) to find the evolution of the Laughlin states. We consider two kinds of deformations. The first are flat toroidal deformations. Although Laughlin states for all flat toroidal geometries have been thoroughly studied before, we believe that our approach via the gCST is novel. It also serves as a testing ground to study the non-flat Kähler deformations. The Hamiltonians used in the flat deformations are quadratic in the generators of translations and therefore non periodic. The second kind of deformations involve nonflat Kähler toroidal deformations, generated by global, thus bi-periodic, Hamiltonians on the torus. The corresponding imaginary time flows are (elliptic curve modulus) $τ$-preserving Mabuchi geodesics in the space of Kähler metrics on the torus, hitting a curvature singularity in finite imaginary time. By restricting to $S^1$-invariant deformations we find explicit analytic expressions for the evolution of the toroidal geometry and of the Laughlin states all the way to the singularity.

</details>


### [4] [The Full Set of KMS-States for Abelian Kitaev Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27436)
*Danilo Polo Ojito,Emil Prodan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a C*-algebraic framework for abelian Kitaev models by identifying the vertex/face operator subalgebra as a C*-diagonal of the quasilocal observable UHF algebra; it then uses the associated Weyl groupoid and a Hamiltonian-induced 1-cocycle to classify all KMS states, proving uniqueness at all inverse temperatures β ≥ 0 and showing the zero-temperature limit recovers the unique frustration-free ground state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous C*-algebraic characterization of thermal and ground states in abelian Kitaev models, leveraging structural tools (C*-diagonals, groupoid C*-algebras, KMS theory) to go beyond traditional finite-volume or perturbative approaches.

Method: Proving that the vertex/face operator subalgebra C is a C*-diagonal of the UHF algebra A of quasilocal observables; constructing the Weyl groupoid G_C encoding the inclusion C ↪ A; realizing A as a groupoid C*-algebra; defining the dynamics via a groupoid 1-cocycle c_H induced by the Hamiltonian; analyzing (c_H, β)-KMS measures on G_C to classify KMS states.

Result: Full classification and uniqueness of KMS states for all β ∈ [0, ∞); existence and identification of the β → ∞ limit as the unique frustration-free ground state.

Conclusion: The abelian Kitaev model admits a complete, canonical description in terms of a groupoid C*-algebra with dynamics governed by a 1-cocycle, enabling exact and model-independent characterization of its thermal equilibrium and ground state structure.

Abstract: We first prove that the subalgebra $\mathcal{C}$ generated by the vertex and face operators of an abelian Kitaev model is a $C^\ast$-diagonal of the UHF algebra $\mathcal{A}$ of quasilocal observables. This gives us access to the Weyl groupoid $\mathcal{G}_\mathcal{C}$ associated with the $C^\ast$-inclusion $\mathcal{C} \hookrightarrow \mathcal{A}$, which supplies a valuable presentation of $\mathcal{A}$ as a groupoid $C^\ast$-algebra where the dynamics of the model are generated by a groupoid 1-cocycle $c_H$. Making appeal to the notion of $(c_H,β)$-KMS measures for this groupoid, we identify the full set of KMS states of the model and prove its uniqueness for $β\in [0,\infty)$. Furthermore, we show that its limit at $β\rightarrow \infty$ exists and coincides with the unique frustration-free ground state of the model.

</details>


### [5] [Pseudo-magnetism in a strained discrete honeycomb lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27503)
*Xuenan Li,Michael I. Weinstein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives an effective magnetic Dirac Hamiltonian for wave packets in slowly strained honeycomb media, proves existence and characterizes exponentially localized edge states under armchair-preserving unidirectional deformations (analogous to Landau levels), and shows absence of such localization for zigzag-preserving deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous mathematical foundation for pseudo-magnetic field effects and strain-induced topological edge states in honeycomb lattices beyond the graphene context, particularly distinguishing between armchair- and zigzag-symmetric deformations.

Method: Starting from a discrete tight-binding model, the authors perform a multiscale asymptotic expansion to derive a continuum effective Dirac Hamiltonian; they then apply spectral theory and perturbation analysis to prove existence and characterize the structure of transversely localized modes for bounded-gradient unidirectional strains preserving armchair translation invariance.

Result: Proof of existence of pseudo-periodic (plane-wave) + exponentially localized modes under armchair-preserving strain; derivation of their leading-order multiscale structure; identification of transverse localization governed by a 1D effective Dirac operator; demonstration that these modes form nearly flat bands (Landau-level-like spectrum); numerical confirmation and contrast with zigzag-preserving strain which yields no localization.

Conclusion: Unidirectional strain preserving armchair symmetry induces a pseudo-magnetic field enabling robust, exponentially localized edge states analogous to Landau levels, while zigzag-preserving strain does not—establishing a fundamental symmetry-dependent dichotomy in strain-engineered topological phenomena in honeycomb media.

Abstract: Slowly varying nonuniform strains of non-magnetic wave propagating media with honeycomb symmetry induce an effective- or pseudo-magnetic field, a phenomenon observed first in graphene, and later in photonic crystals and other physical settings. Starting with a discrete nearest-neighbor tight-binding model of a non-uniformly strained honeycomb medium, we derive the continuum effective magnetic Dirac Hamiltonian governing the envelope dynamics of wave packets, which are spectrally localized near a Dirac point (conical band degeneracy) of the unperturbed honeycomb. For unidirectional deformations of bounded gradient, which preserve translation invariance along the ''armchair'' direction, we prove the existence of time-harmonic states which are plane-wave like (pseudo-periodic) along the armchair direction and exponentially localized transverse to it. We also obtain the leading order multi-scale structure of such modes for small deformation gradients. Their transverse localization are determined by the eigenstates of a one dimensional effective Dirac Hamiltonian. Our rigorous results apply to deformations which induce an approximate perpendicular constant pseudo-magnetic field (Landau gauge), and yield states with nearly flat band (Landau level) spectrum and hence very high density of states. In contrast, the analogous deformation which preserves translations in the zigzag direction induces no such localization. Corroborating numerical simulations for the different deformation types are presented.

</details>


### [6] [Homothetic Hodge$-$de Rham Theory and a Geometric Regularization of Elliptic Boundary Value Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27564)
*Fereidoun Sabetghadam*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a homothetic extension of Weyl integrable geometry using affine homothetic gauge transformations centered at a harmonic, scale-invariant form α_d; after relinearization, it yields a twisted exterior calculus equivalent to Witten’s deformation, enabling a homothetic Hodge theory with decomposition theorem; the scalar homothetic Laplacian gives a geometric diffuse-interface formulation of elliptic BVPs—enforcing Dirichlet/Neumann/Cauchy data via penalty layers—and enables nonsingular point-source models with finite energy and correct far-field asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify geometric regularization of elliptic boundary value problems and resolve singularities in field sources by embedding boundary conditions and scale invariance into a generalized Weyl–geometric framework.

Method: Introduce affine homothetic gauge transformations centered at a harmonic scale-invariant form α_d; relinearize via variable shift to obtain a twisted exterior calculus isomorphic to Witten’s deformed de Rham complex; define twisted codifferential, homothetic Laplacian, and prove Hodge decomposition on compact Riemannian manifolds; interpret the scalar homothetic Laplacian as a volume-penalized elliptic operator with geometric penalty terms encoding boundary data; apply to construct regularized point sources.

Result: A rigorous homothetic Hodge decomposition theorem; equivalence between homothetic geometry and Witten deformation; geometric diffuse-interface representation of elliptic BVPs with consistent weak solutions for incompatible Cauchy data; construction of nonsingular point-source solutions with finite energy and exact Coulomb far-field.

Conclusion: Homothetic Weyl geometry provides a unified geometric framework for both regularization of boundary conditions (via volume penalization) and resolution of field singularities, extending classical Hodge theory and offering new tools for PDE modeling in physics and computational geometry.

Abstract: We introduce a homothetic extension of classical Weyl integrable geometry by generalizing the usual linear gauge transformations to affine homothetic transformations centered at a distinguished harmonic, scale-invariant form $α_d$. After relinearizing these affine gauge transformations via a suitable shift of variables, we obtain a twisted exterior calculus that is structurally equivalent to the Witten deformation of the de Rham complex. On this basis, we develop a corresponding homothetic Hodge theory: we define a twisted adjoint and homothetic Laplacian, and prove a homothetic Hodge decomposition theorem on compact Riemannian manifolds. In the context of partial differential equations, we show that the scalar homothetic Laplacian provides a rigorous diffuse interface (volume penalization) representation of elliptic boundary value problems. Modeling the Weyl scale field as a fixed distribution localized near a hypersurface, the resulting lower order geometric terms form a penalty layer that enforces Dirichlet, Neumann, or Cauchy data within a single geometric equation. This formulation yields consistent weak solutions even in the presence of classically incompatible Cauchy data. As an application, we construct a nonsingular model for point sources in elliptic field equations, which preserves the correct Coulombian far field while removing the core singularity and yielding finite field energy.

</details>


### [7] [Waves within a network of slowly time-modulated interfaces: time-dependent effective properties, reciprocity and high-order dispersion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27641)
*Michaël Darche,Raphaël Assier,Sébastien Guenneau,Bruno Lombard,Marie Touboul*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a homogenisation framework for wave propagation in 1D periodic networks with slowly time-modulated interfaces, deriving effective time-dependent wave equations (first- and second-order) and analysing bandgap formation under periodic modulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous low-frequency homogenisation theory for wave propagation in media with time-varying interfacial properties—specifically, to determine whether spatially homogeneous but time-dependent effective bulk parameters can emerge from purely interfacial temporal modulation, and to characterise dispersion and bandgaps.

Method: Asymptotic two-scale homogenisation applied to a 1D periodic network with time-dependent spring-mass jump conditions at interfaces; derivation of first-order (non-dispersive) and second-order (dispersive, reciprocal) effective wave equations; spectral analysis for k-gap identification under periodic time modulation; validation via time-domain numerical simulations.

Result: First-order homogenisation yields a spatially homogeneous wave equation with time-dependent effective mass density and Young’s modulus—proving bulk-like time dependence arises solely from interface modulation; periodic temporal modulation induces k-gaps; second-order model adds controllable dispersion while preserving reciprocity; simulations confirm validity and delineate model limits.

Conclusion: Time-modulated interfaces alone suffice to generate effective time-varying bulk properties; the derived hierarchy of effective models provides a systematic, physically interpretable framework for designing time-structured metamaterials with tailored dispersion and temporal bandgaps.

Abstract: We consider wave propagation through a 1D periodic network of slowly time-modulated interfaces. Each interface is modelled by time-dependent spring-mass jump conditions, where mass and rigidity interface parameters are modulated in time. Low-frequency homogenisation yields a leading-order model described by an effective time-dependent wave equation, i.e.\ a wave equation with effective mass density and Young's modulus which are homogeneous in space but depend on time. This means that time-dependent bulk effective properties can be created by an array where only interfaces are modulated in time. The occurrence of k-gaps in case of a periodic modulation is also analysed. Second-order homogenisation is then performed and leads to an effective model which is reciprocal but encapsulates higher-order dispersive effects. These findings and the limitations of the models are illustrated through time-domain simulations.

</details>


### [8] [A Concentration of Measure Phenomenon in the Principal Chiral Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27700)
*Tamer Tlas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses concentration of measure to show that the large-N limit of the O(N) principal chiral model becomes a free massive theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large-N behavior of the O(N) principal chiral model using rigorous probabilistic tools.

Method: Application of the concentration of measure phenomenon to analyze the asymptotic behavior of the partition function in the large-N limit.

Result: The partition function converges to that of a free massive field theory in the large-N limit.

Conclusion: The large-N limit of the O(N) principal chiral model simplifies to a free massive theory, implying suppression of interactions due to concentration effects.

Abstract: We utilize the concentration of measure phenomenon to study the large $N$ limit of the $O(N)$ principal chiral model. The partition function in this limit is demonstrated to be that of a free massive theory.

</details>


### [9] [Resonances in a Dirichlet quantum waveguide coupled to a cavity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27825)
*Sylwia Kondej,Nikoloz Kurtskhalia*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how introducing a small gap (aperture) in a cavity attached to a Dirichlet waveguide transforms embedded eigenvalues into resonances, and quantifies the scaling of the resonance’s imaginary part—hence its lifetime—with respect to the gap size in 2D and 3D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric perturbations—specifically small apertures—convert embedded eigenvalues (non-decaying modes) into resonances (quasi-bound, exponentially decaying states) in waveguides, and to characterize the dependence of resonance width (imaginary part of pole) on aperture size.

Method: Spectral analysis of the Laplacian with Dirichlet boundary conditions on a waveguide-cavity system; asymptotic expansion of resonant poles near embedded eigenvalues using matched asymptotics and scattering theory in dimension-dependent geometries.

Result: In 2D, for gap size ε, Im(resonance) ∼ O(ε²); in 3D, for rectangular aperture volume ∼ ε², Im(resonance) ∼ O(ε⁴); thus resonance lifetime scales generically as O((vol_ε)⁻²).

Conclusion: The imaginary part of the resonant pole—and hence the decay rate—is highly sensitive to the aperture volume, following a universal inverse-square law in terms of the aperture’s volume across dimensions.

Abstract: We consider a Dirichlet waveguide in $\mathbb{R}^n$ ($n = 2,3$) with an attached cavity. We show that if the cavity admits a small gap, then the original embedded eigenvalues turn into resonances. The main question we address is how the size of the gap affects the resonant properties, in particular the imaginary part of the resonant pole. For example, in the case of a two dimensional waveguide with a gap of size $\varepsilon$, we show that the leading order term of the resonance behaves as $\mathcal O (\varepsilon^2)$. In the three-dimensional case, if the aperture is defined by a rectangular opening with volume proportional to $\varepsilon^2$, the resonant component behaves as $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^4)$. This shows that, in the analyzed class of models, the characteristic time scale associated with the resonances is generically of order $\mathcal{O}((\mathrm{vol}_\varepsilon)^{-2})$, where $\mathrm{vol}_\varepsilon$ denotes the volume of the aperture inducing the resonance.

</details>


### [10] [A symmetry formula for correlation functions in the superintegrable chiral Potts spin chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27930)
*Haoran Zhu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper proves an exact symmetry formula for two-point correlations in the finite-size superintegrable chiral Potts spin chain, confirming a conjecture by Fabricius and McCoy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve a long-standing conjecture by Fabricius and McCoy regarding symmetry properties of two-point correlation functions in the superintegrable chiral Potts model.

Method: Exact finite-volume analysis using symmetries of the Hamiltonian and translation operator; derivation of a complex-conjugation symmetry relation for $Z$-operator correlations on periodic chains.

Result: Proof of the identity $\langle Z_0^r Z_R^{\dagger r}\rangle^* = \langle Z_0^r Z_{L-R}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ for all $1 \leq r \leq N-1$, implying reality of midpoint correlations when $L$ is even; generalisation from $N=3$ to arbitrary $N$ and all translation eigensectors.

Conclusion: The conjecture of Fabricius and McCoy is rigorously proven, establishing a fundamental symmetry of correlation functions in the superintegrable chiral Potts model for arbitrary $N$ and finite system size.

Abstract: We prove an exact finite-volume symmetry formula for two-point functions in the periodic $N$-state superintegrable chiral Potts spin chain. We show that, for every chain length $L$ and every simultaneous eigenvector of the Hamiltonian and the one-site translation operator, the correlations satisfy $\langle Z_0^r Z_R^{\dagger r}\rangle^*=\langle Z_0^r Z_{L-R}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ for $1\leqslant r\leqslant N-1$. Hence, whenever $L$ is even, the midpoint correlation $\langle Z_0^r Z_{L/2}^{\dagger r}\rangle$ is real. Then we generalise the three-state chain case to arbitrary $N$ and to every translation eigensector. This resolves a conjecture of Fabricius and McCoy.

</details>


### [11] [A Damage-Driven Model for Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy: Early-Stage Dynamics and Invasion Thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28147)
*Gaetana Gambino,Francesco Gargano,Alessandra Rizzo,Vincenzo Sciacca*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a reaction-diffusion-chemotaxis model for early-stage Duchenne muscular dystrophy, showing that disease spreads via invasion (pulled fronts) rather than Turing-type pattern formation; it proves global well-posedness, derives a damage reproduction threshold and minimal front speed, and validates findings numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To mathematically understand early-stage spatial progression of Duchenne muscular dystrophy through a biologically grounded, damage-driven paradigm—specifically, how localized tissue injury triggers immune recruitment and propagates pathology.

Method: Formulated a spatially extended reaction–diffusion–chemotaxis PDE system coupling healthy tissue, damaged fibers, immune cells, and inflammatory signals; performed linear stability analysis around the healthy equilibrium; derived invasion conditions and front speed via traveling-wave analysis; proved global well-posedness; validated with numerical simulations.

Result: Diffusion does not cause Turing instabilities; disease progression is invasion-dominated; an explicit damage reproduction threshold determines transition from decay to invasion; pathological fronts propagate at a minimal speed governed by a pulled-front mechanism; simulations confirm the analytical dichotomy.

Conclusion: Early-stage disease progression in Duchenne muscular dystrophy is best described as spatial invasion of damage—not self-organized patterning—providing a mechanistic, quantitative framework for understanding initiation and spread of pathology.

Abstract: We introduce a spatially extended mathematical model for Duchenne muscular dystrophy based on a damage-driven paradigm, in which immune recruitment is triggered by tissue injury. The model is formulated as a reaction--diffusion--chemotaxis system describing the interaction between healthy tissue, damaged fibers, immune cells and inflammatory signals. We establish the global well-posedness of the system and investigate the early-stage dynamics through linearization around the healthy equilibrium. Our analysis shows that diffusion does not induce Turing instabilities, so that spatial heterogeneity cannot arise from diffusion-driven mechanisms. Instead, disease progression occurs through invasion processes. We derive explicit conditions for the onset of invasion, interpreted as an effective damage reproduction threshold and characterize the minimal propagation speed of pathological fronts, showing that the dynamics is governed by a pulled-front mechanism. Numerical simulations support the analytical results and confirm the transition between decay and invasion. These results provide a mathematical framework for early-stage disease progression and indicate that spatial patterns arise from the expansion of localized damage rather than from intrinsic pattern-forming mechanisms.

</details>


### [12] [Quasi-local probability averaging in the context of cutoff regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28235)
*A. V. Ivanov,I. V. Korenev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates averaged fundamental solutions of Laplace operators in arbitrary-dimensional Euclidean space using probabilistic averaging kernels, derives new representations for deformed fundamental solutions (including their values at zero), and illustrates applications to renormalization in specific quantum field models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structural and analytic properties of averaged fundamental solutions of Laplace operators—particularly those amenable to probabilistic interpretation—and to explore their utility in quantum field theory, especially regarding renormalization.

Method: Analytic construction and manipulation of averaged fundamental solutions using a class of probabilistic averaging kernels; derivation of new integral and distributional representations for deformed fundamental solutions and their pointwise values (e.g., at zero); application to concrete quantum field models.

Result: New explicit representations for averaged and deformed fundamental solutions of the Laplacian in ℝⁿ; characterization of their behavior at the origin; illustrative examples linking these constructions to renormalization properties in specific quantum field theories.

Conclusion: Averaged fundamental solutions defined via suitable probabilistic kernels yield analytically tractable deformations of classical Green’s functions, providing a framework that bridges potential theory, probability, and perturbative renormalization in quantum field theory.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the properties of averaged fundamental solutions of a special type for Laplace operators in the Euclidean space of an arbitrary dimension. We consider a class of kernels suitable for probabilistic averaging, and propose new representations for the deformed fundamental solutions and their values at zero. In addition, we give examples related to specific quantum field models in the context of studying renormalization properties.

</details>


### [13] [Three non-Hermitian random matrix universality classes of complex edge statistics: Spacing ratios and distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28457)
*Gernot Akemann,Georg Angermann,Noah Aygün,Adam Mielke,Patricia Päßler,Christoph Raitzig,Tobias Winkler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes local bulk and edge statistics of non-Hermitian random matrices across three symmetry classes (A, AI†, AII†), using complex spacing ratios and nearest-neighbor (NN) spacing distributions. It provides exact finite-N analytic results for the complex spacing ratio in the Ginibre ensemble (class A), introduces a parameter-dependent N=3 surmise for the elliptic Ginibre case, and numerically compares spectral statistics across ensembles and against 2D Poisson — revealing distinct edge universality classes governed by effective 2D Coulomb gas models with different inverse temperatures β. It finds that complex spacing ratios do not fully unfold edge statistics, while NN distributions universally exhibit cubic repulsion at small arguments in both bulk and edge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend and characterize the recently conjectured three generic local edge statistics—complementing known bulk universality—for non-Hermitian random matrices across all symmetry classes, and to assess the efficacy of complex spacing ratios and NN distributions as probes of local spectral correlations, especially at the spectral edge.

Method: Analytical derivation of the complex spacing ratio distribution for finite-N Ginibre (class A) using a conditional point process; derivation of a parameter-dependent N=3 surmise for the elliptic Ginibre ensemble; extensive numerical simulations of complex spacing ratios, their moments, and NN spacing distributions across Gaussian ensembles of class A (Ginibre), AI† (complex symmetric), and AII† (complex self-dual), compared with 2D Poisson; interpretation via effective 2D Coulomb gas with tunable inverse temperature β.

Result: Exact finite-N expression for complex spacing ratio in Ginibre ensemble; convergence justification of prior large-N approximation; accurate N=3 elliptic Ginibre surmise interpolating to GUE; numerical evidence of distinct edge universality classes with varying degrees of level repulsion explained by β = 1, 2, 4 Coulomb gases; failure of complex spacing ratio to fully unfold edge statistics; universal cubic (r³) small-r behavior of NN spacing distributions in both bulk and edge across all three classes.

Conclusion: The three non-Hermitian symmetry classes exhibit distinct local edge statistics, characterizable via NN spacing distributions and interpretable through an effective 2D Coulomb gas model—but complex spacing ratios alone are insufficient for full unfolding at the edge. The universal r³ repulsion confirms cubic-level repulsion as a robust signature across bulk and edge, reinforcing the existence of three generic edge universality classes analogous to the three bulk ones.

Abstract: The conjectured three generic local bulk statistics amongst all non-Hermitian random matrix symmetry classes have recently been extended to three generic local edge statistics. We study analytically and numerically complex spacing ratios and nearest-neighbour (NN) spacing distributions that characterise such local statistics. We choose the three simplest representatives of these universality classes, given by the Gaussian ensembles of complex Ginibre, complex symmetric and complex self-dual matrices, denoted by class A, AI$^†$ and AII$^†$. In the first part, we analytically study the complex spacing ratio in class A, at finite matrix size $N$. Introducing a conditional point process, we simplify existing expressions and show why an uncontrolled approximation introduced earlier converges well in the large-$N$ limit in the bulk. When specifying to the elliptic Ginibre ensemble, we present a parameter-dependent $N=3$ surmise for the complex spacing ratio, interpolating to that of the Gaussian unitary ensemble (GUE), where such a surmise is very accurate. In the second numerical part, we compare complex spacing ratios, its moments, and NN spacing distributions for all three ensembles with that of uncorrelated points, the two-dimensional (2D) Poisson process, both in the bulk and at the edge. The varying degree of repulsion within these different edge universality classes can be well understood in terms of an effective 2D Coulomb gas description, at different values of inverse temperature $β$. We find indications that the complex spacing ratio does not fully unfold the local statistics at the edge. Finally we verify that for small argument, in all three symmetry classes the NN spacing distributions in the bulk and at the edge are consistent with the universal cubic repulsion.

</details>


### [14] [Geometry of the Ising persistence problem and the universal Bonnet-Manin Painlevé VI distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28632)
*Ivan Dornic,Robert Conte*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a deep connection between the persistence probability distribution of a non-Markovian stochastic process and a distinguished Painlevé VI equation, derived from an exact Fredholm Pfaffian structure associated with the integrable sech kernel; it recovers and geometrically interprets the Derrida–Hakim–Pasquier universal persistence exponent as an asymptotic observable — specifically, as the asymptotic mean curvature of a one-parameter family of Bonnet surfaces in ℝ³.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve first-passage questions in interacting spin systems (e.g., Ising model) by determining the full persistence probability distribution for a non-Markovian stochastic process, going beyond asymptotics to obtain exact analytic structure.

Method: Leverages exact Fredholm Pfaffian theory for the integrable sech kernel K_sech = 1/(2π cosh[(x−y)/2]); derives a second-order nonlinear ODE governing even/odd Fredholm determinants; identifies it as a specific Painlevé VI equation via isomonodromic deformation and connection problem analysis; introduces geometric interpretation via Bonnet surfaces and mean curvature in ℝ³.

Result: The full persistence probability distribution is exactly governed by a Painlevé VI system with Manin coefficients [0,0,0,0]; the universal persistence exponent emerges as the asymptotic value of the corresponding Painlevé transcendent at infinity; this exponent acquires a geometric meaning as the asymptotic mean curvature of a family of Bonnet surfaces.

Conclusion: Persistence in symmetric Ising-like systems is exactly encoded in a Painlevé VI transcendent, whose analytic and geometric structure — rooted in integrable kernel theory and surface immersion geometry — unifies statistical physics observables with classical nonlinear ODEs and differential geometry.

Abstract: We determine the full persistence probability distribution for a non-Markovian stochastic process, motivated by first-passage questions arising in interacting spin systems and allied systems. We show that this distribution is governed by a distinguished Painlevé VI system arising from an exact Fredholm Pfaffian structure associated with the integrable sech kernel, $K_{\mathrm{sech}}=1/(2 π\cosh[(x-y)/2])$. The universal persistence exponent originally obtained by Derrida, Hakim and Pasquier is recovered as an asymptotic observable and acquires a natural geometric interpretation. In the stationary scaling regime, the persistence probability admits an exact Pfaffian decomposition into even and odd Fredholm determinants of the integrable \emph{sech} kernel. These determinants are controlled by a unique global solution of a second-order nonlinear ordinary differential equation, which is identified as a particular Painlevé VI equation. The corresponding Painlevé VI connection problem determines the persistence exponent as a limiting value at infinity.
  We further show that the Painlevé VI system governing persistence admits a direct geometric interpretation: the relevant solution coincides with the mean curvature of a one-parameter family of Bonnet surfaces immersed in $\mathbb R^3$. A folding transformation between such surfaces singles out the Painlevé VI equation with Manin coefficients $[0,0,0,0]$, which in particular governs the universal persistence distribution in the symmetric Ising case. In this framework, the persistence exponent is identified with the asymptotic mean curvature of the associated surface.

</details>


### [15] [Invariant measures of randomized quantum trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28664)
*Tristan Benoist,Sascha Lill,Cornelia Vogel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum trajectories under randomized probe observables, showing that non-singular randomization ensures purification and uniqueness of the invariant probability measure; it introduces 'multiplicative primitivity'—a new ergodicity notion for quantum channels—and analyzes regularity and examples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how randomizing probe observables affects the stationary behavior and regularization of quantum trajectories, particularly regarding purification and uniqueness of invariant measures.

Method: Analytical study of quantum trajectories under randomized probe measurements; introduction and characterization of multiplicative primitivity for quantum channels; computation of invariant measures for canonical channels and examination of boundary cases via examples.

Result: Non-singular randomization guarantees purification and existence of a unique invariant probability measure; multiplicative primitivity is established as an intermediate ergodicity property between primitivity and positivity-improving; explicit invariant measures are computed for canonical channels.

Conclusion: Randomization of probe observables regularizes quantum trajectories, ensuring purification and uniqueness of the invariant measure; multiplicative primitivity provides a refined ergodic framework for analyzing such stochastic quantum dynamics.

Abstract: Quantum trajectories are Markov chains modeling quantum systems subjected to repeated indirect measurements. Their stationary regime depends on what observables are measured on the probes used to indirectly measure the system. In this article we explore the properties of quantum trajectories when the choice of probe observable is randomized. The randomization induces some regularization of the quantum trajectories. We show that non-singular randomization ensures that quantum trajectories purify and therefore accept a unique invariant probability measure. We furthermore study the regularity of that invariant measure. In that endeavour, we introduce a new notion of ergodicity for quantum channels, which we call multiplicative primitivity. It is a priory stronger than primitivity but weaker than positivity improving. Finally, we compute some invariant measures for canonical quantum channels and explore the limits of our assumptions with several examples.

</details>
