### [16] [Universal Geometric Scaling in Cosmic Ray Spallation: Evidence of a Dynamical Causal Horizon from AMS-02](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26824)
*Yi Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the anomaly of energy-independent plateaus in secondary cosmic-ray ratios (Li/B, Be/B, Li/Be) at high rigidity (>30 GV) by proposing a geometric thermal framework—rooted in ultra-relativistic spallation-induced causal horizons and Unruh radiation—yielding a consistent effective temperature (~5.6–6.1 MeV) matching nuclear liquid-gas phase transition scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-precision interpretation of cosmic ray spectra is limited by fragmentation cross-section uncertainties; yet AMS-02 data show unexpected energy-independent plateaus in secondary-to-secondary flux ratios above 30 GV, contradicting standard kinematic models.

Method: Develops a macroscopic geometric model where ultra-relativistic spallation snaps strong-interaction flux tubes, inducing extreme deceleration and a causal horizon; estimates Unruh temperature semi-microscopically using Woods-Saxon potential and pion exchange; calibrates via Be/B ratio and performs blind tests on Li/B and Li/Be.

Result: Derives Unruh temperature T_U ≈ 5.6–5.8 MeV theoretically; extracts asymptotic scale 6.08 MeV from Be/B calibration; confirms universal zero-slope convergence in all lithium ratios via blind tests.

Conclusion: A constant geometric thermal bath—characterized by an Unruh-like temperature near the nuclear liquid-gas phase transition limit—supersedes conventional kinematic descriptions at high rigidity, providing a robust, parameter-free explanation for plateau behavior in secondary cosmic-ray ratios.

Abstract: The interpretation of high-precision cosmic ray spectra is fundamentally bottlenecked by uncertainties in fragmentation cross-sections. Traditional kinematic models, driven by phase-space expansions, typically predict complex, energy-dependent evolutions. However, AMS-02 measurements reveal that at high rigidities ($R > 30$~GV), secondary-to-secondary flux ratios (Li/B, Be/B, and Li/Be) strictly converge to energy-independent plateaus. To understand this anomaly, we explore a macroscopic geometric framework. The ultra-relativistic spallation of a target nucleus snaps residual strong-interaction flux tubes, inducing an extreme deceleration on the remnant. Using a semi-microscopic estimation based on the Woods-Saxon potential and pion exchange, we suggest this dynamically generates a causal horizon with an effective Unruh temperature $T_U \approx 5.6-5.8$~MeV. Utilizing the Be/B ratio as an absolute calibration channel, we extract an asymptotic scale of $6.08$~MeV, remarkably consistent with our theoretical estimation and the established nuclear liquid-gas phase transition limit. Subsequent blind tests on Lithium ratios demonstrate a universal zero-slope convergence, providing compelling evidence that a constant geometric thermal bath effectively supersedes complex microscopic kinematics at high energies.

</details>


### [17] [Global Structure, Non-Invertible PQ Symmetry, and the DFSZ Domain Wall Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26867)
*Gongjun Choi,Sungwoo Hong,Seth Koren*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the DFSZ axion model, emphasizing how global structure—specifically quotiented symmetry groups like (G_EW × U(1)_PQ)/ℤ₂ and (SU(3)_C × G_F)/ℤ₃—affects the domain wall problem; it shows domain wall stability arises from non-invertible chiral symmetries in extended flavor models, proposes UV resolution via small-instanton-induced breaking of such symmetries, and outlines associated gravitational wave signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address the DFSZ axion's domain wall problem by incorporating previously neglected global (i.e., topological/quotient) structure of symmetry groups, recognizing its theoretical and phenomenological significance—especially for cosmology and naturalness.

Method: The authors analyze symmetry group global structure (e.g., quotient identifications), classify domain wall solutions using homotopy π₀ of vacuum manifolds, identify non-invertible chiral symmetries enforcing domain wall stability in Z′ flavor models, apply the 'Non-invertible Naturalness' framework to argue for UV completions with instanton-induced symmetry breaking, and estimate gravitational wave signals from domain wall annihilation.

Result: They find that the domain wall number N_DW = 6 in standard DFSZ is modified by global structure; domain walls become stable due to non-invertible symmetries in quark-flavor Z′ models; small instantons can break those symmetries, dynamically eliminating stable walls; and resulting domain wall networks may produce observable stochastic gravitational wave backgrounds at LISA or BBO frequencies.

Conclusion: Global structure of gauge and global symmetries is essential for accurately assessing topological defects and naturalness in axion models; neglecting it leads to incorrect domain wall counting and stability assessments; incorporating it enables consistent UV resolutions and yields testable gravitational wave predictions.

Abstract: In recent years it has become increasingly clear that the previously overlooked ``global structure'' of symmetry groups can encode significant theoretical structure and, more importantly, have substantial phenomenological implications. With this in mind we re-examine the DFSZ axion, which suffers from a domain wall problem due to the Standard Model generation structure. We show that global structure $(G_{\text{EW}} \times U(1)_{\text{PQ}})/\mathbb{Z}_2$ acting between the Peccei-Quinn symmetry and the electroweak gauge group plays a crucial role in determining the precise nature of the domain wall problem, which has important implications in both cubic and quartic DFSZ. We then demonstrate that the stability of the domain walls is enforced by a non-invertible chiral symmetry in quark flavor $Z'$ models which have additional global structure $(SU(3)_C \times G_F)/\mathbb{Z}_3$ acting between the color and the gauged quark flavor groups. The strategy of Non-invertible Naturalness then leads us to UV theories that resolve the domain wall problem through small-instanton-induced breaking of non-invertible symmetries. Finally, we sketch potential gravitational wave signatures arising from the annihilation of axion domain walls. Our work illustrates the importance of considerations of global structure in realistic models of particle physics.

</details>


### [18] [Simulating Axion Electrodynamics in Magnetized Plasmas: Energy transfer in the inhomogeneous and strongly varying limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26868)
*Fabrizio Corelli,Estanis Utrilla Ginés,Enrico Cannizzaro,Andrea Caputo,Samuel J. Witte*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how axions transfer energy to magnetized plasmas in highly inhomogeneous, time-varying environments, revealing enhanced excitation of sub-luminal plasma modes and novel coupling mechanisms—including LO–Alfvén resonance and localized electric field generation—relevant for axion detection and astrophysical signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize energy transfer and losses from ambient axion fields in extreme, strongly varying magnetized plasma backgrounds—relevant for axion detection and understanding their role in astrophysical environments.

Method: Combined frequency-domain and time-domain numerical simulations to solve electromagnetic response of axions in magnetized plasmas, analyzing photon excitation efficiency, indirect Alfvén mode excitation via LO–Alfvén resonant coupling, and electric field generation in localized plasma under-densities.

Result: Identification of a regime where sub-luminal plasma modes (ω < k) are excited with higher efficiency than super-luminal modes (ω > k); discovery of resonant LO–Alfvén coupling; demonstration of electric field excitation in small plasma under-densities.

Conclusion: Axions can interact with magnetized plasmas through multiple non-standard channels—beyond simple photon conversion—enabling new observational signatures in extreme astrophysical settings and informing next-generation axion search strategies.

Abstract: In this work we study the electromagnetic response induced by axions in a magnetized plasma, focusing specifically on characterizing energy transfer and energy losses from the ambient axion field in highly inhomogeneous and strongly varying backgrounds. Using a suite of both frequency-domain and time-domain simulations, we solve for: the efficiency of photon excitation in a rapidly varying background, the indirect excitation of Alfvén modes, occurring when a Langmuir-Ordinary (LO) mode is resonantly excited near a combined cutoff-resonance of the dispersion relations of the LO and Alfvén modes, and the excitation of electric fields in small localized plasma under-densities. We identify a particularly interesting regime in which energy can be transferred into sub-luminal plasma modes ($ω< k$) with an efficiency greater than that of super-luminal modes ($ω> k$). Our results highlight a variety of less conventional ways in which axions (and other light degrees of freedom that mix with electromagnetism, such as dark photons or gravitons) can interact in extreme astrophysical environments.

</details>


### [19] [The Physics and Prospects of Super-Tau Charm Factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26879)
*Alexey A. Petrov,Yangheng Zheng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review outlines the physics potential of Super tau-charm factories—high-luminosity e⁺e⁻ colliders (2–7 GeV) enabling precision SM tests and BSM searches via quantum-coherent production, threshold kinematics, and large data samples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the unique energy range (2–7 GeV) covering tau, open-charm, charmonium, hyperon, and light-hadron thresholds for high-precision Standard Model tests and discovery of physics beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Review-based analysis of physics prospects, including precision charm physics, CP violation (mesons/baryons), tau lepton properties/rare decays, and nonperturbative QCD (hadronization, spectroscopy, time-like form factors); also assesses experimental challenges and facility complementarity.

Result: Super tau-charm factories are identified as pivotal for the precision frontier, offering unmatched capabilities in quantum-coherent production, threshold scans, and luminosity-driven statistical precision across multiple physics domains.

Conclusion: These facilities represent a transformative opportunity at the precision frontier, synergistically advancing fundamental parameters, symmetry tests, and nonperturbative QCD understanding, while complementing other high-energy experiments.

Abstract: The proposed Super tau-charm factories are a powerful new class of high-luminosity electron-positron colliders operating in the center-of-mass energy range between 2 and 7 GeV, a region that spans thresholds for tau leptons, open-charm hadrons, charmonium and charmonium-like states, hyperons, and light hadrons. With unprecedented data samples, threshold kinematics, and quantum-coherent production, these facilities offer unique opportunities to advance precision tests of the Standard Model and to search for physics beyond it. In this review, we examine the physics prospects of the Super Tau-Charm Facility, focusing on precision charm measurements, CP violation in mesons and baryons, tau lepton properties and rare decays, and nonperturbative QCD phenomena such as hadronization, spectroscopy, and time-like form factors. We also discuss the experimental landscape, technological challenges, and complementarity with existing and planned facilities. Together, these capabilities position super tau-charm factories at the forefront of the precision frontier in particle physics.

</details>


### [20] [Axion EFT in the BMHV Scheme: Flavor Currents, Evanescent Operators and Ward Identities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26880)
*Deepanshu Bisht,Sabyasachi Chakraborty,Atanu Samanta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically analyzes axion effective field theory in the BMHV scheme, addressing γ₅-related chiral Ward identity violations via evanescent operators; it derives and verifies bare/renormalized Ward identities up to two loops, determines finite renormalization to restore 4D consistency, and clarifies axial current renormalization and anomaly structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies arising from the non-anticommuting γ₅ in d ≠ 4 dimensions within the BMHV scheme, particularly for fermionic dimension-five axion couplings and chiral flavor currents, ensuring valid Ward identities and anomaly matching in multi-loop axion EFT calculations.

Method: Uses the Breitenlohner-Maison-'t Hooft-Veltman (BMHV) regularization scheme; derives bare and renormalized chiral Ward identities including equation-of-motion and evanescent operator contributions; performs diagrammatic two-loop calculations (O(αₛ²)) to verify identities for both pole and finite parts; computes evanescent-to-physical operator mixing and determines required finite counterterms.

Result: Explicit derivation of Ward identities with evanescent and EOM corrections; verification up to O(αₛ²) including finite terms; quantification of evanescent operator mixing into physical operators; determination of finite renormalization that restores four-dimensional chiral Ward identities and correctly reproduces axial current renormalization and the chiral anomaly.

Conclusion: Evanescent operators are indispensable for maintaining scheme consistency in axion EFT beyond one loop; the BMHV scheme—augmented with proper finite renormalization—provides a robust, transparent framework for high-precision multi-loop computations involving chiral fermions and axion couplings.

Abstract: We present a systematic analysis of axion effective field theory within the Breitenlohner-Maison-`t Hooft-Veltman (BMHV) scheme, focusing on the renormalization of fermionic dimension-five operators and the associated chiral flavor currents. In this framework, the non-anticommuting nature of $γ_5$ in $d \neq 4$ dimensions leads to violations of naive Ward identities through the emergence of evanescent operators. We derive the bare and renormalized Ward identities for chiral currents, explicitly identifying the equation-of-motion and evanescent operator contributions. Using diagrammatic calculations, we verify the validity of these identities up to two-loop order $\mathcal{O}(α_s^2)$, including both pole and finite terms. We demonstrate how evanescent operators mix into physical operators and determine the finite renormalization required to restore four-dimensional Ward identities, recovering the expected structure of axial current renormalization and the anomaly. Our results provide a consistent and transparent framework for multi-loop computations in axion EFT and highlight the essential role of evanescent operators in maintaining scheme consistency.

</details>


### [21] [Density screening effects in the NJL model: Chiral condensate, speed of sound, and the Critical End Point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26924)
*Alejandro Rosas Díaz,Alfredo Raya,C. A. Vaquera Araujo,S. Hernández-Ortiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the QCD phase diagram using a modified NJL model with temperature- and density-dependent coupling $G(T,\mu)$, incorporating medium screening, consistent regularization, and Sommerfeld expansion; it finds that screening shifts the Critical End Point (CEP) location and alters the chiral transition nature, supporting heavy-ion experiments and compact star physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To locate the Critical End Point (CEP) in the QCD phase diagram — where the crossover chiral transition at low baryochemical potential meets the expected first-order transition at high density — and to reconcile theoretical predictions with ongoing FAIR/NICA experiments and astrophysical implications.

Method: Uses the Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with an effective, medium-modified coupling $G(T,\mu)$ for $\mu \gg T \sim 0$; employs consistent regularization and Sommerfeld expansion to incorporate low-temperature and high-density corrections into the gap equation; numerically analyzes chiral condensate, dynamical quark mass, and speed of sound.

Result: Screening effects significantly shift the predicted location of the CEP and change the chiral transition from crossover to first-order (or vice versa) in certain regions of the phase diagram; the speed of sound exhibits nontrivial behavior near the CEP, reflecting softening of the equation of state.

Conclusion: Medium screening is essential for reliable CEP prediction in effective QCD models; the modified NJL framework improves consistency with heavy-ion collision observables and provides constraints relevant for neutron star interior modeling.

Abstract: The phase diagram of Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) remains a central topic in high-energy physics. At high temperature and low baryochemical potential, the chiral transition is experimentally observed and theoretically explored to be a smooth crossover, while at high densities, a first-order phase transition is theoretically expected in lack of direct experimental evidence. The search for the Critical End Point (CEP), where both regimes meet, is one of the main objectives of heavy-ion experiments at FAIR and NICA. In this work, we explore the QCD phase diagram structure using the Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, incorporating medium screening effects through an effective coupling $G(T,μ)$ for $μ\gg T\sim 0$. We apply a consistent regularization scheme and Sommerfeld expansion to include low thermal and large density corrections in the gap equation. Our numerical analysis focuses on the behavior of the chiral condensate, the dynamical quark mass, and the speed of sound. We find that screening effects shift the posible position of the CEP and modify the nature of the chiral transition. These findings provide theoretical support for ongoing experimental searches and may have implications for the physics of compact stars.

</details>


### [22] [Signatures from pion condensation and lepton flavor asymmetries in the cosmological gravitational wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26946)
*Osvaldo Ferreira,Eduardo S. Fraga,Jürgen Schaffner-Bielich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how large tau lepton flavor asymmetries during the QCD epoch could trigger pion condensation in the early Universe, leading to a superconformal speed of sound and a distinctive low-frequency gravitational wave (GW) signature detectable by pulsar timing arrays (e.g., NANOGrav 15-yr).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore novel cosmological probes of lepton flavor asymmetries—particularly tau asymmetries—via their impact on QCD-phase dynamics and associated GW production.

Method: Theoretical modeling of pion condensation induced by large lepton flavor asymmetries at the QCD epoch; computation of the equation-of-state and speed-of-sound deviation from conformality; estimation of the resulting stochastic GW spectrum from causal sources; comparison with zero-lepton-asymmetry baseline and confrontation with NANOGrav 15-yr stochastic background data.

Result: Large tau lepton asymmetries can drive pion condensation, yielding c_s^2 > 1/3; this enhances low-frequency GW power from causal sources relative to the standard symmetric case, producing a potentially observable spectral feature in the nanohertz band.

Conclusion: Detection (or exclusion) of this enhanced low-frequency GW signal provides a new, model-independent constraint on primordial lepton flavor asymmetries—especially tau—complementing BBN and CMB bounds, and offering an interpretable explanation or tension with the NANOGrav 15-yr stochastic background.

Abstract: Large lepton flavor asymmetries at the QCD epoch could generate a pion condensation phase in the early Universe. For large enough tau lepton flavor asymmetries, the speed of sound can exceed the conformal value, leaving a distinctive imprint on the low-frequency gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from causal sources. Beyond probing the formation of a pion condensation phase, the detection or non-detection of this signature would provide a novel constraint on lepton asymmetries in the early Universe. We estimate the GW signal and compare it with the standard case of vanishing lepton asymmetry. Finally, we discuss the implications for the stochastic GW background reported by Pulsar Timing Arrays, using the NANOGrav 15-year dataset.

</details>


### [23] [Magnetic moments of open bottom--charm molecular pentaquark octets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27657)
*Halil Mutuk,Xian-Wei Kang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates magnetic moments of open heavy-flavor molecular pentaquarks (b̄cqqq and c̄bqqq) using a constituent quark model and S-wave molecular picture, distinguishing two SU(3)_f octets (8_{1f}, 8_{2f}) based on light-diquark symmetry; it finds universal magnetic moments in the antisymmetric 8_{2f} case due to spin-singlet diquarks, contrasting with variable moments in 8_{1f}, thereby revealing heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking and providing electromagnetic benchmarks for experimental identification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretically robust electromagnetic observables—specifically magnetic moments—that can discriminate between competing internal structures (e.g., light-diquark symmetry) and spin configurations of experimentally elusive open heavy-flavor pentaquarks (b̄cqqq and c̄bqqq), and to probe heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking in hadronic systems.

Method: Adopts a molecular picture treating pentaquarks as S-wave bound states of a heavy baryon and meson; constructs complete spin–flavor wavefunctions for the two SU(3)_f octet representations (8_{1f} from symmetric, 8_{2f} from antisymmetric light-diquark); computes magnetic moments within the constituent quark model for J^P = 1/2^− (from 1/2^+⊗0^−) and 1/2^−, 3/2^− (from 1/2^+⊗1^−) configurations across both b̄c and c̄b families.

Result: In the 8_{2f} representation (antisymmetric light-diquark), magnetic moments are nearly universal: ≈ −0.062 μ_N (b̄cqqq) and ≈ +0.362 μ_N (c̄bqqq), due to spin-singlet suppression of light-quark contributions; in contrast, 8_{1f} yields a broad, sign-varying spectrum reflecting active symmetric diquark dynamics; clear b̄c vs. c̄b differences confirm explicit heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking in electromagnetic observables.

Conclusion: Magnetic moments serve as sensitive probes of light-diquark symmetry and heavy-quark flavor structure in open heavy-flavor pentaquarks; the predicted hierarchy and sign patterns provide definitive benchmarks for distinguishing molecular configurations in upcoming LHCb and Belle II experiments.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive theoretical investigation of the magnetic moments of open heavy-flavor molecular pentaquarks with quark compositions $b\bar{c}qqq$ and $c\bar{b}qqq$ (where $q=u,d,s$). Employing a molecular picture in which the pentaquarks are treated as S-wave bound states of a heavy baryon and a meson, we systematically construct the complete spin--flavor wavefunctions for the two distinct SU(3)$_f$ octet representations, $8_{1f}$ and $8_{2f}$, arising from symmetric and antisymmetric light-diquark configurations, respectively. Within the framework of the constituent quark model, we calculate the magnetic moments of spin-parity configurations, $J^P = \frac{1}{2}^-(\frac{1}{2}^+\otimes 0^-)$ and $J^P = \frac{1}{2}^-, \frac{3}{2}^-(\frac{1}{2}^+\otimes 1^-)$, for each member of the $b\bar{c}$ and $c\bar{b}$ octets. Our results reveal a striking hierarchy: in the $8_{2f}$ representation, the $\frac{1}{2}^+\otimes 0^-$ states exhibit near-universal magnetic moments ($μ\approx -0.062\,μ_N$ for $b\bar{c}qqq$ and $μ\approx +0.362\,μ_N$ for $c\bar{b}qqq$), as a direct consequence of the spin-singlet light-diquark that suppresses light-quark contributions. In contrast, the $8_{1f}$ representation shows a broad spectrum of values with frequent sign changes, reflecting the active role of the symmetric light-diquark. The clear differences between the $b\bar{c}$ and $c\bar{b}$ families demonstrate explicit heavy-quark flavor symmetry breaking in electromagnetic observables. These predictions provide a detailed set of electromagnetic benchmarks that can serve as discriminants for the internal flavor structure and spin configuration of future experimentally observed open heavy-flavor pentaquarks, offering valuable guidance for ongoing and future searches at facilities such as LHCb and Belle II.

</details>


### [24] [Agentic Diagrammatica: Towards Autonomous Symbolic Computation in High Energy Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26990)
*Tony Menzo,Alexander Roman,George T. Fleming,Sergei Gleyzer,Konstantin T. Matchev,Stephen Mrenna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Diagrammatica is an extension to the HEPTAPOD framework that enables LLM agents to perform reliable symbolic computations in theoretical particle physics via diagram-based, tool-constrained planning—using human-auditable diagram specifications and trusted backends for NDA (order-of-magnitude estimates) and EDA (exact tree-level symbolic calculations with FeynCalc)—validated on decay-width enumeration and muon decay multiplicity analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Symbolic computation with LLM agents suffers from unreliability due to implicit mathematical conventions not enforceable by computational backends; Diagrammatica addresses this by constraining agent actions to convention-fixing tool calls grounded in diagrammatic semantics.

Method: Introduces a tool-constrained agentic architecture where the LLM plans using compact, human-auditable diagram specifications, and a trusted backend executes exact symbolic/numerical computations; implements two complementary calculation paths—Naive Dimensional Analysis (NDA) and Exact Diagrammatic Analysis (EDA)—with automatic Feynman diagram enumeration, FeynCalc code generation, and integration with a navigable theory knowledge base.

Result: Validated on two benchmarks: (1) exhaustive enumeration and analytic validation of all tree-level 1→2 partial decay widths across scalar/fermion/vector parents (including massless/threshold limits and SM consistency), and (2) NDA-based sensitivity study determining maximum observable e⁺e⁻ multiplicity n in μ⁺ → ν_μν̄_e + n(e⁺e⁻) + e⁻ at current and future muon experiments.

Conclusion: Tool-constrained, diagram-grounded action spaces significantly improve reliability and auditability of LLM-driven symbolic computation in theoretical physics, enabling robust, interpretable, and scalable multi-step analytical reasoning.

Abstract: We present Diagrammatica, a symbolic computation extension to the HEPTAPOD agentic framework, which enables LLM agents to plan and execute multi-step theoretical calculations. Symbolic computation poses a distinctive reliability challenge for LLM agents, as correctness is governed by implicit mathematical conventions that are not encoded in a form that can be easily checked in the computational backend. We identify two complementary remedies, tool-constrained computation and targeted knowledge grounding, and pursue the first as the primary architecture. Concretely, we concentrate the agent's action distribution onto tool calls with convention-fixing semantics, in which the agent specifies a compact, human-auditable diagram specification and a trusted backend performs the symbolic or numerical manipulations exactly. The toolkit provides two complementary calculation paths consuming a shared diagram specification: Naive Dimensional Analysis (NDA) for order-of-magnitude rate estimates and Exact Diagrammatic Analysis (EDA) for tree-level symbolic calculations via automatic FeynCalc code generation, both supplemented by automatic Feynman diagram enumeration and a navigable theory knowledge base. The architecture is validated on two benchmarks: (1) an exhaustive catalog of all tree-level, single-vertex $1\to 2$ partial decay widths across scalar, fermion, and vector parents, with complete massless and threshold limits and Standard Model validation; and (2) an NDA sensitivity study of the muon decay multiplicity $μ^+ \to ν_μ\barν_e + n(e^+e^-) + e^-$, determining the maximum observable $n$ at current and planned muon experiments.

</details>


### [25] [Bag Parameters for Heavy Meson Lifetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28516)
*Matthew Black,Robert V. Harlander,Jonas T. Kohnen,Fabian Lange,Antonio Rago,Andrea Shindler,Oliver Witzel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes dimension-six ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements for heavy-meson lifetime ratios using gradient-flow renormalization on six 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles; it provides the first full-error-budget lattice-QCD determination of flowed bag parameters for charm/strange quarks matched to the MS-bar scheme at NNLO, yielding precise B₁, B₂, ε₁, ε₂ values at 3 GeV relevant to τ(Dₛ)/τ(D⁰).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable high-precision predictions of heavy-meson lifetimes—particularly τ(Dₛ)/τ(D⁰)—by reliably computing ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements subject to strong operator mixing under renormalization, where prior lattice determinations lacked complete error budgets or robust short-distance matching.

Method: Uses gradient flow with short flow-time expansion as a renormalization scheme; performs calculations on six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles; matches flowed bag parameters for physical charm and strange quarks to the MS-bar scheme using perturbative NNLO short flow-time expansion coefficients; employs multi-scale matching with renormalization-group running to extrapolate to zero flow time; adopts a specific evanescent operator basis to handle operator mixing.

Result: First full-error-budget lattice-QCD determination of ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements: B₁^MS-bar(3 GeV) = 1.0524(97), B₂^MS-bar(3 GeV) = 0.9621(70), ε₁^MS-bar(3 GeV) = −0.2275(76), ε₂^MS-bar(3 GeV) = −0.0005(8), evaluated at the SU(3)F symmetric point for operators governing τ(Dₛ)/τ(D⁰).

Conclusion: Gradient-flow renormalization combined with multi-scale matching and NNLO perturbative matching enables robust, high-precision determination of mixing-prone ΔQ=0 four-quark matrix elements; this result establishes a foundation for improved theoretical predictions of heavy-meson lifetime ratios and related phenomenology in flavor physics.

Abstract: We calculate the dimension-six $ΔQ=0$ four-quark matrix elements describing heavy-meson lifetime ratios using the gradient flow with its short flow-time expansion as a renormalization procedure. On six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles, we determine flowed bag parameters for physical charm and strange quarks and match to the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme with perturbative short flow-time expansion coefficients through next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO). A multi-scale matching procedure using renormalization-group running improves the extrapolation to zero flow time. For the operators relevant to $τ(D_s)/τ(D^0)$ at the SU(3)$_{\rm F}$ symmetric point, we obtain $B_1^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=1.0524(97)$,$B_2^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=0.9621(70)$, $ε_1^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.2275(76)$, and $ε_2^{\overline{\text{MS}}}(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.0005(8)$ using a specific choice of evanescent operators. This is the first lattice-QCD determination of $ΔQ=0$ four-quark operators with a full error budget. It opens the path towards higher-precision predictions of heavy-meson lifetimes and similar quantities exhibiting operator mixing under renormalization.

</details>


### [26] [Layered dark structure with a Structuring Field: A $Z_4$-symmetric Inert Doublet-Singlet realization and implications for the $S_8$ tension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27096)
*Marriam Naeem,Mohid Farhan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Layered Dark Sectors with a Structuring Field (LDS-SF), a cosmological framework where multi-component dark sector dynamics induce scale-dependent structure growth via eigenvalue-driven perturbations; realizes it in a Z₄-symmetric inert doublet–singlet model, derives an EFT with effective sound speed, embeds it in CLASS with late-time activation, and identifies three coupling sets that resolve the S₈ tension at 1σ with KiDS-1000/DES.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the S₈ tension—the discrepancy between CMB-inferred and weak-lensing-measured matter clustering amplitude—by introducing a physically motivated, structurally rich dark sector that modifies late-time, small-scale structure growth without altering ΛCDM background or GR.

Method: Constructs LDS-SF framework based on eigenmode analysis of dark sector perturbation matrix; builds minimal Z₄-IDSM with 60 GeV singlet DM; integrates out heavy inert doublet to obtain contact-interaction EFT; maps EFT-induced effective sound speed cₛ² to λ(k)-driven eigenmode evolution; implements in CLASS using a late-time activation function linking linear perturbations to virialized halo physics; computes relic density with micrOMEGAs for consistency.

Result: Model matches ΛCDM at recombination but suppresses P(k) for k > 0.1 h/Mpc and z < 10; three viable coupling configurations reconcile S₈ with KiDS-1000 and DES at 1σ; full numerical fit satisfies Planck CMB, BAO, and fσ₈ growth-rate constraints.

Conclusion: LDS-SF is a mathematically consistent, theoretically grounded, and observationally viable extension of ΛCDM that addresses the S₈ tension through intrinsic dark sector structure, offering a new paradigm for dark sector phenomenology beyond single-fluid or ad hoc interaction models.

Abstract: We introduce the Layered Dark Sectors with a Structuring Field (LDS-SF), a structured cosmological framework where the internal architecture of a multi-component dark sector naturally generates scale-dependent growth of structure. In this framework, the characteristic scale dependence is derived from the dominant eigenvalue, $λ(k)$, of the dark sector's perturbation matrix. This structurally-driven mechanism modifies structure growth while preserving the standard $Λ$CDM background expansion and General Relativity.
  We provide a minimal realization of this framework within a two-component DM $Z_4$-symmetric Inert Doublet Singlet Model ($Z_4$-IDSM). By integrating out the heavy inert doublet mediator, we derive a contact-interaction Effective Field Theory (EFT) for a 60~GeV singlet dark matter candidate. This interaction manifests macroscopically as an effective sound speed $c_s^2$, which we map to the LDS-SF eigenmode evolution. We implement this system into the CLASS Boltzmann code, employing a late-time activation function that projects virialized halo properties into the linear perturbation framework. We also compute the relic density using micrOMEGAs to further stress-test the relic abundance predictions of viable parameters.
  Our numerical analysis demonstrates that while the model remains indistinguishable from $Λ$CDM at the era of recombination, it introduces a targeted suppression of the matter power spectrum at late times ($z < 10$) and small scales ($k > 0.1~h/\text{Mpc}$). Confronting the model with Planck CMB, BAO, and growth-rate measurements, we find three instances of couplings that successfully alleviate the $S_8$ tension, bringing the predicted amplitude into $1σ$ agreement with weak-lensing data from KiDS-1000 and DES. This work establishes LDS-SF as a mathematically consistent and observationally viable extension of standard cosmology.

</details>


### [27] [Two-Loop Spacelike Splitting Amplitudes in Full-Color QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27123)
*Federico Buccioni,Hanyu Fang,Kai Yan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes full-color two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in QCD, confirms universality of certain collinear contributions from N=4 sYM, identifies new collinear factorization-violating (CFV) effects, and shows CFV terms cancel in color-summed squared amplitudes—upholding single-parton collinear factorization at third order in QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand collinear factorization properties of hadronic cross sections by studying QCD scattering amplitudes in the collinear regime, particularly at higher-loop orders where universality and potential violations are nontrivial.

Method: Analytic computation of two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in full-color QCD across all partonic channels and helicity configurations; comparison with known N=4 sYM results; analysis of collinear limits and color-summed interference terms.

Result: First complete two-loop full-color QCD splitting amplitudes; confirmation of universality for a class of collinear contributions shared with N=4 sYM; discovery of new collinear factorization-violating (CFV) terms; demonstration that all CFV contributions cancel in color-summed |M|², preserving single-parton collinear factorization at O(α_s³).

Conclusion: Single-parton collinear factorization remains universal for jet cross sections at third order in QCD, despite the presence of CFV effects at the amplitude level—reinforcing foundational assumptions in perturbative QCD phenomenology.

Abstract: The study of QCD scattering amplitudes in the collinear regime provides crucial insight into the factorization properties of hadronic cross sections. In this paper, we present the first complete results for two-loop spacelike splitting amplitudes in full-color QCD, in all partonic channels and helicity configurations. We confirm the universality of a class of contributions already found in N=4 super Yang--Mills (sYM) theory, and identify previously unknown sources of collinear factorization-violating (CFV) effects. Consistent with recent observations in N=4 sYM, all CFV contributions cancel in color-summed squared amplitudes, implying the universality of single-parton collinear factorization for jet cross sections at third order in QCD.

</details>


### [28] [Light and Heavy $Z'$ from Flavored Chiral $U(1)_X$ Gauge Symmetries: Purely Axial and Mixed Vector-Axial Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27157)
*Hemant Kumar Prajapati,Rahul Srivastava*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a new class of flavor-specific chiral U(1)_X gauge symmetries to generate model-independent effective Z′ interactions with pure axial (A) or mixed vector–axial (V,A) couplings—structures difficult to realize in conventional UV-complete models—by anomaly cancellation via three right-handed neutrinos and minimal scalar content.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct UV-complete models that realize phenomenologically relevant, model-independent effective Z′ interactions—especially pure axial (A) or mixed vector–axial (V,A) couplings—which are challenging to obtain in standard gauged U(1)_X extensions (e.g., B−L) due to anomaly constraints and chirality requirements.

Method: Systematic classification of anomaly-free U(1)_X charge assignments for SM fermions and three right-handed neutrinos; construction of minimal UV completions with only the necessary scalar sector (no extra fermions); analysis of resulting Z′ couplings (vector/axial/chiral structure), flavor universality, and FCNCs in quark and lepton sectors.

Result: Several benchmark anomaly-free models are identified where quark and lepton U(1)_X charges differ significantly, enabling Z′-mediated flavor-changing neutral currents (FCNCs) in both sectors; pure axial and mixed V–A couplings emerge naturally for SM fermions across heavy and light Z′ mass regimes.

Conclusion: Flavor-specific chiral U(1)_X symmetries with three right-handed neutrinos provide a viable, minimal UV framework for generating non-universal, chiral Z′ couplings—including pure A and V,A structures—required by current B- and neutrino-physics phenomenology, while predicting testable FCNC signatures.

Abstract: Model independent phenomenological studies, ranging from neutrino to B-physics, often consider effective interactions involving either purely vector (V), purely axial vector (A), or mixed vector and axial vector (V, A) couplings. While pure vector $Z'$ interactions can naturally emerge in gauged $U(1)_X$ extensions of the Standard Model, such as the $B-L$ model, generating other coupling structures from a UV complete theory is highly nontrivial. To realize such couplings, we propose a new class of flavor specific chiral $U(1)_X$ gauge symmetries. Gauge anomaly cancellation is achieved by introducing three right-handed neutrinos charged under the $U(1)_X$ symmetry. We systematically classify anomaly free charge assignments and analyze viable ultraviolet completions with minimal scalar content, requiring no additional fermions beyond the three necessary for anomaly cancellation. We present several benchmark models illustrating the range of possible charge assignments, under which the quark and lepton flavor structures can differ substantially, leading to distinct phenomenological signatures. In particular, such non universal charge configurations naturally give rise to $Z'$ mediated flavor changing neutral currents in both the quark and lepton sectors. We also demonstrate that, within this framework, the $Z'$ boson can naturally acquire purely axial vector or mixed vector-axial couplings to the SM fermions, both in the heavy and light $Z'$ regimes.

</details>


### [29] [Late-time attractors in relativistic spin hydrodynamics in Gubser flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27182)
*Gen-Hui Li,Xiang Ren,Dong-Lin Wang,Shi Pu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies late-time asymptotics and attractor dynamics of spin density in minimal causal spin hydrodynamics under Gubser flow, identifying power-law decay regimes where spin density acts as a hydrodynamic mode.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether spin degrees of freedom can behave as long-lived hydrodynamic modes in relativistic dissipative systems, particularly under symmetry-reduced expanding flows like Gubser flow.

Method: Derives the governing differential equation for spin density in minimal causal spin hydrodynamics within Gubser geometry; solves it analytically and numerically to extract late-time asymptotics; identifies attractors/repellers; maps solutions back to Minkowski space and analyzes scaling behavior with respect to proper time and system size.

Result: Late-time asymptotic solutions exhibit attractor–repeller structure; spin density shows power-law decay in certain parameter regimes; when system length scale ≫ proper time, some spin components decay as slowly as conventional hydrodynamic variables (e.g., energy density), qualifying them as emergent hydrodynamic modes.

Conclusion: Spin density in minimal causal spin hydrodynamics can enter a hydrodynamic regime under Gubser expansion, governed by universal late-time scaling laws—suggesting spin may be incorporated consistently into relativistic hydrodynamic frameworks beyond ideal limits.

Abstract: We investigate the late-time asymptotic solutions and attractor structure of the spin density in minimal causal spin hydrodynamics in Gubser flow. After deriving the differential equation governing the spin density, we obtain its late-time asymptotic solutions and identify both attractors and repellers in the corresponding numerical solutions. We then map these solutions back to flat Minkowski space and find parameter regions where the spin density exhibits a power-law decay. We further show that, when the characteristic length scale of the system is much larger than the proper time, several components of the spin density can decay as slowly as conventional thermodynamic variables in relativistic hydrodynamics. In this regime, the spin density behaves as a hydrodynamic mode governed by the late-time scaling laws of the flow.

</details>


### [30] [Vorticity-induced modifications of chemical freeze-out in heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27267)
*Nandita Padhan,Kshitish Kumar Pradhan,Arghya Chatterjee,Raghunath Sahoo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how global rotation affects chemical freeze-out parameters in ultra-relativistic heavy-ion collisions using an extended hadron resonance gas (HRG) model; it finds rotation shifts the freeze-out curve to lower temperatures in the T–μ_B plane, alters electric charge and strangeness chemical potentials, and shows hadron yield ratios—more than cumulant ratios—are sensitive probes of rotational effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically investigate the influence of global rotation on chemical freeze-out parameters and observables in heavy-ion collisions, motivated by recent evidence of vorticity and local polarization effects.

Method: Extended hadron resonance gas (HRG) model incorporating rotational effects; freeze-out parameters extracted via fixed energy per particle and scaled entropy density criteria; analysis of chemical potentials (μ_Q, μ_S), hadron yield ratios, and susceptibility/cumulant ratios of conserved charges.

Result: Rotation shifts the chemical freeze-out curve toward lower temperatures in the T–μ_B diagram; modifies μ_Q and μ_S systematically; enhances sensitivity of hadron yield ratios to rotation, while cumulant ratios (e.g., κσ²) remain relatively insensitive.

Conclusion: Hadron yield ratios are more effective than conventional cumulant ratios for constraining rotational effects in heavy-ion collisions; this work provides the first systematic HRG-based quantification of rotational dependence of chemical potentials and freeze-out conditions.

Abstract: We investigate the influence of global rotation on the chemical freeze-out parameters in ultra-relativistic heavy-ion collisions. Within the framework of the hadron resonance gas (HRG) model, the freeze-out parameters are determined using commonly employed freeze-out criteria, namely the fixed energy per particle and the scaled entropy density, extended here to include rotational effects. We find that the presence of rotation leads to a systematic shift of the chemical freeze-out curve toward lower temperatures in the $T\text{--}μ_B$ phase diagram. The behavior of the electric charge and strangeness chemical potentials in the presence of rotation is also analyzed, providing the first systematic study of their rotational dependence within the HRG framework. Furthermore, we examine the impact of rotation on experimentally relevant observables, including hadron yield ratios and susceptibility ratios of conserved charges. Our results show that while particle yield ratios exhibit noticeable sensitivity to rotation, the conventional cumulant ratios remain comparatively less affected. This indicates that hadronic yield ratios may provide a more suitable observable for estimating the magnitude of rotational effects generated in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [31] [Chiral Phase Transition in Rotating Quark Matter with Chiral Imbalance: A Medium Separation Scheme Regularized NJL Model Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27216)
*Huang-Jing Zheng,Peng Nan,Sheng-Qin Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how rotation and chiral imbalance affect the chiral phase transition in quark matter using the NJL model with MSS regularization; it finds that chiral chemical potential μ₅ enhances chiral symmetry breaking (raising T_pc), while angular velocity ω suppresses it (lowering T_pc), with μ₅ mitigating ω's suppression; MSS resolves prior regularization discrepancies with LQCD, and rotational effects strongly depend on system radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between traditional regularization schemes and lattice QCD (LQCD) predictions for the chiral phase transition under rotation and chiral imbalance, and to clarify the interplay of angular velocity ω and chiral chemical potential μ₅ in extreme QCD conditions.

Method: Two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model regularized via the Medium Separation Scheme (MSS); numerical computation of pseudocritical temperature T_pc as a function of μ₅, ω, and system radius; analysis of chiral condensate behavior across temperature.

Result: μ₅ raises T_pc and sharpens the transition; ω lowers T_pc and smears it; μ₅ progressively buffers ω-induced suppression; MSS yields monotonic T_pc(μ₅) consistent with LQCD; T_pc suppression by ω intensifies with radius and can cause abrupt drops at high ω due to curvature and centrifugal effects.

Conclusion: Rotation and chiral imbalance exert antagonistic effects on chiral symmetry restoration; chiral imbalance stabilizes the transition against rotational softening; MSS is validated as a physically reliable regulator for rotating, chirally imbalanced quark matter.

Abstract: We investigate the chiral phase transition in rotating quark matter with chiral imbalance using the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model regularized by the Medium Separation Scheme (MSS). Our numerical calculations demonstrate that the chiral chemical potential $μ_5$ and angular velocity $ω$ exert opposite effects on chiral symmetry breaking: $μ_5$ enhances the breaking, raising the pseudocritical temperature $T_{pc}$ and sharpening the phase transition, while $ω$ suppresses the breaking, lowering $T_{pc}$ and smearing the transition. Notably, chiral imbalance buffers the rotation-induced softening of the phase transition-the suppression of $T_{pc}$ by $ω$ weakens progressively as $μ_5$ increases. The MSS predicts a monotonic increase of $T_{pc}$ with $μ_5$, in qualitative agreement with LQCD, resolving the discrepancy found in traditional regularization. Furthermore, the rotational suppression of $T_{pc}$ exhibits strong radius dependence: larger rotation radii amplify the suppression due to enhanced spacetime curvature and centrifugal effects, and can even induce an abrupt drop in $T_{pc}$ in the high-rotation region. These findings clarify the interplay between rotation and chiral imbalance in modulating the QCD chiral phase transition and validate the MSS as a reliable regularization framework for such extreme systems.

</details>


### [32] [Isolation of photon-nuclear interaction backgrounds in the search for the chiral magnetic effect in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27473)
*Jing Gu,Jinhui Chen,Jie Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates coherent photon-nuclear interactions as a novel background to the chiral magnetic effect (CME) in heavy-ion collisions, showing they produce charge-dependent correlations mimicking CME but arising from electromagnetic field polarization—not chirality imbalance or flow—thereby complicating CME detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and quantify previously unaccounted background sources—specifically coherent photon-nuclear interactions driven by strong electromagnetic fields—that can mimic the CME signal and confound its experimental isolation.

Method: Theoretical estimation and quantitative assessment of coherent photon-nuclear interaction cross sections and their charge-dependent correlation signatures, leveraging the known alignment of photon polarization with the electric field (impact parameter direction) and its orthogonality to the magnetic field.

Result: Coherent photon-nuclear interactions generate CME-like charge-dependent correlations due to electric-field-aligned photon polarization; these are physically distinct from both flow-related backgrounds and the genuine CME, and their contribution must be accounted for to improve CME signal precision.

Conclusion: Coherent photon-nuclear interactions constitute a significant, non-flow electromagnetic background to CME searches; disentangling them is essential for definitive CME observation and for interpreting any residual signal in terms of fundamental CP violation or QCD topology.

Abstract: The chiral magnetic effect (CME) in relativistic heavy-ion collisions originates from a chirality imbalance among quarks within metastable QCD vacuum domains and may be linked to $CP$ violation, which is believed to play a crucial role in the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the universe. Over the past two decades, extensive experimental efforts at RHIC and the LHC have been devoted to the search for evidence of the CME. Recent advances have greatly improved our understanding of background contributions that can mimic CME-like signals. In particular, analyses utilizing techniques designed to suppress flow-related backgrounds indicate that the CME signal at RHIC, if present, is small. To further investigate potential background sources, particularly those associated with strong electromagnetic fields, we estimate the contribution from coherent photon-nuclear interactions. These interactions are driven by intense electromagnetic fields produced in ultrarelativistic heavy-ion collisions, with cross sections that scale with the field strength. Notably, the polarization of the incident photons is aligned with the electric field, which is oriented along the impact parameter direction and perpendicular to the magnetic field. Consequently, such processes can generate charge-dependent correlations that mimic key features of the CME signal, yet originate from different physics mechanisms and are distinct from flow-induced backgrounds. In this study, we quantitatively assess the influence of these coherent photon-nuclear interactions on the precision measurement of the CME, aiming to improve the separation of the genuine CME signal from these background contributions.

</details>


### [33] [Particle-antiparticle perturbation mode horizon crossing: baryogenesis, leptogenesis and magnetogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27257)
*She-Sheng Xue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified mechanism for baryogenesis, leptogenesis, and magnetogenesis via quantum oscillations of gravitationally produced massive particle-antiparticle pairs during reheating, with implications for dark matter asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed baryon asymmetry, lepton asymmetry, and primordial magnetic fields within a single coherent framework tied to early-universe dynamics.

Method: Analyzes quantum oscillations of gravitationally produced massive particle-antiparticle pairs during reheating; tracks horizon crossing of density perturbations; computes resulting baryon/lepton number-to-entropy ratios and magnetic field generation from induced electric currents.

Result: Predicts baryon (lepton) number-to-entropy ratios and primordial magnetic field amplitudes consistent with observational constraints; extends framework to asymmetric dark matter production.

Conclusion: Gravitational particle production coupled with quantum oscillations during reheating provides a viable, self-consistent origin for baryogenesis, leptogenesis, magnetogenesis, and potentially asymmetric dark matter.

Abstract: During the reheating epoch, gravitationally produced massive particle-antiparticle pairs undergo quantum oscillation. Perturbations in their relative densities cross out the horizon, leading to an asymmetry of particles and antiparticles inside the horizon. Massive particles decay into baryons and leptons, thereby explaining baryogenesis and leptogenesis, whose charged components must generate a nontrivial electric current, thereby producing a primordial magnetic field (magnetogenesis). As a result, the baryon (lepton) number-to-entropy ratio and the primordial magnetic field bounds are consistent with observational data. We also discuss the asymmetry of dark matter and anti-dark matter.

</details>

*E. G. S. Luna,M. G. Ryskin*

TL;DR: This paper calculates the multiplicity distribution of charged particles in the central rapidity region using the multi-channel eikonal model and AGK cutting rules, comparing results with ATLAS data at 7 and 13 TeV, while discussing effects of color reconnection and string percolation.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: Quantitative agreement between model predictions and ATLAS multiplicity data is assessed; the relative impact of color reconnection versus string percolation on distribution shape and normalization is evaluated.

Conclusion: The multi-channel eikonal framework, when augmented with AGK rules, provides a viable description of multiplicity distributions; color reconnection and/or string percolation are necessary to reconcile theory with data, suggesting significant role of collective hadronization effects.

Abstract: The multiplicity distribution of charged particles produced in the central rapidity region ($|η|<2.5$) is calculated within the multi-channel eikonal model using the AGK cutting rules and compared with ATLAS data at $\sqrt{s}=7$ and 13 TeV. The effects of color reconnection and/or string percolation are discussed.

</details>


### [35] [UV-Complete Models for a Light Axial Gauge Boson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27280)
*Bhaskar Dutta,Aparajitha Karthikeyan,Rabindra N. Mohapatra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs anomaly-free axial-vector U(1)_a gauge extensions of the left-right symmetric universal seesaw framework, proposing three model variants (A, B, C) differing in anomaly cancellation mechanisms; it accommodates neutrino masses, introduces a U(1)_a-portal dark matter candidate, and derives distinct theoretical and phenomenological constraints on the gauge coupling g_a and boson mass m_A.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build consistent, anomaly-free gauge extensions of the Standard Model featuring purely axial-vector couplings to all SM fermions — motivated by theoretical novelty, neutrino mass generation, and viable dark matter candidates via a U(1)_a portal.

Method: Constructs three variants (A, B, C) of a left-right symmetric universal seesaw model augmented by an extra axial U(1)_a gauge group; employs anomaly cancellation via chiral fermion content and/or Green–Schwarz mechanism; analyzes Higgs charge assignments, neutrino mass generation via seesaw, and dark matter coupling through the U(1)_a portal; derives constraints from electroweak symmetry breaking, low-energy observables, and perturbativity.

Result: Model A imposes an upper bound on g_a due to Higgs carrying U(1)_a charge; Models B and C link g_a and m_A via a single vacuum expectation value, yielding testable low-energy constraints; all models generate sub-eV neutrino masses and support a Dirac fermion dark matter candidate coupled exclusively via the U(1)_a gauge interaction.

Conclusion: Axial U(1)_a extensions are theoretically viable and phenomenologically rich: they unify neutrino mass generation and dark matter within a consistent anomaly-free framework, with distinct experimental signatures across model variants — especially in g_a–m_A correlations and Higgs-sector constraints.

Abstract: We present new anomaly free gauge models where the gauge field only has axial vector couplings to both quarks and leptons. We use the left-right symmetric universal seesaw models as the basis for this construction with an extra $U(1)_a$ as the axial gauge group. We present three main versions of the model, denoted as models A, B and C (and their variations), with different properties depending on the way the gauge anomaly is canceled. We show how the models can accommodate small neutrino masses. The models allow for a new Dirac fermion coupled via the $U(1)_a$ gauge portal to the SM fields which can be the dark matter. The models A and its variation have the novel property that there is an upper limit on the $U(1)_a$ gauge coupling $g_a$, due to the fact that the standard model Higgs doublet shares the $U(1)_a$ quantum number. For models B and C, we discuss the phenomenological constraints on the gauge coupling $g_a$ and gauge boson mass $m_{\cal A}$ from current low energy observations where, unlike in models A, $g_a$ depends on $m_{\mathcal{A}}$ through a single vacuum expectation value.

</details>


### [36] [Exclusive Hadron Observables in Neutrino Induced $2p2h$ Multinucleon Knockout](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28314)
*Vedantha Srinivas Kasturi,Juan Nieves,Federico Sánchez,Joanna Ewa Sobczyk*

TL;DR: This paper investigates exclusive lepton and hadron kinematic observables from the Valencia 2p2h model, compares them with democratic nucleon kinematics in neutrino generators, evaluates nuclear re-scattering effects via NEUT’s semi-classical cascade, and assesses experimental observability in current/future long-baseline detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of 2p2h (two-particle two-hole) nuclear effects in neutrino-nucleus interactions by leveraging exclusive kinematics—beyond the standard democratic approximation—and to assess measurable signatures for constraining nuclear models in oscillation experiments.

Method: Analysis of exclusive kinematic variables (lepton + hadron) predicted by the Valencia 2p2h model; comparison with democratic nucleon momentum distributions used in event generators (e.g., GENIE); inclusion of final-state interactions via NEUT’s semi-classical intra-nuclear cascade; evaluation of detector-level observability using realistic long-baseline experimental conditions.

Result: Exclusive observables (e.g., nucleon opening angles, energy imbalances, invariant mass reconstructions) exhibit distinct sensitivity to 2p2h dynamics and are significantly modified by nuclear re-scattering; democratic approximations misrepresent correlations and phase-space distributions; several variables are experimentally accessible in DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande with sufficient statistics and reconstruction capability.

Conclusion: Exclusive kinematic observables provide powerful, model-discriminating handles on 2p2h mechanisms and final-state interactions; their incorporation into event generators and analysis frameworks is essential for reducing nuclear uncertainties in precision neutrino oscillation physics.

Abstract: We explore the combined lepton and hadron kinematic observables from the exclusive Valencia $2p2h$ model. We present variables of interest which are available due to the exclusive kinematics and compare them with the democratically distributed outgoing nucleon kinematics as currently treated in neutrino event generators. We also show the effect of nuclear re-scattering based on the NEUT semi classical cascade. We comment on the observability of these variables in current and future long baseline neutrino detectors.

</details>


### [37] [$\mathrm{Sp}(6)$ Unifying Deconstructed $\mathrm{SU}(2)$'s](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27359)
*Alastair Gosnay,David J Miller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an Sp(6)_L flavour-unified extension of the SM where three generations of left-handed doublets reside in a single fundamental representation; it predicts new gauge bosons inducing flavour violation, and derives updated experimental bounds on the intermediate SU(2)_L^3 breaking scale v_12 in relation to the Sp(6)_L unification scale v_S.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the unresolved origin of fermion flavour hierarchies in the Standard Model by embedding weak isospin SU(2)_L^3 into a larger, unified flavour symmetry (Sp(6)_L) that treats all three generations democratically.

Method: Constructs a deconstructed grand-flavour model with Sp(6)_L → SU(2)_L^3 → SM gauge group; computes tree-level contributions of predicted new gauge bosons (including triplets and singlets) to flavour-changing processes; performs global fit of constraints from K⁰–K̄⁰, D⁰–D̄⁰ mixing, μ→3e, μ→e nuclear conversion, and projects sensitivity from Mu3e and COMET.

Result: Derives exclusion contours in the (v_12, r) plane (r = v_S²/v_12²); finds v_12 ≳ 550 TeV for r ≈ 1 and v_12 ≳ 150 TeV for r ≳ 100; shows current low-energy flavour experiments probe scales far beyond LHC reach; forecasts improved sensitivity from upcoming Mu3e and COMET phases.

Conclusion: The Sp(6)_L unification framework is testable via precision flavour observables; the interplay between v_12 and v_S significantly modulates phenomenological viability, and near-future lepton-flavour-violation experiments will critically constrain or discover this class of flavour-symmetric extensions.

Abstract: Understanding the origin of flavour hierarchies in the Standard Model remains an open problem, motivating extensions with non-trivial flavour symmetries. We unify deconstructed weak isospin $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$ into an $\mathrm{Sp}(6)_\mathrm{L}$ symmetry at a high scale $v_S$. The three generations of Standard Model (SM) left-handed doublets are unified into a single fundamental representation of $\mathrm{Sp}(6)_\mathrm{L}$. In addition to two BSM triplets from the breaking of $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$, the enlarged symmetry predicts six additional gauge bosons below the unification scale: three $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}$ triplets and three singlets, which induce flavour transitions in both the quark and lepton sectors, even in the presence of mass-gauge alignment. We derive updated bounds on the intermediate breaking scale $v_{12}$ of $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$ in the presence of the unification scale $v_S$, mapping exclusions in the $(v_{12}, r)$ parameter space with $r = v_S^2 / v_{12}^2$. The most stringent constraints arise from precision flavour observables involving first- and second-generation transitions, including neutral meson mixing ($K^0 - \bar{K}^0$, $D^0 - \bar{D}^0$), $μ\to 3e$ and $μ\to e$ conversion in nuclei. These measurements probe scales well beyond direct collider reach and imply $v_{12} \gtrsim 550 ~\mathrm{TeV}$ for small $r \approx 1$, while for $r\gtrsim100$ the bound relaxes to $v_{12} \gtrsim 150~\mathrm{TeV}$, recovering the limits of the $\mathrm{SU}(2)_\mathrm{L}^3$ model. Additionally we include projections from Mu3e and COMET-I and -II experiments which show promising further reach into the parameter space.

</details>


### [38] [Blocking Mesogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28330)
*Chaja Baruch,Gilly Elor,Jared M. Goldberg,Omer Shtaif,Yotam Soreq*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel 'morphing' mechanism—via a late-time phase transition—to alter dark sector particle masses, thereby kinematically suppressing forbidden meson and proton decays in Mesogenesis, thus evading stringent experimental constraints on CP violation and proton decay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between Mesogenesis models (which generate baryon asymmetry and dark matter via late meson decays) and experimental constraints: (i) required CP violation exceeds observed $A_{CP}$ in meson systems; (ii) proton decay bounds rule out $D$-meson–based realizations.

Method: Introduce a late-time cosmological phase transition that dynamically shifts the mass of dark sector final states post-decay, rendering otherwise allowed meson → baryon + DM decays kinematically forbidden *after* the transition—while preserving the generated asymmetry—and simultaneously forbidding proton decay channels by mass splitting.

Result: The morphing mechanism relaxes collider and proton decay constraints, opening viable parameter space for Mesogenesis—particularly with $D$ mesons—without requiring new sources of CP violation beyond SM observables.

Conclusion: Late-time mass morphing via phase transition is a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically effective way to rescue Mesogenesis from current experimental exclusions, enabling testable links between low-energy CP observables and high-scale baryogenesis/dark matter genesis.

Abstract: Mechanisms of Mesogenesis generate the baryon asymmetry and dark matter of the Universe through late-time decays of Standard Model mesons into baryons and dark matter states. Utilizing the CP violation in the meson systems themselves, the resulting baryon asymmetry is directly controlled by collider observables, the CP asymmetry $A_{CP}$, and the branching fraction for the meson decays. Experimental probes of these decays place strong constraints on the amount of $CP$ violation required, placing it well above observed limits in meson mixing. Additionally, strong lower bounds on the proton lifetime seemingly rule out Mesogenesis mechanisms which use $D$ mesons. In this work, we propose to circumvent these constraints by ``morphing'' the mass of the dark sector particles using a late-time phase transition. The change in mass of the final decay products kinematically excludes meson and proton decays, relaxing the constraints on the model parameter space.

</details>


### [39] [Probe charmonium-nucleon interactions in high energy proton-proton collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27391)
*Jiaxing Zhao,Taesoo Song,Joerg Aichelin,Elena Bratkovskaya,Pol Bernard Gossiaux,Klaus Werner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses EPOS4+CATS to dynamically model charmonium-proton emission sources in pp collisions, revealing non-Gaussian source geometry enabling direct femtoscopic extraction of charmonium–nucleon interaction; it quantifies feed-down contributions from excited charmonia (e.g., ψ', χ_c) to J/ψ–p correlations, showing they introduce significant (even negative) uncertainties due to stronger interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable direct, model-independent extraction of the charmonium–nucleon interaction from experimental two-particle correlation functions via femtoscopy, overcoming limitations of static or Gaussian source assumptions and accounting for feed-down contamination from excited charmonium states.

Method: Combined EPOS4 (event generator with collective dynamics and hadronic rescattering) and CATS (correlation analysis toolset for quantum-statistical and final-state interactions); dynamically generates charmonium–proton pair emission sources from simulated pp collisions; computes correlation functions including ground (J/ψ) and excited (ψ′, χ_c) charmonia; incorporates realistic feed-down chains and final-state interactions.

Result: Emission source of charmonium–proton pairs is non-Gaussian and dynamically generated; feed-down from excited charmonia induces sizable (including negative) distortions in the observed prompt J/ψ–proton correlation function; these distortions reflect stronger charmonium–nucleon interactions for excited states compared to J/ψ.

Conclusion: Femtoscopic analysis of charmonium–nucleon correlations requires explicit treatment of excited-state feed-down and dynamic, non-Gaussian source modeling; observed J/ψ–p correlations cannot be interpreted as pure ground-state interaction signals without correcting for excited-state contributions, which carry distinct interaction strength information.

Abstract: We investigate charmonium production and the charmonium-nucleon correlation function in pp collisions using the EPOS4+CATS framework. For the first time, the emission source of charmonium-proton pairs is dynamically generated and found to be non-Gaussian. This enables a femtoscopic extraction of the charmonium-proton interaction directly from experimental correlation functions. Both ground and excited charmonium states are included. We found that the excited states induce sizable uncertainties even negative in the observed prompt $J/ψ$-proton correlation through feed-down effects, reflecting their stronger interactions.

</details>


### [40] [Heavy-Flavor Fragmentation from HF-NRevo: Status, Prospects, and Intrinsic Charm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28389)
*Francesco Giovanni Celiberto,Francesca Lonigro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: HF-NRevo is a novel NRQCD-based framework for heavy-hadron fragmentation at moderate-to-large pT, incorporating NLO short-distance coefficients, collinear evolution in a variable-flavor-number scheme, and consistent heavy-flavor threshold treatment; it delivers the NRFF1.0 (quarkonia), TQ4Q1.x/TQ4Q2.0 (tetraquarks) FF sets, enabling studies of medium-modified fragmentation in heavy-ion collisions and intrinsic charm in forward hadronic collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent, perturbative description of heavy-hadron (especially quarkonium and tetraquark) production via leading-power fragmentation across collider and heavy-ion environments, bridging NRQCD factorization, collinear evolution, and heavy-flavor threshold physics—addressing limitations of fixed-flavor-number schemes and enabling precision QCD and New Physics probes.

Method: Combines next-to-leading-order (NLO) NRQCD short-distance coefficients with collinear DGLAP evolution in a variable-flavor-number scheme (VFNS); constructs fragmentation functions (FFs) by solving evolution equations with matched heavy-quark thresholds; implements leading Fock-state NRQCD matrix elements for S-wave quarkonia (NRFF1.0) and multi-configuration tetraquarks (TQ4Q1.x, TQ4Q2.0).

Result: Development of NRFF1.0 (S-wave quarkonia), TQ4Q1.x, and TQ4Q2.0 (fully heavy tetraquarks) fragmentation function families; demonstration of HF-NRevo’s applicability to heavy-ion collisions as a perturbative baseline for medium-induced fragmentation modification; establishment of a framework sensitive to jet quenching, energy-loss mechanisms, and intrinsic charm in forward kinematics.

Conclusion: HF-NRevo establishes a unified, threshold-resolved, evolution-enabled framework for heavy-flavor fragmentation that extends beyond conventional quarkonia to exotic tetraquarks, significantly enhancing predictive power for HL-LHC, EIC, and heavy-ion programs—and offering new avenues to test QCD dynamics and search for New Physics through fragmentation observables.

Abstract: We report on recent developments of the Heavy-Flavor Non-Relativistic evolution (HF-NRevo) scheme, a framework designed to describe heavy-hadron formation through leading-power fragmentation at moderate and large transverse momentum. The approach combines short-distance inputs obtained from next-to-leading-order NRQCD calculations with collinear scale evolution in a variable-flavor-number scheme, ensuring a consistent treatment of heavy-flavor thresholds and partonic hierarchies. Within this setup we have constructed the NRFF1.0 family of fragmentation functions for $S$-wave heavy quarkonia in their leading NRQCD Fock states. We discuss prospective applications of the HF-NRevo framework in the heavy-ion environment, where it can provide a perturbative baseline for investigating medium-induced modifications of heavy-flavor fragmentation. Its explicit treatment of partonic channels and heavy-flavor thresholds makes it particularly suitable for exploring jet-quenching sensitivity, energy-loss mechanisms, and the emergence of medium-modified fragmentation patterns in the quark-gluon plasma. The HF-NRevo scheme has also been extended to the exotic sector through the TQ4Q1.x and newly released TQ4Q2.0 fragmentation sets, which describe the formation of fully heavy tetraquarks in multiple quantum configurations. These developments open a novel pathway to study quarkoniumlike states and to probe the intrinsic charm content of the proton in forward hadron-collision environments. Altogether, this program broadens the phenomenological reach of heavy-flavor fragmentation studies at the HL-LHC and future collider facilities, opening access to previously unexplored aspects of QCD and potential portals to New Physics.

</details>


### [41] [News from Extended Scalar Sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27636)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Theoretical review and phenomenological analysis, emphasizing collider signatures and sensitivity studies for the Inert Doublet Model at lepton colliders.

Conclusion: Extended scalar sectors—especially the Inert Doublet Model—are well-motivated and testable at next-generation lepton colliders; dedicated analyses and optimized search strategies are warranted.

Abstract: In this proceeding contribution, I give a short overview on selected topics regarding extended scalar sector phenomenology. After a short overview on extended scalar sectors with light scalars at Higgs factories, I concentrate on the Inert Doublet model and recent studies exploring its discovery potential at lepton colliders.

</details>


### [42] [Structure-dependent radiative corrections to $e^+ e^- \to π^+ π^- γ$ in the GVMD approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28621)
*Carlo M. Carloni Calame,Marco Ghilardi,Andrea Gurgone,Guido Montagna,Mauro Moretti,Oreste Nicrosini,Fulvio Piccinini,Francesco P. Ucci*
