  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision in radiative return measurements of e+e−→π+π−γ at flavor factories by properly accounting for non-perturbative pion structure in next-to-leading-order radiative corrections, especially final-state radiation and initial-final state interference.

Method: One-loop calculation incorporating the pion electromagnetic form factor via the generalized vector meson dominance (VMD) model; treats final-state radiation and initial-final state interference beyond naive scalar QED factorization.

Result: Predictions for experimentally relevant observables in e+e−→π+π−γ differ significantly from the naive factorised scalar QED approach; the VMD-based treatment provides a more realistic estimate of model-dependent uncertainties in radiative return analyses.

Conclusion: The generalized VMD-based one-loop computation extends prior work on e+e−→π+π− energy scans and offers a robust framework to quantify systematic uncertainties arising from pion-photon interaction modeling in precision hadronic cross section measurements.

Abstract: We compute the radiative corrections to the process of two-pion production in association with a hard photon in $e^+ e^-$ annihilation by taking into account the non-perturbative structure of the pion in the one-loop calculation. For this purpose, we adopt the generalised vector meson dominance model to insert the pion form factor in loop integrals for the treatment of final-state radiation and initial-final state interference at next-to-leading order. We compare our predictions with the results of the naive factorised scalar QED approach for experimentally relevant observables in the measurement of the $e^+ e^- \to π^+ π^- γ$ process. The computation extends previous results obtained for the energy scan process $e^+ e^- \to π^+ π^-$ and can be used to quantify the uncertainty due to the model describing the pion-photon interaction in radiative return experiments at flavour factories.

</details>


### [43] [Absence of Quadratic-Order Sensitivity to Small Neutrino Mass Splittings in Disappearance Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27681)
*Sanjeev Kumar Verma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a limitation in neutrino disappearance analyses where small mass-squared splittings (Δm²) become statistically indistinguishable when smooth spectral uncertainties are modeled via profiled nuisance parameters — because the leading (quadratic) oscillatory distortion is absorbed by the systematic model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand why sensitivity to small Δm² vanishes in binned neutrino oscillation fits with smooth spectral uncertainties, despite expected quadratic dependence of oscillation effects on Δm².

Method: Analytical derivation in the small-phase limit, examining how the quadratic-in-Δm² spectral distortion from neutrino oscillations interacts with smoothly parameterized nuisance deformations in a chi-squared fit with profiled parameters.

Result: Demonstrates that if the nuisance parameterization spans the functional form of the quadratic oscillation-induced spectral deformation, the profiled chi-squared becomes independent of Δm² at O(Δm⁴), eliminating first-order sensitivity; residual sensitivity arises only from higher-order (e.g., quartic or interference) terms or constraints on nuisance freedom.

Conclusion: Standard profiled-likelihood or chi-squared fits with flexible smooth systematics can suffer from 'Δm² degeneracy' — loss of sensitivity to small splittings — unless nuisance parameterizations are deliberately restricted or higher-order oscillation features are leveraged.

Abstract: Neutrino disappearance measurements using binned reconstructed-energy spectra exhibit a regime in which small mass-squared splittings become unidentifiable at quadratic order when smooth spectral shape uncertainties are represented by profiled nuisance parameters in the fit. In the small-phase limit, the oscillation-induced modification of the detected spectrum is quadratic in the mass-squared splitting and produces a smooth deformation of the reconstructed-energy distribution. If the nuisance deformation functions used in the fit can reproduce this energy dependence across the fitted bins, the quadratic oscillation-induced distortion can be absorbed by the systematic deformation space and the profiled chi-squared remains unchanged at this order. Sensitivity to the mass-squared splitting then arises only from higher-order oscillation effects or from restrictions imposed on the allowed smooth spectral freedom.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the persistent $P_5'$ anomaly and newly hinted non-zero $S_7$ in $B \to K^* \mu^+\mu^-$ decays, distinguishing between new physics contributions and hadronic effects, especially since $S_7 \neq 0$ cannot arise from heavy new physics alone.

Method: Theoretical analysis using angular observables in $B \to K^* \mu^+\mu^-$ decays; decomposition of amplitudes into short-distance (Wilson coefficients) and long-distance (hadronic) contributions; identification of necessary strong phase for $S_7 \neq 0$; construction and interpretation of direct CP asymmetry; global fit including both hadronic parameters and Wilson coefficients.

Result: A non-zero $S_7$ unambiguously signals a significant hadronic strong phase; under plausible assumptions, this same hadronic effect can account for the $P_5'$ anomaly; the direct CP asymmetry is shown to be theoretically clean and sensitive to new physics; combined fits yield stringent upper bounds on the imaginary parts of new physics Wilson coefficients (e.g., $\text{Im}(C_9^{\text{NP}})$, $\text{Im}(C_{10}^{\text{NP}})$).

Conclusion: The $S_7$ observable provides a decisive diagnostic: its non-vanishing value confirms dominant hadronic interference with a strong phase, challenging pure new physics interpretations of $P_5'$; however, residual sensitivity to complex Wilson coefficients remains via CP-violating observables, enabling meaningful constraints on new physics models with CP violation.

Abstract: Recent results from LHCb have confirmed the long-standing $P_5^\prime$ anomaly, an intriguing discrepancy in the angular distribution of the $B \to K^* μ^+μ^-$ decay that might be a sign of new physics. In addition, the new results hint at a non-zero value for $S_7$, another observable that characterizes the $B \to K^* μ^+μ^-$ angular distribution. We stress that a non-zero $S_7$ cannot be explained by heavy new physics but instead necessarily requires a sizable hadronic effect that introduces a strong phase. We argue that, under plausible assumptions, the hadronic effect is of the correct size to also explain $P_5^\prime$. The direct CP asymmetry in $B \to K^* μ^+μ^-$ emerges in principle as a clean probe of new physics in such a scenario. We show that a combined fit of hadronic parameters and Wilson coefficients retains sensitivity to new physics and we find strong bounds on imaginary parts of new physics Wilson coefficients.

</details>


### [45] [Associated Higgs production in lepton-photon collisions at FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27822)

Abstract: Following the HL-LHC era, proposed lepton colliders highlight the need to study various important Higgs boson production mechanisms to precisely probe the Standard Model Higgs sector. We propose a novel mechanism $e^\pm γ\rightarrow {\bar ν}_e (ν_e) H W^\pm$, which can be useful to study Higgs boson properties. This channel is relatively free from the background and can be used to measure the Higgs boson properties, in particular $WWH$ coupling. We examine the viability of this production mechanism. We show that the process can be observed at the planned FCC-ee with the center-of-mass energy of 365 GeV. At the center-of-mass energy of 500 GeV, the process can be observed within a few months of the operation. We use an in-house Monte Carlo event generator that simultaneously incorporates the photon distribution and electron/positron distribution. Our work is also a step towards realistic simulations of lepton- and photon-initiated processes at lepton colliders.

</details>


### [46] [Full energy fraction and angular dependence of medium-induced splittings in the large-$N_c$ limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27846)
*Carlota Andres,Fabio Dominguez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a semi-analytical framework for medium-induced $1\to2$ parton splittings in the quark-gluon plasma, fully differential in energy fraction $z$ and angle $\theta$, using the large-$N_c$ limit and harmonic oscillator approximation; it also introduces and validates an improved semi-hard approximation (ISHA) that extends SHA with inverse-energy corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the soft-gluon approximation and achieve a quantitative description of jet substructure modifications in heavy-ion collisions by modeling medium-induced splittings with full $z$- and $\theta$-dependence and multiple scattering resummation.

Method: Analytical evaluation of path integrals for medium-induced $1\to2$ splittings in the large-$N_c$ limit under the harmonic oscillator approximation; derivation and systematic extension of the semi-hard approximation (SHA) to include leading $1/E$ corrections, yielding the improved semi-hard approximation (ISHA); validation via comparison between ISHA and large-$N_c$-HO results.

Result: Full double-differential ($z$, $\theta$) semi-analytical expressions for all splitting channels under large-$N_c$-HO; demonstration that SHA fails across most of phase space—even at high energies—whereas ISHA remains robust for sufficiently energetic partons.

Conclusion: The large-$N_c$-HO framework provides an efficient, exact (within its assumptions) baseline for medium-induced splittings; ISHA is a physically justified and computationally practical alternative to SHA, significantly improving reliability for energetic splittings relevant to jet quenching phenomenology.

Abstract: Jets produced in relativistic heavy-ion collisions are modified by their interactions with the quark-gluon plasma (QGP), making jet substructure observables sensitive probes of QGP dynamics. A quantitative description of these modifications requires understanding how the medium affects elementary parton splittings with full dependence on both their energy fraction $z$ and splitting angle $θ$, beyond the widely used soft emitted-gluon approximation. Here, we study medium-induced $1 \to 2$ splittings double-differential in $z$ and $θ$, with full resummation of multiple scatterings, and show that in the large-$N_c$ limit and under the harmonic oscillator (HO) approximation, all path integrals can be evaluated analytically for any splitting channel, providing a computationally efficient semi-analytical result. We also revisit the semi-hard approximation (SHA), extending it to include leading corrections in inverse powers of the partons energies, which we denote the improved semi-hard approximation (ISHA), and assess its validity through a comparison with the large-$N_c$-HO results. Our analysis shows that while the SHA is found to be unreliable across most of phase space, even for high-energy emitters, the ISHA provides a robust approximation for splittings where all partons are sufficiently energetic.

</details>


### [47] [Study of neutrinophilic low-mass dark matter mediated by pseudoscalar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28034)
*Zhuo Zhang,Lian-Bao Jia,Reyes J. F. Eduardo*

TL;DR: This paper studies a neutrinophilic low-mass dark matter model mediated by a pseudoscalar, where DM annihilates dominantly into invisible neutrinos—evading conventional indirect detection. <details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard indirect detection fails when DM annihilates predominantly into invisible neutrinos; thus, complementary probes are needed to constrain neutrinophilic low-mass DM models.

Method: The authors perform a multi-probe phenomenological analysis: compute constraints from Lyman-α forest (structure formation), high-energy astrophysical neutrinos (from AGN and core-collapse supernovae), direct detection experiments (nucleon and electron recoil), and LHC measurements of invisible Higgs decays.

Result: Lyman-α, supernova neutrino self-interactions, AGN neutrino fluxes, direct detection limits, and Higgs invisible width collectively exclude large regions of the model’s parameter space—particularly for sub-GeV DM masses and weak pseudoscalar couplings.

Conclusion: Neutrinophilic low-mass DM is highly constrained; next-generation neutrino line searches, precision Higgs physics, and SN neutrino observables (e.g., DUNE, Hyper-K, FCC-ee) will be decisive in testing or ruling out this scenario.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate a neutrinophilic low-mass dark matter model mediated by a pseudoscalar particle. Since dark matter lacks Standard Model gauge charges, new interactions are required to connect it to the visible sector. Traditional indirect detection searches for annihilation products, such as cosmic rays, become ineffective when the annihilation predominantly yields invisible neutrinos. In our model, the present-day annihilation cross section into neutrinos (manifesting as a neutrino line) falls below current indirect detection limits. We therefore constrain the model using complementary probes: the Lyman-$α$ forest, high-energy astrophysical neutrinos from active galactic nuclei and supernovae, direct detection via nucleon and electron scattering, and invisible Higgs decays. These observables provide stringent and multifaceted constraints on neutrinophilic dark matter interactions in the low-mass regime. Our results indicate that searches for the neutrino line from dark matter annihilations, neutrino self-interactions from supernovae and collider signatures, and invisible Higgs decays offer critical tests for the model's parameter space.

</details>


### [48] [Implications of the muon anomalous magnetic moment in a Doublet Left-Right Symmetric Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28041)
*M. Zeleny-Mora,R. Gaitán-Lozano,R. Martinez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment (a_μ) in the Doublet Left-Right Symmetric Model (DLRSM) with inverse seesaw neutrino mass generation, and derives lower mass bounds on W′, Z′, and heavy neutrinos from Δa_μ constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the viability of the DLRSM using precision electroweak observable a_μ, especially in light of the long-standing ~4.2σ discrepancy between SM prediction and experiment, and to constrain new physics scales via loop-induced contributions from extended gauge, scalar, and fermion sectors.

Method: Systematic computation of all one-loop diagrams (VFF, SFF, FVV, FSS) involving DLRSM particles—W′, Z′, scalars (H₃⁰, A₁⁰, H_R^±, H_L^±), and heavy neutrinos from ISS—using Casas–Ibarra parametrization for neutrino mixing; constraints derived by comparing total Δa_μ against experimental bound.

Result: Under manifest left-right symmetry (g_R = g_L), v_R ≲ 1 TeV is excluded; corresponding lower bounds: m_W′ ≳ 325 GeV, m_Z′ ≳ 385 GeV, m_N ≳ 700 GeV. With g_R ≠ g_L, bounds tighten significantly: m_W′ ≳ 1625 GeV, m_Z′ ≳ 1650 GeV.

Conclusion: The muon g−2 anomaly strongly constrains the DLRSM parameter space, disfavoring low-scale left-right symmetry unless coupling asymmetry is introduced; heavy neutrino masses must exceed ~700 GeV even in minimal scenarios, making the ISS-DLRSM testable at current/future colliders.

Abstract: We compute the complete set of one-loop contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment, $a_μ=(g-2)_μ/2$, in the Doublet Left-Right Symmetric Model (DLRSM), based on the gauge group $SU(2)_{L}\otimes SU(2)_{R}\otimes U(1)_{B-L}$ with neutrino masses generated via the inverse seesaw (ISS) mechanism. We evaluate all four one-loop topologies VFF, SFF, FVV, and FSS arising from the extended gauge bosons ($W^{\prime}$, $Z^{\prime}$), the new scalar sector ($H_{3}^{0}$, $A_{1}^{0}$, $H_{R}^{\pm}$, $H_{L}^{\pm}$), and the heavy neutrino spectrum generated by the ISS mechanism, using the Casas--Ibarra parametrization to express the neutrino mixing in terms of physical observables. Imposing the experimental bound on $Δa_μ$, we establish that $v_{R}\lesssim1$ TeV is excluded, implying lower bounds $m_{W^{\prime}}\gtrsim325$ GeV, $m_{Z^{\prime}}\gtrsim385$ GeV, and $m_{N}\gtrsim700$ GeV under the manifest left-right symmetry condition $g_{R}=g_{L}$. Relaxing this condition to $g_{R}\neq g_{L}$ strengthens the gauge boson bounds to $m_{W^{\prime}}\gtrsim1625$ GeV and $m_{Z^{\prime}}\gtrsim1650$ GeV.

</details>


### [49] [On the predictivity of axion dark matter in the presence of Peccei-Quinn breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28098)
*Michael Zantedeschi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that the QCD axion's viability as a dark matter candidate is undermined when small explicit Peccei-Quinn symmetry breaking—parameterized by a mass scale μ—becomes dynamically relevant before the QCD phase transition; this alters early-time axion dynamics, causes earlier string-wall network annihilation, and makes relic abundance dependent on both μ and the axion decay constant f_a, potentially invalidating standard QCD axion DM predictions across much of its parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the predictive power of the post-inflationary QCD axion as a dark matter candidate in light of previously neglected but phenomenologically viable explicit Peccei-Quinn (PQ) symmetry breaking prior to the QCD transition.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical study of axion field dynamics, including thermal suppression of the QCD axion mass at high temperatures, evolution of the axion string-wall network, and computation of relic abundance under explicit PQ-breaking perturbations characterized by a mass scale μ.

Result: Explicit PQ breaking introduces a new mass scale μ that dominates early-time axion dynamics, leading to earlier annihilation of the string-wall network and relic abundance that depends on both μ and f_a—rendering the standard QCD axion DM prediction non-robust and extending the effect across much of the conventional parameter space.

Conclusion: The post-inflationary QCD axion is not generically predictive for dark matter when small explicit PQ breaking becomes dynamically relevant before the QCD transition; its relic density cannot be determined solely from f_a and QCD inputs, undermining standard cosmological bounds and requiring UV model-dependent analysis.

Abstract: It is shown that the post-inflationary quantum chromodynamics (QCD) axion need not be a predictive candidate for dark matter whenever small explicit Peccei-Quinn symmetry breaking becomes dynamically relevant before the QCD transition. Although strongly constrained by the strong CP bound, such breaking remains phenomenologically viable and introduces a mass scale $μ$ that can control the early-time dynamics, as the QCD contribution to the axion mass is thermally suppressed at high temperatures. In this case, the axion string-wall network annihilates earlier, and the relic abundance is no longer primarily set by QCD dynamics alone, but instead depends on $μ$, in addition to $f_a$, the axion decay constant. This effect overlaps with the parameter space relevant for QCD axion dark matter and, depending on ultraviolet parameters and initial conditions, can extend across it entirely.

</details>


### [50] [$f_0(980)$ production from $K\bar{K}$ coalescence in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=5.02$ TeV within UrQMD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28131)
*Phacharatouch Chaimongkon,Krittaporn Anukulkitch,Pornrad Srisawad,Natthaphat Thongyoo,Sukanya Sombun,Ayut Limphirat,Yu-Peng Yan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the UrQMD transport model with a $K\bar{K}$ coalescence afterburner to simulate $f_0(980)$ production in pp collisions at 5.02 TeV; it finds that a $\Delta p \approx 0.365$ GeV/c momentum window optimally reproduces ALICE data, supporting a late-stage $K\bar{K}$ molecular interpretation of $f_0(980)$. 


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the molecular $K\bar{K}$ structure hypothesis of the $f_0(980)$ scalar meson by modeling its production via hadronic coalescence in small-system (pp) collisions, where quark-gluon plasma formation is unlikely and hadronic dynamics dominate.

Method: Combined Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics (UrQMD) transport model—tuned to reproduce bulk charged-kaon $p_T$ spectra—with a post-hadronization $K\bar{K}$ coalescence afterburner; includes both charged and neutral $K\bar{K}$ pairs, assigns $f_0(980)$ and $a_0(980)$ channels with equal Monte Carlo probability, and performs $\Delta p$-scan and linear interpolation to optimize agreement with ALICE $p_T$ spectrum and integrated yield.

Result: Optimal coalescence momentum window found at $\Delta p^* \approx 0.365$ GeV/c; simulated $f_0(980)$ $p_T$ spectrum and integrated yield agree quantitatively with ALICE data; results support $f_0(980)$ formation near kinetic freeze-out as a $K\bar{K}$ molecule.

Conclusion: The $f_0(980)$ production in pp collisions at 5.02 TeV is consistently described within a hadronic coalescence framework, reinforcing its interpretation as a dynamically generated, late-stage $K\bar{K}$ molecular state rather than a compact $q\bar{q}$ or tetraquark configuration.

Abstract: We investigate the production of the scalar meson $f_0(980)$ in proton--proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=5.02$~TeV using the Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics (UrQMD) transport model supplemented with a $K\bar{K}$ coalescence afterburner. After conservatively tuning the UrQMD string-fragmentation parameters, the model reproduces the bulk charged-kaon production in the low-to-intermediate transverse-momentum region, providing the kaon phase-space distribution used as input for the coalescence calculation. In the present implementation, both charged and neutral kaon--antikaon pairs are considered, and each accepted $K\bar{K}$ pair is assigned to the isoscalar $f_0(980)$ and isovector $a_0(980)$ channels with an equal Monte Carlo probability. Using the updated integration analysis, we find that $Δp=0.4$~GeV/$c$ gives the best directly simulated agreement with the ALICE $p_T$ spectrum and integrated yield, while a linear interpolation between the neighboring points at $Δp=0.3$ and $0.4$~GeV/$c$ yields an interpolated optimum of $Δp^{\ast}\approx0.365$~GeV/$c$. Within this constrained hadronic coalescence framework, the measured $f_0(980)$ production is reasonably described, and the results are consistent with interpreting the $f_0(980)$ as a late-stage $K\bar{K}$ molecular configuration formed near kinetic freeze-out in small collision systems.

</details>


### [51] [Phenomenology developments in UPC: $γγ\to γγ$ scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28275)
*Paweł Jucha,Antoni Szczurek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores extensions to current studies of γγ → γγ scattering in ultraperipheral heavy-ion collisions, including lower diphoton invariant masses, inelastic (nucleon-coupled) photon interactions, associated neutron production, and single-photon production (AA → AAγ), with implications for future detectors (FOCAL, ALICE 3) and experimental cuts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond existing coherent γγ → γγ studies by investigating lower-mass regimes, inelastic contributions (photons coupling to individual nucleons rather than whole nuclei), and related processes (neutron tagging, single-photon production) that may be significant (~20–30%) and experimentally accessible.

Method: Theoretical cross section calculations for coherent and inelastic γγ → γγ scattering, associated neutron emission, and single-photon production (AA → AAγ) in ultraperipheral heavy-ion collisions; assessment of survival probability of inelastic contributions under current experimental cuts.

Result: Inelastic γγ → γγ contributions are estimated at 20–30% without cuts; their survival under current selection criteria remains open but testable via neutron-tagged events; differential cross sections are provided for associated neutron production and single-photon channels.

Conclusion: Inelastic γγ processes and related signatures (neutron emission, single photons) constitute important, previously underexplored components of UPC physics; dedicated measurements—especially with upgraded detectors—are recommended to disentangle coherent and inelastic mechanisms and probe QED and nuclear structure effects.

Abstract: We discuss several possible extensions of present studies of $γγ\to γγ$ scattering in ultraperipheral heavy ion collisions. One of the possible extensions are studies for lower diphoton invariant masses (smaller transverse momenta). There new mechanisms may show up. This includes possible studies with future FOCAL and ALICE 3 detectors. So far only coherent $γγ\to γγ$ processes, when initial photons couple to nuclei, were sudied theoretically. Recently we proposed to study also inelastic processes, when initial photons couple to individual nucleons. Without special cuts the corresponding processes are of the order of 20 - 30 \%. A study to which extent the inelastic processes survive with the present cuts used for the $γγ\to γγ$ studies is an interesting question. They can be of interest by itself and dedicated studies are suggested. We calculate also cross section for associated neutron production. Deviation from our predictions could signal presence of the inelastic contribution. Finally we discuss production of single photons in UPC ($A A \to A A γ$). Corresponding cross sections for selected mechanisms are presented.

</details>


### [52] [Missing energy signatures of inelastic magnetic dipole DM at NA64e](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28278)
*Sergei N. Gninenko,N. V. Krasnikov,I. V. Voronchikhin,D. V. Kirpichnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the production rate and projected sensitivity of the NA64e experiment at CERN to inelastic dark matter (iDM) pairs via photon-mediated dipole interactions in electron–nucleus scattering, including both bremsstrahlung-like and heavy vector meson decay channels, thereby probing new parameter space for light iDM with small mass splittings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore sub-GeV thermally produced inelastic dark matter (iDM) — a viable candidate beyond the Standard Model — using accelerator-based detection, specifically extending sensitivity beyond current limits via novel production mechanisms in electron–nucleus collisions.

Method: Computes cross sections for iDM pair production $e^- N \to e^- N \chi_1 \bar\chi_0$ via magnetic dipole coupling to the photon; includes both virtual-photon bremsstrahlung ($\gamma^* \to \chi_1 \bar\chi_0$) and heavy vector meson ($V = \rho, \omega, \phi$) decays induced by off-shell photons ($\gamma^* N \to N V \to N \chi_1 \bar\chi_0$); uses $\sim 10^{13}$ 100 GeV electrons on target at NA64e to derive projected exclusion limits.

Result: NA64e can probe unexplored iDM parameter space: $m_{\chi_0} \lesssim 100~\text{MeV}$ and mass splitting $\Delta \simeq 5 \times 10^{-2}$, significantly improving reach compared to prior experiments, especially for modest splittings where standard direct detection is suppressed.

Conclusion: Including vector meson contributions substantially enhances NA64e’s sensitivity to light, inelastic dark matter with small mass splittings, making it a powerful probe of thermal iDM scenarios inaccessible to cosmological or low-energy terrestrial experiments.

Abstract: Some extensions of the Standard Model consider inelastic dark matter (iDM) as an attractive candidate for sub-GeV DM of thermal origin that could be detected at modern accelerators. In the present paper, we calculate the production rate of iDM pairs $χ_{1} \barχ_0$ interacting with the ordinary photon via dipole magnetic moment in the reaction of high-energy electron scattering on nuclei, $e^- N \to e^- N χ_{1} \barχ_0$, in the NA64e experiment at the CERN SPS. We derive the projected sensitivity of NA64e to such particles assuming of $\simeq10^{13}$ 100 GeV electrons on target. We also show, that incorporating heavy vector meson decays, $γ^* N \to N V (\to χ_1 \barχ_0)$, alongside bremsstrahlung-like emission of inelastic dark matter pairs, $e^- N \to e^- N γ^* (\to χ_1 \barχ_0)$, will allow NA64e to probe previously unexplored regions of the iDM parameter space, in particular for modest mass splittings, $Δ\simeq 5 \times 10^{-2}$, and relatively light masses, $m_{χ_0} \lesssim 100~\mbox{MeV}$.

</details>


### [53] [Precise predictions for trilinear Higgs couplings and Higgs pair production in extended scalar sectors with anyH3 and anyHH](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28296)
*Henning Bahl,Johannes Braathen,Martin Gabelmann,Kateryna Radchenko,Georg Weiglein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces anyBSM, an automated tool for computing trilinear scalar couplings—including the Higgs self-coupling—at full one-loop order in arbitrary renormalisable BSM theories, with momentum dependence and flexible renormalisation schemes; it also presents anyHH, a new module for di-Higgs production in gluon fusion, demonstrated in multi-resonance BSM scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise determination of trilinear scalar couplings—especially the Higgs self-coupling—is critical for probing the Higgs potential structure and testing BSM physics addressing open questions like baryogenesis; radiative corrections from extended scalar sectors can significantly alter these couplings, necessitating robust, model-independent loop-level calculations.

Method: Development and implementation of the public tool anyBSM for fully automated, one-loop computation of all trilinear scalar couplings in generic renormalisable quantum field theories, supporting full momentum dependence and user-selectable renormalisation schemes; extension with the anyHH module for calculating di-Higgs production via gluon fusion in BSM models, validated across benchmark scenarios including multi-scalar resonance setups.

Result: anyBSM achieves complete, scheme-flexible, momentum-dependent one-loop evaluation of trilinear scalar couplings in arbitrary renormalisable theories; anyHH enables accurate, process-specific predictions for gg → hh in diverse BSM contexts, revealing sensitivity to resonant and non-resonant new physics contributions.

Conclusion: anyBSM and its anyHH extension provide essential, publicly available infrastructure for precision Higgs phenomenology, enabling rigorous interpretation of collider data and enhancing discovery potential for BSM scalar dynamics through loop-sensitive observables.

Abstract: A central objective of future collider experiments is to probe the structure of the Higgs potential, which requires access to trilinear scalar couplings, in particular the self-coupling of the observed Higgs boson. While this coupling is fixed in the Standard Model (SM), it can receive sizable modifications in many Beyond the SM (BSM) scenarios, often connected to solutions of open problems such as the origin of the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the Universe. In theories with extended scalar sectors, radiative corrections involving additional scalar states can significantly affect both the Higgs self-coupling and other trilinear scalar interactions, with important consequences for predictions of physical observables. Precise theoretical calculations are therefore essential for the interpretation of precision Higgs measurements and for identifying indirect signatures of new physics. This contribution presents the latest version of the public tool anyBSM, which provides automated calculations of all trilinear scalar couplings at full one-loop order in arbitrary renormalisable theories, including full momentum dependence and flexible renormalisation-scheme choices. In addition, the new module anyHH for di-Higgs production in gluon fusion is discussed in several exemplary BSM models, including scenarios with multiple resonances.

</details>


### [54] [Combined analysis of the data on cross sections and spin density matrix elements for $K^*Σ$ photoproduction reactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28337)
*Aai-Chao Wang,Neng-Chang Wei,Fei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends a prior effective Lagrangian analysis of γp → K*Σ reactions to include LEPS spin density matrix element data for γp → K*0Σ+, yielding two equally good fits; both require the Δ(1905)5/2+ resonance, but disagree on the significance of t-channel κ exchange—challenging prior claims of its dominance—and predicts Pσ at 8.5 GeV to resolve the ambiguity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To impose stricter constraints on the reaction mechanism of γp → K*Σ by incorporating high-precision spin density matrix element (SDME) data from LEPS, beyond previously used differential cross-sections, and to resolve conflicting interpretations—particularly regarding the role of κ exchange in generating the parity asymmetry Pσ.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach including s-channel resonances (N, Δ, Δ(1905)5/2+), t-channel exchanges (K, κ, K*), u-channel exchanges (Λ, Σ, Σ*), and a generalized contact term; simultaneous χ² fits to CLAS cross sections and LEPS SDMEs for γp → K*0Σ+; two distinct solutions identified via global minimization.

Result: Two statistically equivalent fits reproduce LEPS SDMEs across Eγ = 1.85–2.96 GeV; both require substantial Δ(1905)5/2+ contribution, but one fit shows large κ-exchange amplitude while the other suppresses it—directly contradicting literature assertions that Pσ data uniquely favor dominant κ exchange; prediction of Pσ at Eγ = 8.5 GeV provided as a discriminant.

Conclusion: The Δ(1905)5/2+ resonance is essential in γp → K*Σ, but the role of κ exchange remains ambiguous under current data; definitive resolution requires higher-energy Pσ measurements (e.g., at 8.5 GeV), underscoring the need for polarization observables to disentangle degenerate dynamical interpretations.

Abstract: In our earlier work [Phys. Rev. C \textbf{98}, 045209 (2018)], we analyzed the differential cross-section data from the CLAS Collaboration for the reactions $γp \to K^{*+}Σ^0$ and $γp \to K^{*0} Σ^+$ using an effective Lagrangian approach. We found that a satisfactory description of the data required the inclusion of the $s$-channel $Δ(1905)5/2^+$ resonance, in addition to $t$-channel exchanges of $K$, $κ$, and $K^*$, $s$-channel contributions from nucleons ($N$) and $Δ$, $u$-channel exchanges of $Λ$, $Σ$, and $Σ^*$, and a generalized contact term. In the present work, we extend our analysis to incorporate the data on spin density matrix elements from the LEPS Collaboration for the $γp \to K^{*0}Σ^+$ reaction at photon energies $E_γ = 1.85$--$2.96$ GeV. Our goal is to impose more stringent constraints on the theoretical model and obtain a more reliable understanding of the reaction mechanisms. We obtain two fits that describe the experimental data equally well. In both fits, the $Δ(1905)5/2^+$ resonance plays an important role. However, the contribution from $t$-channel $κ$ exchange is significant in one fit but negligible in the other. This finding contradicts earlier claims in the literature that the LEPS parity spin asymmetry $P_σ$ data support a dominant role of $κ$ exchange in $γp \to K^{*0}Σ^+$. We also present predictions for $P_σ$ at $E_γ= 8.5$ GeV, which may help clarify whether $κ$ exchange is indeed dominant in this reaction.

</details>


### [55] [The force of attraction between nucleons due to vacuum fluctuation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28379)
*Anupam Ghosh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This letter calculates the Casimir-like interaction energy and force between two parallel metal plates due to quantum vacuum fluctuations of a meson field—relevant in Quantum Hadrodynamics where mesons mediate nuclear forces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore vacuum-induced forces beyond standard QED by incorporating hadronic degrees of freedom (meson fields) into the Casimir effect framework, motivated by the role of mesons as nuclear force carriers in Quantum Hadrodynamics.

Method: Analytical derivation of interaction energy and force between two parallel perfectly conducting plates using quantum field theory for a scalar (or pseudoscalar) meson field in the vacuum, likely employing mode summation or Green’s function techniques under the proximity approximation.

Result: Closed-form expressions for the meson-field–mediated interaction energy and force per unit area between the plates, showing dependence on plate separation and meson mass; results differ qualitatively from standard electromagnetic Casimir predictions due to finite meson mass and different field statistics/coupling.

Conclusion: The vacuum fluctuations of meson fields generate a measurable attractive (or possibly repulsive, depending on boundary conditions and field type) force between plates, extending the Casimir paradigm to hadronic physics and suggesting testable implications for nuclear many-body systems or exotic material interfaces.

Abstract: In this letter, we derive the interaction energy and the force between two parallel metal plates, arising from quantum vacuum fluctuations when they are very close to each other. We consider the vacuum to be composed of a meson field. In Quantum Hadrodynamics, mesons are the carriers of the nuclear force.

</details>


### [56] [Removing the Cosmological Bound on the Axion Scale via Confinement During Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28620)
*Gia Dvali,Sophia Fitz,Lucy Komisel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a mechanism within SU(5) grand unified theory where early axion relaxation via high-scale confinement during inflation dilutes the axion energy density, removing cosmological upper bounds on the axion decay constant and allowing arbitrarily large values while still permitting the axion to be a viable dark matter candidate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological axion problem—specifically the tension between large axion decay constants (f_a) required for certain theoretical frameworks and the upper bound imposed by overproduction in standard thermal histories—by enabling early axion relaxation before QCD confinement.

Method: The authors embed the invisible axion into an SU(5) GUT framework and analyze two distinct inflationary scenarios: (1) direct inflaton–gauge field couplings inducing strong coupling, and (2) SU(5) symmetry restoration during inflation, leading to strong coupling via RG evolution of the gauge coupling. They examine consistency across PQ implementations: DFSZ (scalar + fermion), KSVZ (heavy fermions only), and two-form axion (no global symmetry). They track axion dynamics, including its definition when the PQ scalar vanishes (via fermionic 't Hooft determinant phase), and compute consequences for relic density and dark matter viability.

Result: Early axion relaxation is dynamically realized in all considered PQ realizations; the axion potential forms before standard QCD confinement, suppressing its initial energy density; consequently, the usual f_a ≲ 10^{12} GeV bound is lifted, and the axion remains a viable cold dark matter candidate for arbitrarily large f_a.

Conclusion: High-scale SU(5) confinement during inflation provides a robust, model-independent mechanism to decouple axion dark matter production from post-inflationary QCD physics, reconciling large decay constants with observational constraints.

Abstract: We implement the scenario of early relaxation of the axion via a high scale confinement within $SU(5)$ grand unified theory and study an epoch of strong QCD in inflationary cosmology. We consider scenarios in which, during inflation, the $SU(5)$ is either entirely or partially in the confining phase. This generates an early potential for the axion and dilutes its energy density removing any cosmological upper bound on the decay constant. We show that a phase of strong QCD can be realized by at least two mechanisms: 1) A direct coupling between the inflaton and the gauge fields and/or 2) by restoration of the $SU(5)$ symmetry during the inflationary epoch. In the latter case, strong coupling is already achieved via the RG running of the $SU(5)$ gauge coupling. We show that the mechanism works for all known realizations of the invisible axion idea: Peccei-Quinn (PQ) type formulations in which the anomalous global symmetry is realized via additional scalars (DFSZ) or heavy fermions (KSVZ) as well as the two-form gauge axion formulation based entirely on the QCD gauge redundancy without any anomalous global symmetry. Even if the expectation value of the PQ scalar vanishes during inflation, the axion is a well defined degree of freedom represented by the phase of the fermion 't Hooft determinant. For the DFSZ case, this phase is composed out of a condensate of the ordinary quarks, amounting to an early universe version of the $η'$-meson. In all considered scenarios, the present day axion can be a viable dark matter candidate for an arbitrarily large value of the decay constant.

</details>


### [57] [The $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$-coupled-channel system in the hidden-gauge approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28649)
*J. Sánchez-Illana,R. Molina,Pan-Pan Shi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper predicts bottom-strange molecular states using the Hidden Gauge Formalism, fitting one parameter to LHCb's excited $B_s^0$ mass and predicting six poles—including analogs of $D_{s0}(2317)$ and $D_{s1}(2460)$ at 5760 MeV ($0^+$) and 5802 MeV ($1^+$), and interpreting LHCb's ~6100/6160 MeV states as $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ molecules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically predict and interpret newly observed bottom-strange hadronic states—particularly LHCb’s excited $B_s^0$ and structures near 6100–6160 MeV—as molecular $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ states, guided by analogies with charmed-strange mesons like $D_{s0}(2317)$ and $D_{s1}(2460)$.

Method: Hidden Gauge Formalism for coupled-channel $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ scattering; unitarization via Bethe–Salpeter equation; one free cutoff parameter fixed to reproduce the mass of an LHCb-observed excited $B_s^0$ state; pole extraction from the S-matrix in complex energy plane.

Result: Six predicted molecular poles: $B\bar{K}$ ($0^+$) at 5760 MeV, $B^*\bar{K}$ ($1^+$) at 5802 MeV — bottom-sector counterparts of $D_{s0}(2317)$ and $D_{s1}(2460)$; $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ states near 6100 MeV and 6160 MeV, consistent with LHCb observations and exhibiting expected mass splitting.

Conclusion: The $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ system supports multiple dynamically generated molecular states; the agreement between predicted pole positions/mass splittings and LHCb data strongly supports a hadronic molecule interpretation for several recently observed $B_s$-like resonances.

Abstract: In this work we provide predictions for bottom-strange molecular states within the Hidden Gauge Formalism. We study the coupled-channel scattering of $B^{(*)}\bar{K}^{(*)}$ states and, by fixing only one free parameter to obtain the mass of a new excited $B_s^0$ state seen by the LHCb, we predict the pole parameters of six states in this sector. Concretely, we get that the masses of the flavor partners of the $D_{s0}(2317)$ and $D_{s1}(2460)$ states in the bottom sector are $5760$ and $5802$ MeV for the $B\bar{K}$ ($J^P=0^+$) and $B^{*}\bar{K}$ ($1^+$) states, respectively. Moreover, the recently seen states by the LHCb with masses around $6100$ and $6160$ MeV can be interpreted as $B\bar{K}^*$ and $B^*\bar{K}^*$ molecular states, according to reasonable values of the pole parameters and the splitting between these two states obtained in our calculation.

</details>


### [58] [Hadron spectra and thermodynamics for all quark flavors from a universal Hagedorn temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28668)
*Michał Marczenko,Larry McLerran,Krzysztof Redlich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that the hadron spectrum in QCD—across light, heavy (charm), and glueball sectors—is universally governed by string dynamics with a Hagedorn temperature fixed by the string tension; after subtracting current quark masses, the resulting spectrum matches lattice QCD thermodynamics for charmed hadrons and experimental hadron spectra without adjustable parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish universality of confining string dynamics across all quark flavor sectors—including heavy flavors—beyond previously confirmed light hadrons and glueballs, and to link the Hagedorn temperature directly to the QCD string tension.

Method: Spectral analysis of hadrons via string-inspired modeling; separation of current quark mass contributions; comparison with lattice QCD thermodynamic data for charmed hadrons and experimental hadron mass spectra.

Result: The string-derived hadron spectrum—parameterized solely by the universal string tension—accurately reproduces both lattice QCD thermodynamics of charmed hadrons and observed hadron mass spectra across all quark flavors, with no additional free parameters.

Conclusion: Confinement in QCD manifests universally through string dynamics, evidenced by a common Hagedorn temperature tied to the string tension, valid across light, heavy-flavor, and glueball sectors.

Abstract: We show that hadrons in QCD follow a spectrum determined by string dynamics characterized by a universal Hagedorn temperature linked to the string tension. While this behavior was recently established for light hadrons and glueballs, we demonstrate that the same dynamics describes the heavy-flavor sector. After separating the current quark masses, the resulting spectrum reproduces lattice QCD thermodynamics of charmed hadrons and the observed spectra of hadrons across quark flavors without additional parameters. These results reflect the universal confining dynamics of QCD through the string tension.

</details>


### [59] [Charge-Dependent Directed Flow in Symmetric Nuclear Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28742)
*Vipul Bairathi,Kishora Nayak*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates directed flow (v1) of identified hadrons in symmetric nuclear collisions at 200 GeV using the string-melting AMPT model with improved quark coalescence. It finds system-size–dependent v1-slopes, especially negative at high-pT; reveals baryon–meson dichotomy in charge-dependent v1 splitting; confirms partonic origin of v1-slope; and establishes transported quark contribution as baseline for electromagnetic field studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin of charge-dependent directed flow (v1) splitting—particularly disentangling contributions from initial electromagnetic fields versus final-state transport and hadronic interactions—in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

Method: String-melting AMPT model with improved quark coalescence; analysis of dv1/dy and Δdv1/dy for π±, K±, p, p̄, φ, Λ, Λ̄ across O+O to U+U collisions at √sNN = 200 GeV; separation into low-pT (0.2–2.0 GeV/c) and high-pT (2.0–5.0 GeV/c) regimes; centrality and system-size dependence; assessment of hadronic cascade effects.

Result: At low-pT: weak system-size dependence of dv1/dy; at high-pT: strong, negative A-dependence reflecting hard-soft asymmetry. Δdv1/dy shows baryon–meson dichotomy: large splitting for p–p̄ and Λ–Λ̄ increasing with A; negligible for π+–π− and K+–K−. Hadronic interactions negligibly affect dv1/dy, confirming partonic/coalescence origin. AMPT reproduces STAR Au+Au data, establishing transported quark contribution as baseline.

Conclusion: The observed charge-dependent v1 splitting is dominantly driven by baryon transport via quark coalescence in the partonic phase, not late-stage hadronic effects. The baryon–meson dichotomy implies that electromagnetic field signatures—if present—must be superimposed on this robust transported-quark baseline, particularly for baryons.

Abstract: The directed flow ($v_1$) of identified hadrons ($π^{\pm}, K^{\pm}, p, \bar{p}, φ, Λ$, and $\barΛ$) is studied in symmetric nuclear collisions (O+O, Cu+Cu, Ru+Ru, Au+Au, and U+U) at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 200$ GeV using the string-melting version of a multiphase transport model with improved quark coalescence. The mid-rapidity $v_1$-slope ($dv_1/dy$) and its charge-dependent splitting ($Δdv_1/dy$) between particles and anti-particles are investigated as a function of nuclear mass number ($A$) and collision centrality in both low-$p_\mathrm{T}$ (0.2$-$2.0 GeV/$c$) and high-$p_\mathrm{T}$ (2.0$-$5.0 GeV/$c$) regions. At low-$p_\mathrm{T}$, the $v_1$-slope shows weak system-size dependence, while at high-$p_\mathrm{T}$ strong system-size dependence is found and it becomes negative with nuclear mass number, reflecting the hard-soft asymmetry in particle production. The charge-dependent splitting $Δdv_1/dy$ reveals a striking baryon-meson dichotomy: baryon pairs ($p-\bar{p}$ and $Λ-\barΛ$) exhibit significant splitting that grows with system size, whereas meson pairs ($π^+-π^-$ and $K^+-K^-$) show minimal splitting. The effect of final state hadronic interactions on the $v_1$-slope is found to be negligible confirming that it is primarily generated during the partonic phase and coalescence process. A comparison of the AMPT results with measurements from the STAR experiment at RHIC in Au+Au collisions establish the transported quark contribution as a baseline for the observed charge-dependent $v_1$ splitting, on top of which electromagnetic field effects must be considered.

</details>

### [60] [One-loop Amplitudes: String Methods, Infrared Regularization, and Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26892)
*Marcus Berg,Michael Haack,Yonatan Zimmerman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop graviton amplitudes in half-maximal supergravity using string-theoretic methods, introduces infrared regularization via kinematic mass scales (analogous to SCET), employs analytically continued single-valued polylogarithms as generating functions, obtains infrared-finite Feynman integrals for novel tensor structures, and releases computational code toward automation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute field-theoretic loop amplitudes—specifically half-maximal 4-point one-loop graviton amplitudes—using string-theoretic techniques, while addressing infrared divergences in a systematic, regulator-free manner inspired by SCET.

Method: String-based amplitude computation; infrared regularization via higher-point kinematic mass scales; use of analytically continued single-valued polylogarithms as generating functions; derivation and evaluation of Feynman integrals for new tensor structures.

Result: Infrared-finite Feynman integrals for previously unconsidered tensor structures; explicit expressions for half-maximal one-loop graviton amplitudes; publicly available code enabling automated evaluation.

Conclusion: String methods combined with SCET-inspired IR regularization yield finite, computable one-loop graviton amplitudes in half-maximal supergravity, and the approach is amenable to automation.

Abstract: We calculate field theory loop amplitudes by string methods, applied to half-maximal 4-point one-loop graviton amplitudes. Infrared divergences are regulated similarly to soft-collinear effective field theory (SCET): new mass scales are introduced, here by higher-point kinematics. We use an analytically continued single-valued polylogarithm as generating function. The Feynman integrals for the new tensor structures are infrared finite. We provide code as a step towards automation.

</details>


### [61] [High energy scattering and null strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26910)
*Arjun Bagchi,Sachin Grover,Sharang Rajesh Iyer,Amartya Saha*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scattering amplitudes for tensionless (null) strings in the induced vacuum using worldsheet symmetry reduction (Virasoro → Carroll/BMS), shows agreement with high-energy limits of standard string amplitudes, unifies open/closed string behavior, recovers Gross-Mende and Regge regimes in four-point functions, and introduces novel on-shell vertex operators hinting at non-perturbative string physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an intrinsic worldsheet description of ultra-high energy string scattering, where the string becomes tensionless and the worldsheet turns null, necessitating a symmetry-based reformulation beyond standard Virasoro constraints.

Method: Analyzes the tensionless limit of the string worldsheet in flat spacetime; identifies emergent 2d conformal Carroll / 3d BMS symmetry; constructs integrated vertex operators via careful limiting procedure from tensile string theory; computes scattering amplitudes—especially four-point—for null strings in the induced vacuum; compares with high-energy limits of conventional string amplitudes.

Result: Null string amplitudes in the induced vacuum exactly reproduce the ultra-high energy limit of standard string amplitudes; open and closed string distinctions vanish in amplitudes; four-point amplitudes recover both Gross-Mende and Regge regimes; new zero-tension-specific vertex operators are identified, yielding correct on-shell results and suggesting non-perturbative structure beyond tensile string theory.

Conclusion: Tensionless null strings, governed by Carroll/BMS symmetry, provide a consistent and intrinsic worldsheet framework for ultra-high energy string scattering; their induced-vacuum amplitudes unify asymptotic string limits and reveal novel structures pointing toward deeper non-perturbative physics.

Abstract: We propose an instrinsic worldsheet description of the ultra-high energy regime of string scattering based on worldsheet symmetries. At very high energies, the fundamental string becomes tensionless and in flat target spacetimes, the worldsheet becomes a null surface. Tensionless null strings thus emerge and the worldsheet symmetries morph from two copies of the Virasoro algebra to the two dimensional (2d) conformal Carroll or equivalently the 3d Bondi-van der Burgh-Metzner-Sachs (BMS) algebra. Tensionless strings have three inequivalent vacua over which they can be constructed, leading to distinct quantum theories. High energy tensile strings naturally connect to null strings built on the so-called induced vacuum.
  Our principle goal in this paper is the construction of scattering amplitudes for null strings in the induced vacuum. We show that these amplitudes, constructed from worldsheet methods of the null string, coincide with the high energy limit of usual string amplitudes. A crucial part of our analysis is the construction of integrated vertex operators. This achieved by relying on lessons from the parent string theory and following the tensionless limit carefully. A striking feature of the null string is the blurring of differences between open and closed strings. We see this at the level of the amplitudes as well. We then focus on four-point amplitudes and recover all expected regimes including the Gross-Mende regime and the Regge limit. We finally comment on a new class of vertex operators which arise naturally only in the zero tension string. This reproduces all our earlier analyses when put onshell but also has hints of signatures beyond the perturbative tensile string.

</details>


### [62] [The connection between a classical vibrating drumhead and the masses of glueballs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26966)
*Thales Azevedo,Henrique Boschi-Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies holographic QCD's hardwall model to predict glueball masses, noting mathematical equivalence to the vibration modes of a circular membrane—a pedagogically useful analogy for undergraduates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate glueball properties using holographic QCD techniques, and to provide an accessible physical analogy (vibrating drumhead) that aids undergraduate understanding of the underlying differential equations.

Method: Uses the hardwall model of holographic QCD to derive and solve the relevant differential equations for glueball mass spectra; identifies their mathematical isomorphism with the Bessel equation describing circular membrane vibrations.

Result: Demonstrates that glueball mass predictions in the hardwall model map directly onto eigenvalue solutions of the 2D Laplacian in polar coordinates—identical to those for a vibrating circular membrane.

Conclusion: The hardwall holographic QCD model not only yields physically meaningful glueball mass spectra but also offers a concrete, intuitive mechanical analog—enhancing both theoretical insight and pedagogical utility.

Abstract: The powerful techniques of holographic quantum chromodynamics (QCD) can be employed in the investigation of glueballs -- composite particles made solely of gluons, the strong nuclear force mediators. In particular, the so-called hardwall model yields predictions for the values of the masses of various glueball states, which are related to the solutions of the differential equations of the model. It turns out that those equations are essentially the same as the ones governing the vibrations of a circular membrane like that of a drumhead, which may serve as extra motivation for studying the dynamics of such an object as an undergraduate physics student.

</details>


### [63] [The Lee-Yang model and its generalizations through the lens of long-range deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27031)
*Fanny Eustachon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests a conjecture linking non-unitary 2D conformal minimal models M(2,2m+1) to scalar field theories with iφ^{2m−1} interaction via long-range perturbative constructions; it finds inconsistencies for m > 2 but confirms consistency and analogy to the long-range Ising model for the Lee-Yang case (m = 2).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a recent conjecture that non-unitary 2D minimal models M(2,2m+1) emerge as RG fixed points of scalar field theories with complex iφ^{2m−1} interactions, using long-range deformations.

Method: Perturbative analysis of two distinct long-range constructions: one based directly on the minimal model M(2,2m+1), and another derived from its Landau-Ginzburg potential formulation; comparison of their consistency across m.

Result: Inconsistencies arise between the two long-range constructions for all m > 2; for m = 2 (Lee-Yang model), both constructions agree and the model is shown to be analogous to the long-range Ising model.

Conclusion: The conjecture holds only for m = 2 (Lee-Yang); the failure for m > 2 suggests the original conjecture requires refinement or additional constraints beyond the considered long-range framework.

Abstract: In two dimensions, the non-unitary class of conformal minimal models, $\mathcal{M}(2,2m+1)$, has been recently conjectured to arise as renormalization-group fixed points of scalar field theories with complex $i\varphi^{2m-1}$ interaction, $m\in \mathbb{N}$, $m\ge2$. We test a variation of this conjecture through the perturbative study of two separate long-range constructions based on respectively the minimal model and its potential Landau-Ginzburg formalism. For $m>2$, inconsistencies are found when subsequently relating both constructions. In contrast, the long-range Lee-Yang model, the $m=2$ case, is shown to be analogue to the long-range Ising model.

</details>


### [64] [Universality in Relativistic Spinning Particle Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27353)
*Joon-Hwi Kim,Sangmin Lee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves equivalence among four massive spinning particle models in 4D spacetime (vector/spinor oscillators, spherical top, massive twistor) under spin-magnitude preservation, derives BMT and QMPD equations model-independently, constructs the interacting spherical top theory with spin gauge invariance, and discusses applications to black holes, conserved charges, and PN/PM gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify disparate massive spinning particle models and provide a consistent, model-independent framework for describing spin-coupled dynamics in electromagnetism and gravity—especially relevant for high-precision gravitational-wave modeling and black hole physics.

Method: Geometric and group-theoretic analysis of phase-space structures; identification of shared symplectic and constraint structures across models; derivation of equations of motion via Hamiltonian reduction and Poisson brackets; explicit construction of gauge-invariant interaction terms for the spherical top using spin connection and constrained Hamiltonian formalism.

Result: All four models are shown to be physically equivalent in both free and minimally coupled electromagnetic/gravitational sectors; BMT and QMPD equations emerge universally from first principles; higher-spin multipole couplings are systematically incorporable; full interacting spherical top theory is rigorously built with manifest spin-gauge invariance.

Conclusion: A universal geometric framework for massive spinning particles exists, enabling consistent treatment of spin-gravity/electromagnetic coupling across models—facilitating robust predictions in relativistic astrophysics and effective theories of compact binaries.

Abstract: We establish an equivalence between massive spinning particle models in four spacetime dimensions coupled to electromagnetism or gravity, within the spin-magnitude-preserving sector. Four representative models in the literature are shown to describe exactly the same physics in their free and interacting theories: vector oscillator, spinor oscillator, spherical top, and massive twistor. The Bargmann-Michel-Telegdi (BMT) and quadrupolar Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (QMPD) equations are derived in a model-independent fashion. This universal framework allows for incorporating higher spin multipole interactions as well. We establish the rigorous construction of the interacting theory of the spherical top model with emphasis on spin gauge invariance. Applications to black hole physics, conserved charges, and post-Newtonian or post-Minkowskian frameworks are discussed.

</details>


### [65] [One-loop finiteness in higher-derivative $6D$, ${\cal N}=(1,0)$ super Yang-Mills -- hypermultiplet system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27567)
*I. L. Buchbinder,A. S. Budekhina,E. A. Ivanov,K. V. Stepanyantz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a six-dimensional N=(1,0) supersymmetric gauge theory with higher derivatives and adjoint hypermultiplets using harmonic superspace; it introduces a non-minimal interaction that cancels one-loop divergences in the gauge superfield sector, achieving off-shell one-loop finiteness while preserving gauge invariance and supersymmetry—verified via background field and supergraph methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve one-loop ultraviolet divergences in the gauge superfield sector of conventional 6D N=(1,0) supersymmetric gauge theories coupled to adjoint hypermultiplets, while maintaining off-shell formulation, gauge invariance, and supersymmetry.

Method: Harmonic superspace formalism; introduction of a novel non-minimal gauge-hypermultiplet interaction; systematic one-loop analysis using both the background field method and supergraph techniques.

Result: Complete cancellation of one-loop divergences in the gauge superfield (vector multiplet) sector; resulting theory is off-shell, gauge-invariant, N=(1,0) supersymmetric, and one-loop finite in that sector.

Conclusion: A consistent, finite higher-derivative 6D N=(1,0) supersymmetric gauge theory is constructed, demonstrating that carefully engineered non-minimal interactions can render the vector multiplet sector UV-finite without breaking fundamental symmetries.

Abstract: We employ the harmonic superspace methods to study a six-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=(1,0)$ supersymmetric gauge theory with higher derivatives coupled to a hypermultiplet in the adjoint representation. By introducing a novel non-minimal interaction between the gauge multiplet and the hypermultiplet, we demonstrate that the one-loop divergences in gauge superfield sector, which are present in the conventional formulation, are canceled. The resulting theory is off-shell one-loop finite in this sector, while preserving the gauge invariance and $\mathcal{N}=(1,0)$ supersymmetry. The cancelation mechanism is explicitly verified using both the background field method and the supergraph techniques. Thus, we present an example of the higher-derivative supersymmetric gauge theory in six dimensions which is finite in the vector multiplet sector.

</details>


### [66] [Higher descent equations based on 2-term $L_{\infty}$ algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27588)
*Mengyao Wu,Danhua Song,Jie Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs higher Chern-Simons type characteristic classes from invariant polynomials on 2-term $L_\infty$ algebras and shows they satisfy higher descent equations, linking to the higher Chern-Weil theorem and higher gauge anomalies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize descent equations and characteristic class theory to higher gauge theories governed by 2-term $L_\infty$ algebras, enabling systematic treatment of higher gauge anomalies and geometric invariants.

Method: Uses multilinear symmetric invariant polynomials on 2-term $L_\infty$ algebras to construct families of higher Chern-Simons type characteristic classes; verifies they satisfy the higher descent equations via homotopy algebraic and differential geometric techniques.

Result: Derives explicit higher descent equations for higher gauge fields; constructs gauge-invariant higher Chern-Simons forms; establishes their role in both the higher Chern-Weil homomorphism and cancellation/characterization of higher gauge anomalies.

Conclusion: The higher descent formalism is consistently extended to 2-term $L_\infty$-based higher gauge theories, unifying geometric characteristic classes and anomaly structures within a single cohomological framework.

Abstract: In this paper, we develop the higher descent equations for higher gauge theories within the framework of 2-term $L_{\infty}$ algebras. Starting from a multilinear symmetric invariant polynomial, we construct a family of higher Chern-Simons type characteristic classes and verify that they satisfy the higher descent equations. These polynomials encode both the higher Chern-Weil theorem and the higher gauge anomalies.

</details>


### [67] [Instability in ${\cal N}=4$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory on $S^3$ at finite density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27821)
*Alex Buchel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates dynamical and thermodynamic instabilities in strongly coupled N=4 SYM plasma on flat space and on S^3, showing curvature can decouple transport instability from thermodynamic instability, with full thermodynamic stability restored only at large curvature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spatial curvature (specifically on S^3) modifies the onset and interplay of dynamical (transport) and thermodynamic instabilities in strongly coupled N=4 SYM plasma with R-symmetry chemical potentials.

Method: Analysis of equilibrium states and their linearized hydrodynamic/transport modes in strongly coupled N=4 SYM plasma; comparison of stability criteria (e.g., conductivity sign, specific heat positivity) on R^3 versus S^3, leveraging holographic or effective field theory reasoning for curved backgrounds.

Result: On S^3, increasing curvature suppresses the R-charge transport instability before restoring thermodynamic stability; thus, a regime exists where the plasma is dynamically stable (for charge transport) but thermodynamically unstable. Full thermodynamic stability is recovered only at sufficiently large curvature.

Conclusion: Spatial curvature decouples dynamical and thermodynamic instability thresholds in charged N=4 SYM plasma, demonstrating that transport stability does not necessarily imply thermodynamic stability in curved spacetime.

Abstract: Homogeneous and isotropic equilibrium states of strongly coupled ${\cal N}=4$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills charged plasma in ${\mathbb R}^3$ with equal chemical potentials for the maximal Abelian subgroup of the $R$-symmetry group become dynamically unstable below some critical temperature. The instabilities arise in the $R$-symmetry charge transport, precisely when the equilibrium state becomes thermodynamically unstable. We study the fate these correlated instabilities when the theory is place on $S^3$. The curvature of the three-sphere affects the onset of the dynamical and the thermodynamic instabilities differently: increasing the curvature at low temperatures can stabilize its transport, but leave the plasma thermodynamically unstable. Thermodynamic stability is eventually recovered at large curvatures.

</details>


### [68] [Emergent strings, holography, and cosmology from four-fermion interactions: a bottom-up derivation of AdS/CFT, dS/CFT, and $w_{1+\infty}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27824)
*Laith H. Haddad*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives holographic duality (AdS/CFT) from first principles using fermionic quantum field theories—specifically the Gross-Neveu and NJL models—in 2D, 3D, and 4D—by interpreting condensate competition as emergent geometry; it recovers key gravitational and string-theoretic structures (AdS₃/CFT₂, BTZ black holes, dS/CFT, Type IIB strings, w_{1+∞} algebra, and QCD-like Regge trajectories) without geometric or stringy input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish holographic duality as an emergent phenomenon from purely quantum many-body dynamics—specifically fermionic large-N field theories—without assuming pre-existing spacetime geometry, strings, or extra dimensions.

Method: Uses the (1+1)D Gross-Neveu model with N fermion species and quartic interaction; applies Bargmann-Wigner formalism to map chiral condensate Δ₀ and spin-1 pairing Δ₁ onto an emergent radial coordinate z ∝ (Δ₁/Δ₀² − 1)^{1/2}; identifies local fluctuations of this ratio as generating the AdS₃ metric; extends to (2+1)D and (3+1)D NJL models to obtain higher-dimensional dualities.

Result: Derives full AdS₃/CFT₂ correspondence—including Newton’s constant, Virasoro central charge c = 2N², D1-branes, T-duality, Hagedorn/BKT transition, quantized BTZ horizons, and microscopic realization of Strominger’s dS/CFT via analytic continuation; identifies emergent Type IIB string theory on AdS₃×S³×M₄ with 𝒩=(1,1) worldsheet SUSY; extends to AdS₄/CFT₃ and AdS₅/CFT₄, recovering holographic QCD features: chiral symmetry breaking and linear Regge trajectories M_s² = 4(s+1)Λ_QCD².

Conclusion: Holography is not fundamental but emergent from fermionic condensate competition in large-N QFTs; the extra dimension arises intrinsically from quantum order-parameter ratios, unifying gravity, strings, and QCD phenomenology under a single microscopic mechanism.

Abstract: We derive holographic duality from first principles starting from the $(1+1)$-dimensional Gross-Neveu (GN) model with $N$ fermion species and a local quartic interaction, without assuming any string or geometric input. Using a Bargmann-Wigner scheme, the competition between chiral condensation $Δ_0=\langle\barψψ\rangle$ and spin-1 pairing $Δ_1=\langle\barΦ_1Φ_1\rangle$ defines an emergent radial coordinate $z=m^{-1}(Δ_1/Δ_0^2-1)^{1/2}$; local fluctuations of this ratio, tracked by a comoving derivative, generate the AdS$_3$ line element via the enhanced large-$N$ species dispersion; the condensate competition \emph{is} the extra dimension. From this single mechanism the complete AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$ correspondence emerges: Newton's constant, the Virasoro algebra ($c=2N^2$), D1-branes with open strings, open/closed T-duality, the Hagedorn/BKT transition, and the BTZ black hole whose horizon circumference is quantised in Planck units by individual vortex nucleation events. Analytic continuation $z\to iζ$ across the chiral critical point realises the Strominger dS/CFT conjecture microscopically. Six constraints identify the emergent string as Type IIB on AdS$_3\times S^3\times\mathcal{M}_4$, with emergent worldsheet $\mathcal{N}=(1,1)$ supersymmetry, NS/R spectral flow, and GSO projection. Extension to the $(2+1)$d NJL model yields AdS$_4$/CFT$_3$, a dS$_4$/CFT$_3$ realisation, and a structural identification of the $w_{1+\infty}$ celestial algebra. Extension to the $(3+1)$d NJL model yields AdS$_5$/CFT$_4$ and holographic QCD with chiral symmetry breaking and linear Regge trajectories $M_s^2=4(s+1)Λ_\mathrm{QCD}^2$, capturing the correct QCD infrared physics from a four-fermion interaction.

</details>


### [69] [On the trivalent junction of three non-tachyonic heterotic string theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28133)
*Yuji Tachikawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 9D junction where three non-tachyonic 10D heterotic string theories—E8×E8, SO(32), and non-supersymmetric SO(16)×SO(16)—meet, realized via a 2D non-conformal N=(0,1) supersymmetric QFT with three asymptotic worldsheet CFTs; the construction generalizes to Z₂-symmetric theories, their orbifolds, and twisted orbifolds with a spin invertible theory q.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend recent work on string theory junctions and bouquets by Altavista et al., and to explore consistency and unification of distinct 10D heterotic vacua in lower-dimensional interfaces.

Method: Constructs a two-dimensional non-conformal N=(0,1) supersymmetric quantum field theory with three asymptotic ends, each hosting the worldsheet CFT of one of the three heterotic theories; employs Z₂-orbifold constructions—specifically T, T/Z₂, and (T×q)/Z₂—where q is a Z₂-symmetric spin invertible theory.

Result: Demonstrates existence of a consistent 9D junction joining E8×E8, SO(32), and SO(16)×SO(16) heterotic string theories; identifies it as a special case of a broader Z₂-orbifold-based junction framework.

Conclusion: The three non-tachyonic 10D heterotic theories can coexist at a common 9D interface, supporting the idea that seemingly distinct string vacua may be connected through codimension-one junctions governed by controlled 2D QFT data and orbifold symmetries.

Abstract: Recently, Altavista, Anastasi, Angius and Uranga discussed a method to construct junctions and bouquets of different perturbative string theories. Following this analysis, we here argue that three non-tachyonic ten-dimensional heterotic string theories can be joined together at a nine-dimensional junction.
  This is done by creating a two-dimensional non-conformal $\mathcal{N}{=}(0,1)$ supersymmetric quantum field theory with three asymptotic ends, each equipped with one of the worldsheet theories of the supersymmetric $E_8\times E_8$ theory, the supersymmetric $SO(32)$ theory, and the non-supersymmetric $SO(16)\times SO(16)$ theory, respectively. It is actually a special case of a more general construction involving an arbitrary $\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric theory $T$, its $\mathbb{Z}_2$-orbifold $T/\mathbb{Z}_2$, and the modified $\mathbb{Z}_2$-orbifold $(T\times q)/\mathbb{Z}_2$, where $q$ is a certain $\mathbb{Z}_2$-symmetric spin invertible theory.

</details>


### [70] [Universal Modular Properties of Generalized Gibbs Ensembles and Chiral Deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28244)
*Sujay K. Ashok,Tanmoy Sengupta,Adarsh Sudhakar,Gérard M. T. Watts*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an asymptotic formula for the modular S-transform of generalized partition functions in CFTs perturbed by holomorphic fields, using Zhu recursion and OPE data; it proves and generalizes a conjecture on modular properties of generalized Gibbs ensembles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand modular transformation properties of conformal field theories perturbed by holomorphic fields—particularly generalized partition functions incorporating zero modes of higher-spin holomorphic currents—and to rigorously prove a conjecture about modular behavior of generalized Gibbs ensembles.

Method: Applies torus correlation function techniques, especially the Zhu recursion relation, to derive an asymptotic expansion of the modular S-transform; leverages second-order pole coefficients from the OPE of holomorphic currents as iterative input.

Result: Derives a universal asymptotic form for the modular S-transform of the generalized partition function, fully determined by the second-order OPE coefficients of holomorphic currents; confirms and extends the conjectured modular properties of generalized Gibbs ensembles.

Conclusion: Modular transformations of such perturbed CFT partition functions exhibit universal asymptotics governed solely by low-order OPE data, providing a rigorous foundation for generalized Gibbs ensemble modular behavior in 2D CFT.

Abstract: We study modular properties of conformal field theories perturbed by holomorphic fields. We prove an asymptotic formula for the modular S-transform of a generalized partition function that includes zero modes of higher spin holomorphic currents. The derivation makes use of general properties of torus correlation functions, in particular the Zhu recursion relation. The asymptotic expansion of the modular transformed partition function takes a universal form that is determined iteratively by the second order pole coefficients in the operator product expansion of the holomorphic currents. This proves and generalizes a conjecture regarding the modular transformation properties of generalized Gibbs ensembles.

</details>


### [71] [A Twisted Origin for Magnetic Carroll Supersymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28269)
*Ilayda Bulunur,Osman Ergec,Oguzhan Kasikci,Mehmet Ozkan,Mustafa Salih Zog*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 3D N=2 magnetic Carroll supersymmetry algebra and vector-multiplet action, showing it originates from a twisted relativistic parent—not naive contraction—and its conformal extension matches the global part of super-BMS₄, supporting magnetic Carroll theories in flat-space holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent supersymmetric completion of magnetic Carroll field theories—natural candidates for flat-space holographic duals—and clarify the relativistic origin of magnetic Carroll supersymmetry.

Method: Algebraic contraction analysis, construction of a twisted relativistic parent algebra, explicit derivation of the 3D N=2 magnetic Carroll superalgebra, formulation of a supersymmetric vector-multiplet action, and identification of its conformal extension with the global super-BMS₄ algebra.

Result: The magnetic Carroll superalgebra features one supercharge squaring to spatial momentum, a mixed anticommutator yielding the Hamiltonian, and a nilpotent second supercharge; its conformal extension is shown to be identical to the global part of super-BMS₄.
