Conclusion: Magnetic Carroll supersymmetry has a physical, twisted relativistic origin—not a naive contraction—and its alignment with super-BMS₄ reinforces its role in flat-space holography and supersymmetric asymptotic symmetry algebras.

Abstract: Magnetic Carrollian theories provide a natural setting for field theories with nontrivial spatial structure in the Carroll limit and are therefore natural candidates for flat-space holographic duals. Embedding such boundary theories into a top-down framework requires a consistent supersymmetric completion and, in particular, an understanding of the relativistic origin of magnetic Carroll supersymmetry. We show that the relevant magnetic Carroll algebra does not arise from a naive contraction of the standard relativistic supersymmetry algebra, but instead descends from a twisted relativistic parent. As an explicit realization, we construct a three-dimensional ${\mathcal{N}}=2$ magnetic Carroll algebra together with a supersymmetric vector-multiplet action. Unlike the electric case, the resulting structure contains one supercharge that squares to spatial momentum, a mixed anticommutator that yields the Hamiltonian, and a nilpotent second supercharge. We further show that its conformal extension coincides with the global part of a supersymmetric BMS$_4$ algebra. This provides a physical and relativistic origin for a super-BMS$_4$ structure recently identified by complementary algebraic methods, and strengthens the case for magnetic Carroll theories in flat-space holography and supersymmetric asymptotic symmetries.

</details>


### [72] [Oscillons in the broken vacuum and global vortex annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28298)
*D. Canillas Martínez,A. González-Parra,D. Miguélez-Caballero,A. Wereszczynski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a stable, long-lived oscillon in the complex φ⁶ theory during vortex-antivortex collisions—unlike the φ⁴ model—and attributes its persistence to a far-distance potential modification introducing an unbroken vacuum, despite absence of a mass gap from the flat direction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological defect dynamics (specifically vortex-antivortex collisions) differ between φ⁴ and higher-order scalar field theories, and to explain the emergence of unexpected long-lived structures in the absence of a conventional mass gap.

Method: Analytical and numerical investigation of vortex-antivortex scattering in the complex φ⁶ scalar field theory, focusing on the structure and stability of post-collision excitations, particularly oscillons, and their relation to vacuum structure and potential asymptotics.

Result: Discovery of a remarkably stable, large-amplitude oscillon in the broken vacuum of the φ⁶ theory; demonstration that its longevity arises not from a mass gap but from a far-distance modification of the potential enabling an unbroken (true or false) vacuum state.

Conclusion: The resonant scattering behavior and oscillon stability in φ⁶ theory are governed by global potential features—specifically the existence of an unbroken vacuum at large field amplitudes—rather than local spectral properties like a mass gap, revealing a new mechanism for metastable localized structures in scalar field theories.

Abstract: In contrast to the complex $φ^4$ model, vortex-antivortex collisions in the complex $φ^6$ theory reveal a resonant structure due to the existence of a remarkably stable, long-lived, large amplitude oscillon in the broken vacuum. Surprisingly, it persists despite the absence of a mass gap associated with the flat direction in the broken vacuum. We demonstrate that its existence is related to a far-distance modification of the potential, namely, the appearance of an unbroken (false or true) vacuum.

</details>


### [73] [Physics as Code: From Scans to Theorems with ITP APIs in $SU(5)$ Model Building](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28406)
*Sven Krippendorf,Joseph Tooby-Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces an API-based methodology in Lean (an interactive theorem prover) to formally verify classifications of bounded charge spectra in SU(5) F-theory models—replacing ad hoc scans with reusable, theorem-backed reduction theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of exhaustive and statistical model scans in theoretical physics by enabling globally reliable, formally verified classifications via interactive theorem proving.

Method: Formalizing charge spectra, physical predicates (e.g., top-Yukawa coupling, absence of dangerous operators), and reduction moves as reusable Lean definitions; deriving classification via proved reduction theorems—not enumeration.

Result: A formally verified classification: every viable bounded charge spectrum satisfying the constraints arises from finitely many minimal 'top-Yukawa witnesses' plus certified completions and closure steps.

Conclusion: The approach establishes correctness-first, reusable infrastructure (integrated into PhysLib) for combinatorially hard model-building problems, enabling certified inputs for downstream physics analyses.

Abstract: A recurring challenge in theoretical physics is to make reliable global statements about bounded but combinatorially large model spaces. Exhaustive scans quickly become opaque or impractical, while statistical exploration does not by itself provide theorem-backed guarantees. This motivates workflows in which the model-building problem itself is formalized inside an interactive theorem prover (ITP).
  In this paper we develop an API-based methodology for formalizing such bounded model-building questions inside Lean, an interactive theorem prover. The central step is to represent the relevant charge spectra, predicates, and reduction moves as reusable ITP definitions, and then to derive the classification from proved reduction theorems rather than from an ad hoc scan. We demonstrate the strategy in a concrete $SU(5)$ case study motivated by F-theory model building with additional Abelian symmetries.
  At the charge-spectrum layer, we classify bounded spectra that admit a top-quark Yukawa coupling, avoid a selected set of dangerous operators, and satisfy a minimal charge-spectrum completeness condition. Our main result shows that every such spectrum in the bounded search space arises from finitely many minimal top-Yukawa witnesses together with controlled completions and certified closure steps. This classification represents a formally verified description of the full viable class in the charge-spectrum setting studied here.
  The development is implemented inside PhysLib as reusable infrastructure rather than as a one-off verification script. It provides a proof of principle for how interactive theorem provers can turn combinatorially difficult model-building problems into correctness-first, reusable workflows, and we discuss how the resulting certified classification can serve as reliable input for downstream analyses.

</details>


### [74] [Lecture Notes on Positivity Properties of Scattering Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28454)
*Prashanth Raman*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews completely monotone (CM) and Stieltjes functions—classes defined by infinite positivity constraints—and explores their emerging role in quantum field theory, especially in Euclidean Feynman integrals and $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM amplitudes; it connects their mathematical structure to physical principles (unitarity, analyticity), parametric representations, and applications in S-matrix constraints, numerical bootstrap, and positive geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To elucidate the unexpected appearance and utility of completely monotone and Stieltjes functions—a classical analytic concept—in modern QFT contexts, particularly scattering amplitudes and positive geometry, thereby bridging rigorous analysis with physical intuition and computational methods.

Method: Review-based theoretical analysis combining classical real analysis (Hausdorff–Bernstein–Widder theorem, integral representations), QFT foundations (unitarity, analyticity, Feynman parametrization), and geometric insights (positive geometry, volume interpretations); includes synthesis of lecture material from a specialized workshop.

Result: Establishes that CM/Stieltjes properties are pervasive in Euclidean scalar Feynman integrals and Coulomb branch amplitudes; identifies unitarity and analytic structure as key physical origins; demonstrates implications for analytic S-matrix bounds, numerical bootstrap convergence, and geometric volume conjectures in positive geometry.

Conclusion: Completely monotone and Stieltjes functions provide a unifying analytic framework linking positivity, causality, and geometry in QFT; their systematic use enhances both theoretical understanding and computational strategies in amplitude physics and beyond.

Abstract: We review completely monotone (CM) and Stieltjes functions, which are classes of functions obeying an infinite hierarchy of positivity constraints. While these are classical concepts in analysis, such properties have recently been shown to arise in many fundamental building blocks and observables of quantum field theory (QFT), including scalar Feynman integrals in the Euclidean region and Coulomb branch amplitudes in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM.
  After reviewing their mathematical structure, we discuss the physical and geometric origins of these properties, ranging from unitarity and analyticity in scattering amplitudes to the structure of parametric representations of Feynman integrals. We then survey a number of applications, including constraints on the analytic S-matrix, implications for numerical bootstrap methods, and connections to positive geometry, where we present evidence for a close relation between these functions and geometric volume interpretations.
  These notes are based on an extended series of lectures delivered at the \emph{Positive Geometry in Scattering Amplitudes and Cosmological Correlators} workshop, held at the International Centre for Theoretical Sciences (ICTS), Bengaluru, in February 2025.

</details>


### [75] [Supersymmetry and Attractors in N = 4 Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28495)
*Abhinava Bhattacharjee,Bindusar Sahoo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically demonstrates the attractor mechanism for extremal, spherically symmetric black holes in pure N = 4 Poincaré supergravity and shows that generic dyonic solutions with $p^2q^2 > (p \cdot q)^2$ preserve exactly 1/4 of the supersymmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar moduli are fixed at the horizon of extremal black holes (the attractor mechanism) and to classify the residual supersymmetry preserved by such solutions in pure N = 4 supergravity.

Method: Numerical analysis of extremal, spherically symmetric black hole solutions; analytical study of Killing spinor equations restricted to constant moduli configurations.

Result: The attractor mechanism is confirmed numerically; constant-moduli extremal black holes with generic dyonic charges satisfying $p^2q^2 > (p \\cdot q)^2$ preserve precisely 1/4 of the N = 4 supersymmetries.

Conclusion: The attractor behavior and supersymmetry preservation are robust features of extremal black holes in pure N = 4 Poincaré supergravity, tightly constrained by charge geometry.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the attractor mechanism for extremal, spherically symmetric black holes in pure N = 4 Poincaré supergravity, which we demonstrate numerically. We further study the supersymmetries preserved by these solutions by focussing specifically on the constant moduli solutions and show that, for a generic dyonic charge configuration satisfying $p^2q^2>(p.q)^2$, they always preserve 1/4th of the total supersymmetries.

</details>


### [76] [Which Functions Admit a Positive Geometry? From Branch Cuts to String Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28543)
*Hyungrok Kim,Jonah Stalknecht*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends positive geometry to infinite unions of line segments and continuum limits, enabling canonical forms with branch cuts; introduces 'pseudogenus' to classify meromorphic functions, showing canonical forms are dlog of pseudogenus-zero functions; constrains physical spectra (e.g., string/KK towers) compatible with such geometries; and provides a geometric interpretation of the KLT double copy via dlog forms of open/closed string amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize positive geometry beyond rational canonical forms—specifically to accommodate non-rational functions (e.g., with branch cuts) arising in physical amplitudes like string theory—and to geometrically characterize constraints on allowed spectra from scattering amplitudes.

Method: Analyzes one-dimensional positive geometries as infinite unions of line segments and their continuum limits; introduces pseudogenus as a topological invariant for meromorphic functions on ℂℙ¹; derives necessary conditions for a function to be a canonical form via dlog representation; applies this to string and Kaluza–Klein spectra; constructs positive geometries for open and closed string amplitudes’ dlog forms.

Result: Canonical forms of one-dimensional positive geometries are precisely dlog f where f has pseudogenus zero; continuum limits yield canonical forms with branch cuts; only highly truncated stringy or KK spectra (with almost all states decoupled) are compatible with union-of-segments geometries; dlog forms of four-point open and closed string amplitudes admit positive geometric realizations, yielding a geometric derivation of the KLT relation.

Conclusion: The framework of positive geometry can be meaningfully extended to encompass non-rational amplitudes via continuum and infinite-segment limits; pseudogenus provides a sharp classification criterion; physical consistency imposes strong sparsity constraints on infinite towers; and the KLT double copy emerges naturally from shared dlog geometry.

Abstract: Positive geometry provides a geometric framework where physical observables are encoded as canonical forms associated to regions of kinematic space. In this paper we consider a generalisation to an infinite union of line segments, which allows us to capture canonical forms beyond rational functions. In the continuum limit of positive geometries, we show that we can generalise even further and describe positive geometries whose canonical forms contain branch cuts. We will constrain which functions can be obtained as the canonical form of one-dimensional positive geometries. We introduce the notion of the pseudogenus to classify meromorphic functions, and show that canonical forms can be written as the $\mathrm d\log$ of a function with pseudogenus zero. Furthermore, we argue that the spectrum encoded by a union of line segments is consistent with the presence of a stringy tower of states or a Kaluza-Klein tower with three or more compact directions only if nearly all such states do not contribute to the scattering amplitude. In addition, we show how the d log of both open and closed string amplitudes admits a positive geometry. This allows us to give a fully geometric interpretation for the KLT double copy at four points.

</details>


### [77] [Symmetry Resolved Entanglement Entropy: Equipartition under Driven and Non-unitary Evolution in a Compact Boson CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28567)
*Filiberto Ares,Jayashish Das,Arnab Kundu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates symmetry-resolved entanglement entropy dynamics in bulk-driven Floquet 2D CFTs, specifically the free compact boson. It identifies an sl^(k)(2,R) subalgebra of Virasoro that introduces a tunable parameter k controlling equipartition breakdown among charge sectors, attributed to low–high frequency mode coupling; the mechanism is conjectured to extend beyond free bosons. It also examines how non-unitary evolution (e.g., post-selected weak measurements) alters real-time symmetry-resolved entropy dynamics of boundary states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how time-periodic (Floquet) driving and bulk energy injection affect the fine-grained (symmetry-resolved) entanglement structure in conformal systems, particularly whether charge-sector entropies remain equally distributed (equipartition) and what symmetries or mechanisms govern its violation.

Method: Analytical CFT techniques applied to the 2D free compact boson under bulk-driven Floquet protocols; identification and exploitation of an sl^(k)(2,R) subalgebra within the Virasoro algebra; oscillator representation of Virasoro to argue universality; extension to non-unitary evolution via boundary state formalism modeling post-selected weak measurements.

Result: The sl^(k)(2,R) subalgebra introduces a continuous parameter k that continuously tunes the degree of equipartition breaking in symmetry-resolved Rényi entropies; this arises from explicit low–high frequency mode coupling; the mechanism is expected to be generic beyond free bosons; non-unitary evolution qualitatively modifies real-time symmetry-resolved entropy dynamics of boundary states.

Conclusion: Equipartition of symmetry-resolved entanglement is not robust under bulk Floquet driving in CFTs; its controlled breakdown is governed by an emergent sl^(k)(2,R) structure and inter-frequency coupling; such effects are likely universal in driven CFTs, and non-unitary processes further enrich the dynamical landscape of fine-grained entanglement.

Abstract: We study the evolution of symmetry-resolved entanglement entropy in bulk-driven Floquet conformal field theories (CFTs). Focusing on the two-dimensional free compact boson CFT, we analyze how symmetry-resolved Rényi entropies approach or depart from equipartition among charge sectors. We show that the existence of an $\mathfrak{sl}^{(k)}(2,\mathbb{R})$ subalgebra of the Virasoro algebra introduces a free parameter, the label $k$, which allows us to control the breakdown of equipartition. We argue that this effect originates from an explicit coupling between low- and high-frequency modes. Based on a general oscillator representation of the Virasoro algebra, we expect this mechanism to persist beyond the free boson CFT. Finally, we discuss how the real-time dynamics of fine-grained symmetry-resolved entropies of a boundary state are modified under non-unitary evolution, which can be associated with post-selected weak measurements.

</details>


### [78] [3D gravity and double copy theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28639)
*Maor Ben-Shahar,Francesco Bonechi,Maxim Zabzine*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper reformulates 3D gravity using divergenceless vector frames, linking it to Chern-Simons theory and the double copy, with on-shell equivalence to standard 3D gravity and connections to AdS₃.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically transparent reformulation of 3D gravity aligned with the double-copy structure observed in Chern-Simons theory, and to explore its higher-dimensional origins and holographic implications.

Method: Constructs a new formulation of 3D gravity based on divergenceless vector frames; establishes on-shell equivalence with standard 3D gravity; relates the formulation to a Chern-Simons-like action; proposes a higher-dimensional embedding; analyzes extensions yielding AdS₃ solutions.

Result: A consistent, on-shell equivalent reformulation of 3D gravity in terms of divergenceless vector fields is obtained; the theory admits a Chern-Simons-like description; plausible higher-dimensional uplifts are identified; and extensions reproducing AdS₃ geometry are demonstrated.

Conclusion: The vector-frame reformulation deepens the geometric understanding of the double copy in 3D gravity, bridges it with Chern-Simons theory, and opens avenues for exploring AdS₃/CFT₂ via higher-dimensional embeddings.

Abstract: We introduce a novel reformulation of three-dimensional gravity in terms of divergenceless vector frames, inspired by the double copy for Chern-Simons theory. This formulation is on-shell equivalent to conventional 3D gravity and provides a transparent geometric interpretation of the double-copy construction. We relate the resulting theory to a Chern-Simons-like action, propose a higher-dimensional origin, and explore extensions that give rise to $AdS_3$

</details>


### [79] [Scattering in strong field QED in a non-null background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28665)
*Patrick Copinger,James P. Edwards,Karthik Rajeev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives master formulae for N-photon scattering amplitudes in non-null electromagnetic backgrounds (A_μ(n·x) with n² ≠ 0) using the first-quantised worldline formalism, enabling systematic expansion in n² and partial resummation; validated against known low-order results and an exactly solvable crossed-field case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend beyond constant or null (plane-wave) background approximations and incorporate more realistic laser-plasma dispersive effects by treating non-null backgrounds — where n² ≠ 0 — non-perturbatively in QED.

Method: Employs the first-quantised worldline formalism to construct coordinate- and momentum-space propagators, LSZ-truncated amplitudes, and the effective action, all incorporating the non-null background exactly; expands systematically in the non-null parameter n² and performs partial resummation; validates via exact solutions and low-order comparisons.

Result: Derives general master formulae for tree-level and one-loop N-photon amplitudes in scalar and spinor QED for arbitrary non-null backgrounds A_μ(n·x); provides explicit checks against wavefunction renormalisation and non-linear Compton scattering at O(n²), and solves a special non-null constant crossed-field case without photon insertions.

Conclusion: The worldline-based master formulae provide a robust, non-perturbative framework for computing multi-photon processes in physically relevant non-null backgrounds, bridging the gap between idealised plane-wave models and dispersive laser-plasma environments.

Abstract: We examine scattering amplitudes for an arbitrary number of photons in a class of non-null background electromagnetic fields, studying tree-level and one-loop amplitudes in scalar and spinor quantum-electrodynamics in backgrounds defined by a gauge field $A_μ(\mathfrak{n}\cdot x)$ for $\mathfrak{n}^2\neq 0$. Motivated to account for more physically realistic laser-plasma dispersive properties, our approach overcomes prior work studying such amplitudes in a constant background field and relaxes the familiar null criterion assumed for plane waves.
  Master Formulae for the $N$-photon amplitudes dressed by the non-null background are constructed using the first-quantised worldline formalism, which can systematically account for all orders in the non-null parameter, $\mathfrak{n}^2$, treated here as an expansion parameter. These are derived from worldline representations of the coordinate and momentum space propagators (and their LSZ-truncated amplitudes) and the effective action, each incorporating the non-null background non-perturbatively. We then outline a partial resummation of their expansions in $\mathfrak{n}^{2}$. A special exactly solvable case of non-null constant crossed fields without photon insertion in the effective action is explored to test the Master Formulae that result. The validity of the presented master formulae is further checked against known expressions for the wavefunction and non-linear Compton scattering in a non-null background to lowest order in the non-null parameter.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [80] [Holography on the lattice: Evidence from 3D supersymmetric Yang--Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26985)
*Anosh Joseph,David Schaich*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the nonperturbative phase diagram of 3D maximally supersymmetric Yang–Mills theory using a supersymmetry-preserving lattice formulation, and finds that the spatial deconfinement transition temperature scales as $T_c \propto \alpha^3$, consistent with holographic predictions and supporting the gauge–gravity correspondence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the gauge–gravity correspondence nonperturbatively by studying the spatial deconfinement transition in 3D $\mathcal{N}=8$ SYM — a theory dual to homogeneous vs. localized black branes in AdS$_4$ — via controlled lattice simulations.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation with a supersymmetry-preserving discretization; calculations performed on $N_L^2 \times N_T$ lattices with $N=8$, $N_T = 8,10,12$, and aspect ratios $\alpha = N_L/N_T \leq 3$; extraction of deconfinement transition temperatures from observables sensitive to spatial center symmetry breaking.

Result: Measured transition temperatures $T_c$ scale as $T_c \propto \alpha^3$, matching the holographic large-$N$, low-temperature prediction; results are stable across $N_T$ and aspect ratios, confirming robustness.

Conclusion: The agreement between lattice results and holographic scaling provides strong nonperturbative evidence for the validity of the gauge–gravity duality in the 3D $\mathcal{N}=8$ SYM / AdS$_4$ black-brane correspondence.

Abstract: We present new results from our lattice investigations of maximally supersymmetric Yang--Mills theory in three dimensions, focusing on its nonperturbative phase diagram. Using a lattice formulation that preserves part of the supersymmetry algebra at finite lattice spacing, we study the spatial deconfinement transition, which holography relates to the transition between localized and homogeneous black branes in the dual gravity theory. Our analysis employs $N_L^2 \times N_T$ lattices with $N = 8$ colors in the SU($N$) gauge group, considering $N_T = 8$, $10$ and $12$, in each case with aspect ratios $α= N_L/N_T \leq 3$. The resulting transition temperatures are consistent with the holographic low-temperature, large-$N$ prediction $T_c \propto α^3$, providing further evidence for the gauge--gravity correspondence in this setting.

</details>


### [81] [Domain wall fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27608)
*Thomas Blum,Yigal Shamir*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces domain wall fermions in lattice QCD, proves exact chiral symmetry recovery in the infinite fifth-dimension limit, derives the corresponding Ginsparg-Wilson operator, analyzes residual chiral symmetry breaking for finite fifth dimension, links it to Wilson kernel spectral properties, and reviews improvements like Möbius fermions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate domain wall fermions rigorously in lattice QCD and address the challenge of preserving chiral symmetry on the lattice, which is essential for realistic simulations of light quarks and topological phenomena.

Method: Analytical derivation of the domain wall fermion formalism; proof of chiral symmetry restoration in the infinite fifth-dimension limit; spectral analysis of the Wilson kernel to quantify residual symmetry breaking; systematic discussion of fermion variants (e.g., Möbius) aimed at reducing computational cost and improving symmetry realization.

Result: Exact chiral symmetry (via Ginsparg-Wilson relation) is recovered in the infinite fifth-dimension limit; residual breaking is quantified and shown to depend on low-lying eigenvalues of the Wilson kernel; Möbius fermions are identified as an efficient improvement reducing the required extent of the fifth dimension while maintaining good chiral properties.

Conclusion: Domain wall fermions provide a robust framework for chiral symmetry on the lattice; their theoretical foundation is well-established, and practical variants like Möbius fermions enhance feasibility for large-scale simulations in modern lattice QCD.

Abstract: We introduce the formulation of domain wall fermions in the context of lattice QCD. We prove the recovery of exact chiral symmetry in the limit of an infinite fifth direction, and derive the effective four-dimensional operator satisfying the Ginsparg-Wilson relation obtained in this limit. We discuss the residual breaking of chiral symmetry for finite extent of the fifth direction, and how it is affected by spectral features of the Wilson kernel. We also discuss various improvements of domain wall fermions including notably Möbius fermions. These notes are a chapter contributed to the on-line book ``Lattice QCD at 50 years'' (LQCD@50).

</details>


### [82] [Mesonic screening correlators in an external imaginary electric field at finite temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27620)
*Ji-Chong Yang,Zhan Zhao,Xiang-Ning Li,Wen-Wen Li*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies mesonic screening correlators in lattice QCD under imaginary electric fields using staggered fermions, revealing temperature-dependent modifications: scalar screening masses increase at low T, pseudoscalar masses are stable, charge-asymmetric channels show structure, and spatial oscillations emerge at high T with frequencies tied to quark charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real electric fields in QCD suffer from the sign problem; imaginary electric fields serve as a controllable proxy to probe electromagnetic responses of QCD matter without this obstruction.

Method: Lattice QCD simulations with staggered fermions, computing mesonic screening correlators at finite temperature under background imaginary electric fields.

Result: At low T: scalar screening masses increase with field strength; pseudoscalar masses are insensitive; charge-asymmetric channels display nontrivial structure. At high T: screening correlators develop spatial oscillations whose frequencies are determined by quark electric charges.

Conclusion: Imaginary electric fields induce nontrivial, temperature-dependent modifications to mesonic screening properties in QCD, offering new insight into electromagnetic response and deconfinement dynamics.

Abstract: External electromagnetic fields provide a useful probe of QCD matter, but real electric fields are hindered by the sign problem, motivating studies with imaginary electric fields. We investigate mesonic screening correlators in lattice QCD at finite temperature in the presence of such a background using staggered fermions. At low temperature, scalar screening masses increase with the field strength, while pseudo-scalar masses remain largely unchanged, and charge-asymmetric channels show additional structure. At high temperature, the correlators exhibit clear spatial oscillations with frequencies set by the quark electric charges. These results demonstrate nontrivial modifications of screening properties induced by external electric fields.

</details>


### [83] [Extracting $B_s\to D_s^*\ellν_\ell$ form factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27879)
*Anastasia Boushmelev,Matthew Black,Oliver Witzel*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a lattice QCD analysis to extract form factors for exclusive semileptonic $B_s \to D_s^* \ell \nu_\ell$ decays using RBC/UKQCD gauge ensembles and domain-wall + RHQ fermion actions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise determination of CKM matrix elements and tests of lepton flavor universality via semileptonic $B_{(s)}$ decays.

Method: Lattice QCD computation on 2+1-flavor RBC/UKQCD ensembles with Shamir domain-wall fermions (light, strange, charm) and relativistic heavy quark (RHQ) action for bottom; form factor extraction for $B_s \to D_s^*$ in the narrow width approximation.

Result: Demonstration of full analysis pipeline to extract the four relevant hadronic form factors; use of multiple lattice spacings (1.785–2.785 GeV) and pion masses (268–433 MeV) enables controlled continuum and chiral extrapolations.

Conclusion: The methodology establishes a robust framework for computing $B_s \to D_s^*$ form factors from first principles, supporting high-precision Standard Model tests and BSM searches.

Abstract: Semileptonic $B_{(s)}$ decays are of great phenomenological interest because they allow to determine e.g. CKM matrix elements or test lepton flavor universality. Taking advantage of already existing lattice data, we demonstrate the analysis steps to extract the four form factors describing exclusive semileptonic $B_s\to D_s^*\ellν_\ell$ decays using the narrow width approximation. Our data are based on RBC/UKQCD's set of 2+1 flavor gauge field ensembles with Shamir domain-wall fermion and Iwasaki gauge field action featuring inverse lattice spacings of $a^{-1}=$1.785, 2.383, and 2.785 GeV as well as pion masses between 268 and 433 MeV. Light, strange and charm quarks are simulated using domain-wall fermions, whereas bottom quarks are generated with the relativistic heavy quark (RHQ) action.

</details>


### [84] [Heavy-Meson Bag Parameters using Gradient Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28517)
*Matthew Black,Robert V. Harlander,Jonas T. Kohnen,Fabian Lange,Antonio Rago,Andrea Shindler,Oliver Witzel*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces GF+SFTX—a gradient flow plus short flow-time expansion—as a robust renormalization method for four-quark operators in lattice QCD, applied to neutral heavy-meson mixing (ΔQ=2) and heavy-meson lifetimes (ΔQ=0); it delivers high-precision, scheme-consistent bag parameters at 3 GeV in the MS-bar scheme using six 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles and NNLO perturbative matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address power-divergent operator mixing—especially for dimension-six four-quark operators in ΔQ=2 and ΔQ=0 processes—where conventional lattice renormalization is challenging; to enable precise, scheme-connected, and systematically controlled determinations of bag parameters relevant to charm-strange systems and heavy-meson phenomenology.

Method: Gradient flow combined with short flow-time expansion (GF+SFTX), applied to six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles; perturbative SFTX matching to MS-bar up to NNLO; multi-scale matching and renormalization-group improved flow-time evolution to control systematic uncertainties; conversion formulae provided for arbitrary evanescent operator choices.

Result: For a fictitious neutral D_s meson: B̅^MS-bar_1(3 GeV) = 0.7673(123) (ΔQ=2); for ΔQ=0 lifetime-ratio basis: B̅^MS-bar_1(3 GeV) = 1.0524(97), B̅^MS-bar_2(3 GeV) = 0.9621(71), ε̅^MS-bar_1(3 GeV) = −0.2275(76), ε̅^MS-bar_2(3 GeV) = −0.0005(8); all values are in MS-bar at 3 GeV with full error budgets.

Conclusion: GF+SFTX is a viable, precise, and systematically controllable renormalization framework for dimension-six four-quark operators on the lattice; it shifts power-divergent mixing from the lattice regulator to the continuum, where it is handled by SFTX, paving the way for future applications to more complex operator bases in heavy-flavor physics.

Abstract: We demonstrate the use of the gradient flow combined with the short flow-time expansion (GF+SFTX) as a renormalization procedure for four-quark operator matrix elements and associated bag parameters relevant to neutral heavy-meson mixing ($ΔQ=2$) and heavy-meson lifetimes ($ΔQ=0$). Using six RBC/UKQCD 2+1-flavor domain-wall fermion ensembles, we calculate for a charm-strange system with physical quark masses flowed bag parameters and match them to the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme using perturbative SFTX coefficients up to next-to-next-to-leading order in QCD. We employ a multi-scale matching strategy and a renormalization-group improved flow-time evolution which allows for a reliable estimate of systematic uncertainties. For a fictitious neutral $D_s$ meson, we obtain the $ΔQ=2$ $\overline{\text{MS}}$ bag parameter ${\cal B}^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_1(3\,{\rm GeV})=0.7673(123)$, consistent with existing short-distance $D^0$ mixing determinations. For the $ΔQ=0$ lifetime-ratio operator basis, we find the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ results $B^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_1(3\,{\rm GeV})=1.0524(97)$, $B^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_2(3\,{\rm GeV})=0.9621(71)$, $ε^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_1(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.2275(76)$, and $ε^{\overline{\text{MS}}}_2(3\,{\rm GeV})=-0.0005(8)$. We provide conversion formulae to re-express these results for an arbitrary choice of evanescent operators. These results demonstrate that GF+SFTX can deliver precise determinations of dimension-six four-quark operators and establish a framework for future lattice computations including more complex operator bases, where the challenge of power-divergent mixing is shifted to the continuum and handled in the SFTX.

</details>


### [85] [Hadron Structure from lattice QCD in the context of the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28604)
*Constantia Alexandrou*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent lattice QCD advances in hadron structure calculations—especially for pion, kaon, and nucleon—and highlights their relevance to the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) physics program.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge theoretical progress in lattice QCD hadron structure calculations with the experimental goals of the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and other facilities by identifying key quantities that inform EIC science.

Method: Review and synthesis of state-of-the-art lattice QCD results for hadronic observables—including charges, form factors, Mellin moments, generalized parton distributions (GPDs), and transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs)—with emphasis on pion, kaon, and nucleon.

Result: High-precision lattice QCD results now exist for static and dynamical hadron structure observables; GPDs and TMDs are increasingly accessible via direct computation or moment reconstruction; these provide complementary 3D imaging of hadrons.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD has matured into a quantitative tool for hadron structure, delivering predictions and constraints critical for interpreting EIC data and guiding its scientific agenda.

Abstract: Hadron structure calculations using lattice Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) have advanced significantly in recent years. Results for charges, form factors, and lower Mellin moments can be obtained to high precision, generalized parton distributions can now be computed either directly or reconstructed from moments, and transverse-momentum-dependent distributions can be accessed through direct lattice calculations. Together, these quantities provide detailed and complementary insights into the internal structure of hadrons. These theoretical developments are highly relevant to the experimental program of the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and of other facilities. We review the most pertinent lattice QCD results for hadron structure that inform the EIC scientific agenda, with particular emphasis on the pion, kaon, and nucleon.

</details>


### [86] [Applying the Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo method to lattice gauge theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28629)
*Masafumi Fukuma*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces an extension of the Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo (WV-HMC) method to group manifolds, enabling its rigorous application to lattice gauge theories while avoiding sign-problem-induced ergodicity issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the numerical sign problem and ergodicity limitations in Lefschetz-thimble methods for lattice field theories.

Method: Extension of the Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo (WV-HMC) algorithm from flat spaces to compact group manifolds, preserving its geometric and stochastic structure for gauge-invariant sampling.

Result: A mathematically consistent formulation of WV-HMC on group manifolds, establishing a foundation for its use in non-Abelian lattice gauge theories.

Conclusion: The group-manifold extension of WV-HMC provides a viable, ergodicity-safe framework for tackling the sign problem in lattice gauge theories, advancing beyond current thimble-based approaches.

Abstract: The numerical sign problem remains one of the central challenges in computational physics. The Worldvolume Hybrid Monte Carlo (WV-HMC) method has recently been proposed as a reliable and computationally efficient algorithm that crucially avoids the ergodicity issues inherent in Lefschetz-thimble approaches. In these proceedings, after outlining the key ideas behind WV-HMC, we present its extension to group manifolds. This provides a rigorous framework for applying WV-HMC to lattice gauge theories.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [87] [High Sensitivity Methodologies to Detect Radio Band Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26848)
*Wei Hong,Peng He,Tong-Jie Zhang,Shi-Yu Li,Pei Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational wave (GW)–to–radio conversion in pulsar magnetospheres via the Gertsenshtein-Zeldovich effect, simulating detectability of transient and stochastic very-high-frequency (VHF) GWs using FAST and SKA2-MID telescopes; it proposes four observational strategies—most effectively MPMT—and projects characteristic strain sensitivities of hc ~10⁻²³ (transient) and hc ~10⁻³³ (stochastic) at 3 GHz, suggesting radio-band searches may soon probe viable VHF GW models and possibly explain repeating FRBs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether gravitational waves can be converted into detectable radio signals in pulsar magnetospheres via the Gertsenshtein-Zeldovich effect, and to assess observational feasibility for probing very-high-frequency (VHF) GWs—especially from primordial black hole mergers and stochastic backgrounds—that are inaccessible to current interferometers.

Method: Numerical simulations of GW-to-radio conversion in the magnetospheres of PSR J1856-3754 and PSR J0720-3125; sensitivity forecasting for FAST and SKA2-MID telescopes; evaluation of four detection strategies—including 'Multiple Pulsars with Multiple Telescopes' (MPMT)—under 6000 h integration at 3 GHz and 5σ threshold; modeling of transient (PBH-like mergers) and stochastic (primordial GW) sources.

Result: Projected minimum detectable characteristic strains: hc ≈ 10⁻²³ for transient GW events and hc ≈ 10⁻³³ for stochastic backgrounds at 3 GHz; MPMT yields best sensitivity via cross-validation and false-candidate rejection; under optimistic assumptions, radio-band searches approach sensitivity required to test representative VHF GW scenarios.

Conclusion: GW–radio conversion in pulsar magnetospheres is a viable pathway to probe otherwise inaccessible VHF GWs; the MPMT strategy significantly enhances detection credibility; this mechanism may also contribute to explaining galactic repeating fast radio bursts.

Abstract: Gravitational waves (GWs) can resonate with magnetic fields through the Gertsenshtein-Zeldovich effect, producing electromagnetic signals at the same frequency. In pulsar magnetospheres, this conversion may yield a faint radio-band signal that could be detected. In this work, we focus on two specific pulsars, PSR J1856-3754 and PSR J0720-3125, and use numerical simulations to evaluate how well the FAST and SKA2-MID telescopes could detect such signals. We consider transient events, including primordial-black-hole-like mergers, as well as stochastic backgrounds, including primordial GWs. To improve detection sensitivity, we propose four observational methods to lower the detectable energy-density limit of very high-frequency (VHF) GWs; the "Multiple Pulsars with Multiple Telescopes" (MPMT) method performs best because it allows cross-validation and rejection of false candidates. Under the assumption of nearly 6000 hours of observation at 3 GHz and a $5σ$ detection threshold, the minimum detectable characteristic strain is projected to be $h_c \approx 10^{-23}$ for transient events and $h_c \approx 10^{-33}$ for stochastic backgrounds. Under optimistic assumptions on integration time and conversion efficiency, these projections suggest that radio-band searches may approach the sensitivity needed to begin testing representative VHF GW scenarios. More broadly, this conversion in pulsar magnetospheres could be relevant to the origin of some repeating fast radio bursts in the our galaxy.

</details>


### [88] [Dynamical tidal response of neutron stars as a probe of dense-matter properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26886)
*Abhishek Hegade K. R.,Yumu Yang,Mauricio Hippert,Jacquelyn Noronha-Hostler,Jorge Noronha,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how relativistic tidal deformations in binary neutron star systems depend on neutron star internal structure, using nucleonic and MIT bag model equations of state; it finds that dissipative tides from weak-interaction bulk viscosity are observationally negligible, but the conservative dynamical tidal response is highly sensitive to symmetry energy slope and higher-order coefficients—enabling gravitational-wave constraints on nuclear symmetry energy parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dynamical and dissipative tidal deformations in binary neutron star inspirals encode information about neutron star internal structure—particularly nuclear equation-of-state parameters—and assess their detectability in gravitational-wave observations.

Method: Analytic modeling of tidal deformations using two equation-of-state classes: (i) a nucleonic model parameterized by nuclear observables (e.g., symmetry energy coefficients, saturation properties), and (ii) the MIT bag model for quark matter; self-consistent inclusion of weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous dissipation; computation of frequency- and temperature-dependent conservative and dissipative tidal response functions within general relativity.

Result: Dissipative tidal effects from bulk viscosity are too weak (within studied EoS models) for current or near-future GW detectors to resolve; however, the conservative dynamical tidal response exhibits strong dependence on the slope L and higher-order coefficients (e.g., K_sym) of the symmetry energy—implying measurable imprints in gravitational-wave phase evolution.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave observations of binary neutron star inspirals can constrain higher-order symmetry energy parameters via the conservative dynamical tidal response, while weak-interaction-driven dissipative tides remain undetectable with existing or planned detectors under these EoS assumptions.

Abstract: Dynamical tidal deformations play a crucial role in the gravitational waves emitted by binary neutron star systems during their late inspiral. In this work, we systematically explore how relativistic (dynamical and dissipative) tidal deformations depend on the internal structure of a neutron star using two analytic classes of equations of state. The first class is a nucleonic model that is parameterized by nuclear physics observables, such as the symmetry energy coefficients and saturation properties. The second class is a toy model of quark matter, the MIT bag model. To model tidal dissipation, we self-consistently include contributions from weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous effects while considering both the nucleonic and the quark-matter equations of state. The dissipative tide is sensitive to frequency and temperature, but its magnitude, as predicted by weak-interaction-driven bulk-viscous effects, is too small (within the equation-of-state models studied here) to be detectable by current or future observations. However, we find that the (conservative) dynamical tidal response function depends strongly on the slope of the symmetry energy and on higher-order coefficients of the symmetry energy; this implies that gravitational-wave observations could be used to probe higher-order coefficients of the symmetry energy through their effect on the (conservative) dynamical tide.

</details>


### [89] [Covariant Hamiltonian quantization of teleparallel equivalents to general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26961)
*David Chester,Vipul Pandey*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a covariant Hamiltonian framework for gravity using field strengths as velocities, avoiding singularities and primary constraints inherent in canonical GR; it introduces a Tomonaga-Schwinger-type evolution equation without preferred time, leveraging multisymplectic geometry and covariant phase space methods, aiming at a nonperturbative quantum gravity formulation classically equivalent to GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the frozen formalism and constraint-induced difficulties of canonical general relativity (e.g., Wheeler-DeWitt equation) by constructing a non-singular, covariant Hamiltonian formulation that admits well-defined evolution without reliance on a preferred time coordinate.

Method: Constructs a generalized De Donder–Weyl covariant Hamiltonian formalism where field strengths serve as velocity variables; employs multisymplectic geometry and covariant phase space techniques; introduces a Tomonaga–Schwinger-type functional evolution equation; proposes point-splitting regularization with renormalized hypersurface deformation generators to define hypersurface-dependent dynamics.

Result: A non-singular, constraint-free Hamiltonian density for teleparallel-equivalent gravity; a background-independent, time-coordinate-free evolution equation; a candidate framework for nonperturbative quantum gravity that is classically equivalent to general relativity.

Conclusion: The proposed field-strength-based covariant Hamiltonian framework resolves Legendre degeneracy and eliminates primary constraints, enabling a viable path toward a quantum theory of gravity free from the frozen formalism—though UV divergences, operator domain issues, and anomaly freedom remain open challenges.

Abstract: A covariant Hamiltonian formulation generalizing De Donder-Weyl mechanics is constructed with field strengths as velocity fields. Since the teleparallel equivalents to general relativity are quadratic in field strengths, the field-strength Hamiltonian densities are non-singular and avoid primary constraints specifically from Legendre degeneracy. In contrast, canonical general relativity and the Wheeler-DeWitt equation have a frozen formalism due to Hamiltonian constraints, while hypersurface deformations give refoliation gauge transformations. We introduce a Tomonaga-Schwinger-type equation without a preferred time coordinate by combining the generalized multisymplectic geometry with covariant phase space methods. Point-splitting regularization with renormalized hypersurface deformation generators is proposed as a candidate for hypersurface-dependent evolution. While ultraviolet divergences, operator domain issues, and anomaly freedom are still open problems, we provide a new framework for exploring nonperturbative quantum gravity that is classically equivalent to general relativity.

</details>


### [90] [Scattering and absorption sections by an improved Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26977)
*Omar Pedraza,L. A. López,L. O. Téllez Tovar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scattering and absorption cross-sections of an improved Schwarzschild black hole using classical, semi-classical, and partial wave methods; finds small deviations in classical regime but notable differences in interference patterns and amplitudes in semi-classical and partial wave regimes, and absorption deviates from sinc approximation at low frequencies—approaching horizon area—indicating quantum corrections affect black hole phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how quantum corrections to the Schwarzschild black hole modify its scattering and absorption properties, thereby probing phenomenological signatures of quantum gravity.

Method: Differential scattering cross-section analyzed via classical approximation, semi-classical approximation, and partial wave technique; absorption cross-section computed using partial wave method and compared with sinc approximation.

Result: Classical scattering shows only minor deviations from standard Schwarzschild; semi-classical and partial wave analyses reveal distinct interference patterns and amplitude changes; absorption cross-section deviates from sinc approximation in low-frequency regime, converging to horizon area in partial wave result.

Conclusion: Quantum corrections induce measurable modifications in scattering and absorption characteristics of Schwarzschild black holes, offering potential observational handles on quantum-gravitational effects.

Abstract: In this contribution, we investigate the scattering and absorption sections of the improved Schwarzschild black hole. The differential scattering section is analysed using three complementary approaches: the classical approximation, the semi-classical approximation, and the partial wave technique. We show that, while the classical scattering section exhibits only small deviations from the standard Schwarzschild case, the semi-classical and partial wave analyses reveal differences in the interference pattern and in the amplitude. Also, the absorption section is computed using the partial wave method and compared with the sinc approximation. We find that both approaches present deviations that appear in the low-frequency regime, where the partial wave result approaches the horizon area. Our results indicate that quantum corrections in the Schwarzschild can lead to modifications in scattering and absorption properties, providing further insight into the phenomenology of quantum corrected black holes.

</details>


### [91] [Memory effect for generalized modes in pp-waves spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27042)
*F. L. Carneiro,H. P. de Carvalho,M. P. Lobo,L. A. Cabral*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the memory effect of test particles interacting with pp-wave Gaussian pulses, focusing on higher-multipole polarization modes beyond standard quadrupolar (+ and ×) states; it numerically solves massive geodesic equations to analyze velocity and energy memory, finds a quartic scaling of relative kinetic energy variation with wave amplitude at low initial velocities, and links the energy memory effect to the integrated tidal field (i.e., cumulative spacetime curvature).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend memory effect analysis beyond standard quadrupolar gravitational wave polarizations and clarify how higher-multipole modes influence permanent changes in particle kinematics, while avoiding coordinate artifacts.

Method: Numerical integration of massive geodesic equations for test particles in pp-wave Gaussian pulse backgrounds with varying multipolar index m; formulation in terms of relative motion between particle pairs to eliminate coordinate artifacts; analysis of velocity and kinetic energy memory via asymptotic comparison of pre- and post-wave states.

Result: Relative kinetic energy memory scales as ∝ A⁴ (A = wave amplitude) for low initial velocities; the quartic coefficient depends on m, encoding spatial gradient effects of higher-polarization modes; energy memory is exactly determined by the integral of the tidal tensor over time — i.e., ∫C₀ᵗ Rᵢₜⱼₖ(τ) dxᵏ dτ.

Conclusion: Higher-multipole gravitational wave modes induce distinct, amplitude-dependent memory signatures; the energy memory effect is fundamentally governed by the integrated tidal field, confirming a geometric origin tied to spacetime curvature history, not just wave polarization.

Abstract: The memory effect of test particles interacting with pp-wave Gaussian pulses is investigated for polarization modes beyond the standard quadrupolar $+$ and $\times$ states. Massive geodesic equations are solved numerically for several values of the multipolar index $m$, allowing the analysis of velocity and energy memory effects. In order to eliminate possible coordinate artifacts, the study is formulated in terms of the relative motion between two test particles. The results show that the relative kinetic energy variation exhibits a quartic dependence on the wave amplitude in the regime of low initial velocities. The coefficient of this scaling is found to depend on the multipolar structure of the wave, reflecting the spatial gradients associated with higher polarization modes. It is further shown that the energy memory effect is determined by the integrated tidal field, linking the permanent change in the relative kinetic energy to the history of the spacetime curvature carried by the wave.13

</details>


### [92] [Optimized numerical evolution of perturbations across sharp background trajectory turns in multifield inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27080)
*Guillermo F. Quispe Peña,Johor D. Peñalba Quispitupa,José T. Gálvez Ghersi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an efficient numerical method for evolving primordial scalar fluctuations in multifield inflation, capable of accurately handling sharp turns in background trajectories—whether from potential or field-space geometry—while scaling robustly to many fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp turns in background inflationary trajectories (from potential or field-space geometry) challenge standard numerical methods for computing primordial power spectrum features, especially beyond slow-roll and in multifield models.

Method: A novel, efficient numerical scheme for solving the coupled mode equations of scalar perturbations, designed to use timesteps comparable to those required for background evolution, and applicable to arbitrary field-space metrics and general multifield potentials.

Result: The method accurately captures perturbation evolution through rapid turns in arbitrary field-space geometries, maintains numerical stability, and scales efficiently with increasing numbers of fields.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a reliable, scalable computational framework for systematically exploring spectral features in strongly non-slow-roll, multifield inflationary models.

Abstract: Features in the primordial power spectrum require numerical methods that are both accurate and scalable across the wide class of multifield inflationary models that produce them. Sharp turns in the background trajectories, induced by either potential or geometric effects, render these computations particularly challenging. In this work, we introduce an efficient method for evolving primordial scalar fluctuations, requiring timesteps comparable to those used for the background evolution. We demonstrate that the method accurately tracks perturbations through rapidly turning trajectories in arbitrary field-space geometries, enabling systematic exploration of spectral features across diverse multifield scenarios. Our approach scales robustly to large numbers of degrees of freedom, providing a reliable computational framework for probing regimes that significantly depart from slow-roll dynamics.

</details>


### [93] [5D black holes and mirror stars from nonlinear electrodynamics: Existence and stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27174)
*Kirill A. Bronnikov,Sergei V. Bolokhov,Milena V. Skvortsova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies static, spherically symmetric 5D black holes and mirror stars in general relativity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics (NED), deriving analytic solutions and analyzing their radial stability—black holes are fully stable, while mirror stars are only stable in a restricted parameter range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize previous stability results for Einstein-Maxwell systems to the broader class of Einstein–nonlinear electrodynamics (Einstein-NED) in five dimensions, particularly for static, spherically symmetric configurations with magnetic fields.

Method: Constructs exact analytic 5D static, spherically symmetric solutions of Einstein gravity coupled to NED with magnetic ansatz; derives metric and Lagrangian $L(F)$ explicitly for two representative cases; performs linear radial (monopole) perturbation analysis to assess stability.

Result: Two families of analytic solutions are obtained: one describing 5D black holes (black strings), the other describing mirror (topological) stars with perfectly reflecting boundaries; full black hole family is radially stable; mirror star stability holds only in a bounded region of parameter space.

Conclusion: Radial stability behavior observed in Einstein-Maxwell systems extends qualitatively to Einstein-NED in 5D, but with stricter constraints on mirror star viability—highlighting the role of nonlinearity in determining compact object stability and viability.

Abstract: We consider static, spherically symmetric solutions of 5D general relativity with magnetic fields governed by nonlinear electrodynamics (NED) with the Lagrangian $L(F)$, $F = F_{AB} F^{AB}$, and show that generic solutions describe either 5D black holes (also called black strings due to a circular extra dimension) or so-called mirror stars (also called topological stars) with perfectly reflecting boundary surfaces. Two particular examples of such solutions have been obtained, admitting analytic expressions for the metric coefficients and $L(F)$, and their stability under radial (monopole) perturbations is studied. While the whole obtained family of black hole solutions turns out to be stable, mirror star solutions prove to be stable only in a certain range in the parameter space. We thus extend to the Einstein-NED system the results previously obtained for Einstein-Maxwell fields.

</details>


### [94] [Quantum gravitodiamagnetic interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27180)
*Di Hao,Jiawei Hu,Hongwei Yu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a gravitodiamagnetic interaction Hamiltonian in linearized quantum gravity, shows its diamagnetic signature (opposite-sign induced quadrupole), and computes an attractive r^{-11} interaction potential between two massive objects via vacuum fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate quantum gravitational interactions beyond standard gravitoelectric/gravitomagnetic couplings, specifically the quadratic gravitodiamagnetic coupling to vacuum gravitational field fluctuations.

Method: Starts from particle Lagrangian in gravitational field; applies Weyl gravitoelectromagnetism formalism to derive gravitodiamagnetic interaction Hamiltonian; uses leading-order perturbation theory on a spherically symmetric gravitational hydrogen-like ground state to compute induced quadrupole and interaction potential.

Result: Demonstrates opposite-sign induced quadrupole moment (signature of gravitodiamagnetism); obtains explicit attractive interaction potential scaling as r^{-11} for all separations.

Conclusion: Gravitodiamagnetic coupling—quadratic in gravitomagnetic field—induces a universal attractive r^{-11} quantum gravitational interaction between massive bodies, distinct from classical or linear quantum effects.

Abstract: In the framework of linearized quantum gravity, we investigate the quantum gravitational interaction induced by the gravitodiamagnetic coupling of two massive objects to vacuum fluctuations of the gravitational field. Starting from the Lagrangian of a particle in a gravitational field and employing the formalism of Weyl gravitoelectromagnetism, we derive the interaction Hamiltonian associated with gravitodiamagnetic coupling. Unlike the linear couplings that arise in gravitoelectric and gravitomagnetic interactions, the gravitodiamagnetic coupling depends quadratically on the gravitomagnetic field. Based on this Hamiltonian, we show that, for a spherically symmetric gravitational hydrogen-like system in its ground state, the induced quadrupole moment has the opposite sign to the applied gravitomagnetic field, which is the defining signature of gravitodiamagnetism. Using leading-order perturbation theory, we further obtain an explicit expression for the resulting interaction potential, which is attractive and scales as $r^{-11}$ at all separations, where $r$ denotes the distance between the two objects.

</details>


### [95] [Bohmian singularity resolution and quantum relaxation in Bianchi type-I quantum cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27229)
*Vishal,Malay K. Nandy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores singularity resolution and quantum relaxation in Bohmian quantum cosmology using Gaussian and Lorentzian wavepackets in a Bianchi-I minisuperspace model; Lorentzian wavepackets yield more non-singular bounces and better (though incomplete) quantum relaxation compared to Gaussian ones, due to stronger quantum potential and richer velocity fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum effects—specifically wavefunction structure—govern cosmological singularity avoidance and the emergence of Born-rule equilibrium in Bohmian mechanics within quantum cosmology.

Method: Constructs Gaussian and Lorentzian wavepackets in the Wheeler-DeWitt framework for a plane-symmetric Bianchi type-I minisuperspace; computes Bohmian trajectories, quantum potential, velocity fields, and H-function evolution from non-equilibrium initial distributions.

Result: Lorentzian wavepackets generate a significant fraction of non-singular bounce trajectories and monotonic H-function decay toward equilibrium; Gaussian wavepackets mostly yield singular classical behavior and laminar flow with saturated, non-monotonic H-function decay.

Conclusion: The functional form of the wavepacket—particularly its momentum-space tail (power-law vs. exponential)—critically determines both singularity resolution and the efficiency of quantum relaxation via the structure of the Bohmian velocity field and quantum potential.

Abstract: We investigate cosmological singularity resolution and relaxation dynamics within the Bohmian mechanics via the plane-symmetric Bianchi type-I minisuperspace model in the Wheeler-DeWitt framework of quantum cosmology by constructing wave functions as Gaussian and Lorentzian wavepackets. Our analyses of the corresponding Bohmian trajectories reveal that Gaussian superposition predominantly yields classical singular solutions, with only a low fraction of small-amplitude cyclic trajectories. On the other hand, the Lorentzian wavepacket, characterized by the power-law momentum tail, generates stronger quantum potential barrier and a substantially rich velocity field, producing a significant fraction of non-singular bounce trajectories over extended volume ranges. We further examine quantum relaxation by evolving non-equilibrium distributions under the corresponding guidance dynamics. The Gaussian superposition exhibits laminar flow leading to boundary accumulation and incomplete relaxation, with non-monotonic decay of the $H$-function followed by saturation. In contrast, the Lorentzian wavepacket induces more complex trajectories, yielding monotonic decay of the $H$-function and better, though still incomplete, approach to Born-rule equilibrium. These results demonstrate that the inherent structure of the wave packet governs both singularity resolution and quantum relaxation through the nature of the Bohmian velocity field.

</details>


### [96] [The point-particle-limit effective-source approach for computing gravitational self-force in the Lorenz gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27284)
*Chao Zhang,Yungui Gong,Xuchen Lu,Wenting Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces a point-particle-limit effective source method that analytically takes the source size to zero, yielding well-defined jump conditions for the metric perturbation; coupled with a discontinuous Galerkin scheme, it enables accurate, efficient time-domain self-force computation in the Lorenz gauge for circular orbits around Schwarzschild black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome high computational cost and implementation complexity of the traditional effective-source method caused by its complex analytical expressions and smoothness limit.

Method: Analytically takes the effective source size to zero (point-particle limit), deriving a jump condition for the retarded metric field at the particle location governed by the local singular field; implements this via a discontinuous Galerkin scheme capable of enforcing discontinuities accurately.

Result: Superior numerical accuracy and efficiency compared to the traditional effective-source method in computing time-domain gravitational metric perturbations and self-force for a point particle in circular orbit around a Schwarzschild black hole in the Lorenz gauge.

Conclusion: The point-particle-limit effective source method is validated as correct and efficient, establishing a robust numerical foundation for future computations of generic geodesic orbits and long-time self-consistent orbital evolution.

Abstract: The traditional effective-source method is hampered by complex analytical expressions and the inherent smoothness limit, which incur high computational costs and complicate implementation. To overcome these limitations, we introduce the point-particle-limit effective source method, which analytically takes the size of the effective source to zero, thereby transforming the problem into a well-defined jump condition of retarded metric field at the particle position governed by the local singular field. This formulation naturally pairs with a discontinuous Galerkin scheme, whose inherent capacity for accommodating solution discontinuities enables highly accurate enforcement of the jump conditions. We apply both the traditional and point-particle-limit effective source method to calculate the time-domain gravitational metric perturbation and gravitational self-force in the Lorenz gauge on a point particle in a circular orbit around a Schwarzschild black hole. The comparison of numerical results shows the excellent advantage of the point-particle-limit effective source method, which validates the correctness and efficiency of the point-particle-limit effective source method and thereby establishes a numerical foundation for computing generic geodesic orbits or long-time self-consistent orbital evolution.

</details>


### [97] [Quantizing the exterior region of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole leads to a resolution of the information paradox on a quantum level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27339)
*Claus Gerhardt*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper resolves the black hole information paradox by quantizing both exterior and interior Schwarzschild-AdS regions, establishing unitary equivalence between their Hilbert spaces via consistent eigenvalue multiplicities of spatial eigendistributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the black hole information paradox at the quantum level by providing a consistent quantization framework for both exterior and interior regions of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole.

Method: Quantizes the exterior region of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole using a quantum gravity model, yielding a hyperbolic equation solved by temporal eigenfunctions $w_i$ (simple eigenvalues) and spatial eigendistributions $v_{ij}$ (with multiplicities $m_i$); extends the analysis to the interior region to fix $m_i$ by maximizing them there, then applies the same $m_i$ to the exterior to enforce unitary equivalence.

Result: A unitary equivalence is established between the Hilbert spaces and Hamiltonians of the exterior and interior quantizations, eliminating the information paradox in this model.

Conclusion: The information paradox does not arise in this quantum gravity framework because unitarity is preserved across the black hole’s exterior and interior regions via consistent spectral data and shared temporal Hamiltonian structure.

Abstract: We quantize the exterior region of a Schwarzschild-AdS black hole using our model of quantum gravity. The resulting hyperbolic equation is solved by products of temporal eigenfunctions $w_i$, the eigenvalues of which all have multiplicity one, and spatial eigendistributions $v_{ij}$ having the same eigenvalues but with multiplicities $1\le m_i$, where the $m_i$ could in principle be arbitrarily large. Regarding only the exterior region, there was no guidance how to determine the values of the $m_i$. However, considering also the quantization of the interior region, where the same question did not arise since the $m_i$ could be chosen by maximizing the value, it seemed logical to choose the same values, too, in the exterior case. Since the eigenvalues in the interior are the same because the temporal Hamiltonian is the same in both cases, this choice defined a unitary equivalence between the respective Hilbert spaces and the respective Hamiltonians. Hence, there is no information paradox on a quantum level.

</details>


### [98] [Continuous Sensitivity Analysis for $δN$ Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27366)
*S. Mohammad Ahmadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the δN formalism by incorporating spatial gradient interactions via Continuous Sensitivity Analysis, enabling efficient analytical and numerical computation of cosmological observables—including the k-dependent power spectrum and equilateral non-Gaussianity—in models like Starobinsky with ultra-slow-roll transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the technical difficulty of computing phase-space derivatives in the gradient-corrected δN formalism, which is essential for predicting observables beyond the separate-universe approximation.

Method: Applies Continuous Sensitivity Analysis to the gradient-corrected δN framework: solves coupled first-order ODEs for the field Jacobian and Hessian to compute sensitivity of e-folds N to initial conditions, including spatial gradient contributions.

Result: Derives analytical k-dependent power spectrum with full gradient corrections and an analytical estimate of f_NL^eq capturing gradient-sourced non-Gaussianity in the Starobinsky model featuring ultra-slow-roll transition.

Conclusion: Continuous Sensitivity Analysis provides a robust, systematic, and computationally efficient method to implement gradient corrections in the δN formalism, enhancing its predictive power for primordial perturbations in realistic inflationary dynamics.

Abstract: The $δN$ formalism provides a powerful non-perturbative framework for following the evolution of primordial curvature perturbations on super-horizon scales. However, its standard implementation relies on the separate universe assumption, which neglects significant spatial gradient interactions. Recent work has addressed this limitation by incorporating gradient interactions directly into the background dynamics through an effective source term in the Klein--Gordon equation, thereby extending the applicability of the $δN$ framework beyond the separate universe approximation. Despite this conceptual progress, practical calculations within the $δN$ formalism remain technically challenging, as cosmological observables require evaluating the sensitivity of the total number of $e$-folds to initial conditions, a task that becomes even more involved once gradient contributions are included. In this work, we develop a systematic method to simplify these calculations by applying Continuous Sensitivity Analysis to the gradient-corrected $δN$ framework. In this approach, the required phase-space derivatives are obtained by solving a set of coupled first-order differential equations for the field Jacobian and Hessian, which significantly streamlines both analytical and numerical evaluations of $δN$ formalism. As an explicit demonstration, we apply the method to the Starobinsky model, which features a sharp transition into an ultra-slow-roll phase. Within this setup, we derive analytical expressions for the $k$-dependent power spectrum including full gradient corrections, and obtain an analytical estimate of the equilateral non-Gaussianity parameter $f_{\rm NL}^{\rm eq}$ that accurately captures the gradient-sourced contributions.

</details>


### [99] [Thermal channels of scalar and tensor waves in Jordan-frame scalar--tensor gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27386)
*David S. Pereira,Francisco S. N Lobo,José Pedro Mimoso*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear perturbations in Jordan-frame scalar-tensor gravity using an Einstein-like effective-fluid decomposition, showing that scalar and tensor perturbations map exactly to effective thermodynamic quantities (density, pressure, heat flux, anisotropic stress) at first order—particularly identifying gravitational-wave damping with transverse-traceless anisotropic stress—and clarifies the gauge behavior and matching conditions for the invariant κT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a physically transparent, thermodynamically inspired interpretation of scalar-tensor gravity perturbations—especially how scalar degrees of freedom modify gravitational-wave propagation and background evolution—by leveraging an effective-fluid description in the Jordan frame.

Method: Linearized perturbation theory about a spatially flat FLRW background in Jordan-frame scalar-tensor gravity; use of the Einstein-like effective-fluid decomposition; derivation of perturbed effective energy-momentum components (density, pressure, heat flux, anisotropic stress) in the scalar-gradient frame; identification of their roles in each linearized Einstein-type equation (Hamiltonian, momentum, trace, traceless, and tensor); analysis of the invariant κT perturbation and its gauge properties and matching conditions.

Result: Exact Eckart-type constitutive relations hold at linear order between effective fluid variables and geometric/scalar perturbations; each linearized field equation maps uniquely to one effective-fluid channel; Jordan-frame GW damping is fully encoded in the effective transverse-traceless anisotropic stress; the perturbation δ(κT) is not fixed by FLRW flux matching—only its background value κ̄T is determined—implying residual freedom potentially linked to thermodynamic interpretation.

Conclusion: The effective-fluid picture provides a rigorous, exhaustive correspondence between linear perturbations and thermodynamic variables in scalar-tensor gravity, suggesting gravitational waves may admit a deeper intrinsic thermodynamic characterization—though current results only establish an effective (not fundamental) constitutive description.

Abstract: We study first-order scalar and tensor perturbations of Jordan-frame scalar--tensor gravity about a spatially flat FLRW background using the Einstein-like effective-fluid decomposition of the scalar sector. In the scalar-gradient frame, we derive the perturbed effective density, pressure, heat flux, and anisotropic stress, and show that they admit an exact Eckart-type constitutive identification at linear order. We then show that these same quantities appear explicitly and exhaustively in the linearized field equations: the scalar Hamiltonian, momentum, trace, and traceless Einstein-like equations are governed, respectively, by the effective density, heat-flux, pressure, and anisotropic-stress channels, while the tensor propagation equation is governed by the transverse-traceless anisotropic-stress channel.
