In particular, the Jordan-frame modification of gravitational-wave damping is identified with the effective transverse-traceless anisotropic stress of the scalar sector. We also derive the perturbed evolution equation for the invariant product $κT$, clarify its gauge behavior, and show that flux matching on FLRW fixes only the background value $\overline{κT}$, not its perturbation. These results leave open the possibility that gravitational waves in scalar--tensor gravity admit a deeper thermodynamic characterization, perhaps even an intrinsic one, although the present analysis establishes this only at the level of an effective constitutive description.

</details>


### [100] [Gravitational lensing and observational features of a dynamic black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27606)
*Ke-Jian He,Guo-Ping Li,Li-Fang Li,Xiao-Xiong Zeng*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational lensing and shadow evolution of Vaidya black holes using backward ray-tracing, revealing time-dependent features—lensing rings, a transient bright ring (photon + lensing), and a dynamically redshifted contracting/brightening ring—whose asymmetry encodes Doppler and dynamical redshift effects; it identifies dynamical redshift as a novel observable signature of spacetime evolution in accreting black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable imprints of temporal spacetime evolution in accreting black holes, particularly distinguishing evolving (e.g., Vaidya) from stationary black hole geometries via time-resolved shadow and lensing features.

Method: Backward ray-tracing in the celestial sphere framework applied to Vaidya black holes; extended analysis includes thin accretion disk emission, Doppler boosting, and dynamical redshift modeling under varying observer inclinations.

Result: 1) Shadow evolves from stable → expanding → static; 2) A persistent lensing ring appears outside shadow during/after accretion; 3) A bright ring (photon + lensing superposition) exists only at early/late accretion stages; 4) A new inward-contracting, continuously brightening ring emerges due to dynamical redshift; 5) Doppler + dynamical redshift jointly induce strong brightness asymmetry in shadow, bright ring, and redshifted ring.

Conclusion: Dynamical redshift is a novel, intrinsic observable in evolving black hole spacetimes; its associated ring structure provides a discriminant for accreting black holes and enables direct observational probing of temporal metric evolution.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the gravitational lensing effects and the dynamic evolution of the shadow of Vaidya black holes by employing backward ray-tracing techniques. Within the celestial sphere framework, the black hole shadow exhibits a complete evolutionary sequence, transitioning from an initial stable configuration through continuous expansion to a final static state. Notably, during and after the active accretion phase, a distinct lensing ring emerges outside the shadow. Extending this analysis to the thin accretion disk model reveals richer observational signatures. A bright ring, formed by the superposition of the photon ring and lensing ring, appears outside the shadow but persists only during the initial and final stages of accretion, vanishing entirely when accretion becomes active. Interestingly, as the accretion process progresses, an additional ring-like structure, which is caused by the dynamical redshift effect, emerges in the image. This ring-like structure not only contracts inward but also brightens continuously as accretion proceeds. Under varying observational inclinations, the Doppler effect and the dynamical redshift effect jointly modulate the brightness distribution of the image, resulting in significant asymmetry in the inner shadow, bright ring, and additional ring. Our findings uncover dynamical redshift as a novel observable phenomenon intrinsic to evolving spacetimes, offering a potential discriminant for identifying accreting black holes and providing observational access to the imprints of temporal spacetime evolution on black hole images.

</details>


### [101] [$q$-Deformed Quantum Mechanics and the Thermodynamics of Black Hole/White Hole Spectral pair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27621)
*S. Jalalzadeh,R. Jalalzadeh,H. Moradpour*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Schwarzschild black and white holes using a q-deformed Wheeler–DeWitt framework, yielding a bounded mass spectrum, finite entropy, universal logarithmic corrections, minimum temperature, maximum entropy matching the de Sitter bound, and a stable cold remnant—offering a divergence-free semiclassical picture bridging quantum gravity and cosmology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve divergences in black hole evaporation (e.g., infinite temperature/entropy at endpoint) and establish a consistent semiclassical framework incorporating quantum deformation and holography.

Method: Introduces a q-deformed Heisenberg–Weyl algebra at a root of unity within the Wheeler–DeWitt formalism, leading to a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, bounded mass spectrum, adiabatic invariant, and thermodynamic analysis including entropy-mass relation, temperature, heat capacity, and holographic entropy bounds.

Result: Derives bounded entropy and mass spectra, universal logarithmic entropy correction, nonzero minimum temperature, maximum entropy saturating the de Sitter bound, dynamically stable cold remnant with vanishing radiation rate but negative heat capacity, and holographic consistency with limited entropy.

Conclusion: q-deformation provides a natural regularization mechanism: it cures evaporation singularities, enforces an entropy bound, ensures stability of remnants, and supports a smooth quantum-gravity-to-cosmology transition within a semiclassical framework.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the thermodynamics of Schwarzschild black and white holes within a $q$-deformed Wheeler--DeWitt framework. By introducing a $q$-deformed Heisenberg--Weyl algebra at a root of unity, we derive a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, a bounded mass spectrum, and an adiabatic invariant leading to a bounded entropy-mass relation. The deformation results in a universal logarithmic correction, as well as a minimum temperature and a maximum entropy that matches the de Sitter bound. Also, we examine the interpretation of a cold remnant, which is dynamically stable because its radiation rate approaches zero, even though its heat capacity remains negative. We also explore the holographic implications of this limited entropy. Our results thus provide a consistent semiclassical picture, where quantum deformation naturally introduces an entropy bound, avoids divergences at the final evaporation stage, and suggests a smooth transition from quantum gravity to cosmology.

</details>


### [102] [A Minimal and Stable Vacuum Bounce in Exponential $f(R)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27629)
*G. G. L. Nashed,A. Eid*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a minimal, perturbatively stable vacuum cosmological bounce in metric f(R) gravity by extending the Starobinsky R² model with a controlled exponential deformation and a constant term; it proves a no-go for the deformed model alone, identifies instability-free parameter regions, and demonstrates finite, well-behaved scalar and tensor perturbations across the bounce in the Einstein frame.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a nonsingular, perturbatively stable cosmological bounce in metric f(R) gravity beyond background-level constructions, addressing the challenge of avoiding ghost/tachyon instabilities and ensuring well-behaved perturbations through the bounce.

Method: Analytical derivation of bounce conditions; proof of no-go for f(R) = R + αR²(1 − e^{−R/R_b}); exact construction of bounce with constant-term extension; systematic parameter-space scan for ghost (positive effective gravitational coupling) and tachyonic (positive squared mass for scalar degree of freedom) stability; Einstein-frame formulation and numerical evolution of linearized scalar and tensor perturbations across the Gaussian-type bounce.

Result: A minimal f(R) model f(R) = R + αR²(1 − e^{−R/R_b}) + Λ supports an exact vacuum bounce when Λ is fixed algebraically by the bounce condition; stable parameter regions are identified where both scalar and tensor perturbations remain finite and regular across the bounce; no ghost or tachyon instabilities occur in those regions.

Conclusion: A minimal, analytically controlled, and perturbatively stable vacuum bounce is achieved in metric f(R) gravity — the first such realization that ensures full linear stability (ghost- and tachyon-free) and well-defined perturbation evolution through the bounce, going significantly beyond purely background solutions.

Abstract: We investigate the realization of a nonsingular cosmological bounce in metric $f(R)$ gravity using a controlled exponential deformation of the Starobinsky $R^{2}$ model. Adopting a smooth Gaussian-type bouncing scale factor, we first demonstrate a no-go result showing that a positive-curvature vacuum bounce cannot be supported by the model $f(R)=R+αR^{2}(1-e^{-R/R_b})$ alone. We then show that a minimal extension obtained by introducing a constant term restores the bounce exactly, with the constant fixed algebraically by the bounce condition. A systematic parameter-space scan is performed to identify regions free of ghost and tachyonic instabilities. Working in the Einstein frame, we study the evolution of scalar and tensor perturbations across the bounce and show that both remain finite and well behaved. Our results establish a minimal, perturbatively stable realization of a vacuum bounce in $f(R)$ gravity that goes beyond background-level constructions.

</details>


### [103] [Connection Between the Shadow Radius and Quasinormal Frequencies for Black Holes in STVG with Perfect Fluid Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27935)
*Ziqiang Cai,Zhi Li,Zhenglong Ban,Qi-Qi Liang,Zheng-Wen Long*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact analytical link between black hole shadow radius and quasinormal mode (QNM) real frequency in scalar-tensor-vector gravity with perfect fluid dark matter, showing both are governed by the unstable photon orbit; validated numerically across scalar, electromagnetic, and gravitational perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify black hole shadow observations and gravitational wave ringdown as dual probes of strong-field gravity, specifically testing scalar-tensor-vector gravity (STVG/MOG) coupled to perfect fluid dark matter via their shared dependence on the photon sphere.

Method: Combined sixth-order WKB approximation, Padé resummation, and time-domain numerical integration to compute QNMs for s=0,1,2 perturbations; derived exact eikonal-limit relation ω_R = l / b_c using photon sphere geometry and angular velocity, identifying shadow radius R_sh ≡ b_c.

Result: Both QNM real frequencies ω_R and effective potential peak height increase with dark matter intensity λ but decrease with MOG parameter α; the analytical relation ω_R = l / R_sh holds precisely in the eikonal limit and is confirmed numerically across all three methods and spin weights.

Conclusion: Black hole shadow and QNM ringdown are dual manifestations of the same unstable photon orbit, enabling a unified multi-messenger constraint on modified gravity (α) and dark matter (λ) in the strong-field regime.

Abstract: We investigate the connection between black hole shadow and quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra in the context of scalar--tensor--vector gravity (STVG) coupled to perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM), characterized by the MOG parameter $α$ and the dark matter intensity $λ$. Employing complementary methods -- namely the sixth-order WKB approximation, Padé resummation, and time-domain numerical integration -- we compute QNM frequencies for scalar ($s=0$), electromagnetic ($s=1$), and axial gravitational ($s=2$) perturbations. Both the real part of the QNM frequencies and the peak height of the effective potential display a consistent parametric dependence: they increase with $λ$ yet decrease with growing $α$. In the eikonal limit ($l \gg 1$), we derive an exact analytical link between the shadow radius $R_{\mathrm{sh}}$ and the QNM frequency $ω_R$. Noting that $R_{\mathrm{sh}}$ is determined by the critical impact parameter $b_c = r_{\mathrm{ph}}/\sqrt{f(r_{\mathrm{ph}})}$, while $ω_R = Ωl$ with photon angular velocity $Ω= \sqrt{f(r_{\mathrm{ph}})}/r_{\mathrm{ph}}$, we obtain the precise relation $ω_R = l / b_c$, identifying $R_{\mathrm{sh}} \equiv b_c$ for an asymptotically flat observer. This prediction is robustly validated by numerical results across all three computational approaches at large multipole numbers. Our findings reveal that the black hole shadow and gravitational ringdown are not independent phenomena, but dual observational signatures of the same underlying structure -- the unstable photon orbit -- thereby offering a unified multi-messenger framework to simultaneously constrain modified gravity and dark matter in the strong-field regime.

</details>


### [104] [Secular evolution of orbital parameters for general bound orbits in Kerr spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27941)
*Norichika Sago,Ryuichi Fujita,Soichiro Isoyama,Hiroyuki Nakano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically computes secular orbital evolution (energy, angular momentum, Carter constant) for generic bound Kerr orbits up to 6PN and 16th order in eccentricity, validates against Teukolsky-based numerics, and develops hybrid and resummed approximations for efficient EMRI waveform modeling for LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable fast, accurate, analytic adiabatic inspiral and waveform models for extreme mass ratio inspirals (EMRIs), crucial for data analysis with space-based gravitational-wave detectors like LISA, by improving the accuracy and convergence of post-Newtonian (PN) expansions for highly eccentric orbits.

Method: Analytical derivation of secular orbital parameter evolution (energy, angular momentum, Carter constant) in Kerr spacetime at leading order in mass ratio, up to 6PN order and 16th order in orbital eccentricity; validation against high-precision numerical solutions of the Teukolsky equation; development and testing of hybrid approximations combining different PN/eccentricity truncations; assessment of exponential resummation at higher PN orders.

Result: High-accuracy analytical formulas for secular orbital evolution validated against Teukolsky results; quantification of eccentricity’s impact on PN convergence and accuracy; demonstration that hybrid approximations retain high fidelity at reduced computational cost; evidence that exponential resummation improves convergence at higher PN orders.

Conclusion: The derived analytical formulas and improved approximation schemes constitute essential building blocks for efficient, accurate, analytic EMRI waveform models suitable for LISA data analysis, bridging the gap between full numerical relativity and slow-motion PN expansions for generic Kerr orbits.

Abstract: We analytically derive the secular changes of the orbital parameters, i.e., energy, angular momentum, and Carter constant, for general bound orbits in Kerr spacetime, at leading order in the mass ratio, through the 6th post-Newtonian (6PN) order and the 16th order in orbital eccentricity. We validate the formulas against high-precision numerical Teukolsky results and quantify how eccentricity affects both the achievable accuracy and the PN convergence. We then construct and test a simple ``hybrid'' approximation that combines different PN and eccentricity truncations to retain accuracy at reduced computational cost. We also assess the performance of exponential resummation at higher PN orders. These results provide building blocks for fast, (analytic) adiabatic inspiral and waveform models for extreme mass ratio inspirals relevant to space-based detectors such as the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA).

</details>


### [105] [The Hodograph Transform Between Thermodynamics and Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28039)
*Leonid Polterovich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper links relativistic light-ray geometry (skies) of accelerating observers in Minkowski space to thermodynamics via contact geometry and a hyperbolic hodograph transform, deriving an acceleration-dependent effective temperature consistent with Unruh scaling but differing numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a geometric-thermodynamic correspondence for relativistic observers by interpreting light-ray skies — as Legendrian submanifolds in a contact phase space — as equilibrium thermodynamic states.

Method: Uses contact geometry on the spherical cotangent bundle of the Minkowski hyperboloid Cauchy hypersurface; applies a hyperbolic hodograph transform to identify it with a thermodynamic phase space; analyzes generating functions of evolving skies of a uniformly accelerating observer; rescales them to interpret as reduced free energies; derives effective temperature from thermodynamic relations.

Result: Generating functions of skies admit interpretation as reduced free energies; an effective temperature emerges that scales linearly with proper acceleration (T ∝ a), matching Unruh’s scaling law but with a different proportionality constant.

Conclusion: The contact-geometric structure of light propagation encodes thermodynamic behavior for accelerating observers, providing a geometric foundation for observer-dependent thermal phenomena — a kinematic analogue of the Unruh effect rooted in Legendrian dynamics and hodographic duality.

Abstract: In the contact-geometric approach to general relativity, the sky of an event - namely, the set of all incoming light rays - forms a Legendrian submanifold of the spherical cotangent bundle of a Cauchy hypersurface. When the hypersurface is chosen to be the Minkowski hyperboloid, a hyperbolic version of the hodograph transform identifies this bundle with a thermodynamic phase space. We consider a uniformly accelerating observer starting on the hyperboloid and study the evolution of its skies. We show that the associated generating functions, after a suitable rescaling, admit a natural interpretation as reduced free energies of equilibrium thermodynamic systems governed by the relativistic Doppler effect. From this data, we extract an effective temperature that is proportional to the acceleration, in agreement with the scaling of the Unruh effect, although the numerical constant differs from the Unruh value.

</details>


### [106] [Detecting Black hole surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter in Kalb-Ramond fields using quasinormal modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27974)
*Zongyuan Qin,Taiyang Zhang,Qian Feng,Zheng-Wen Long*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of static, spherically symmetric black holes in a spacetime modified by both spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking (via the Kalb-Ramond field) and perfect fluid dark matter. Using EHT M87* shadow data, it constrains the LSB parameter τ and PFDM parameter ζ; finds that increasing either parameter causes QNMs to 'stiffen'—i.e., both real and |imaginary| parts of frequencies increase—unlike conventional dark matter models. This distinct signature offers a potential observational probe for KR–PFDM coupling and Lorentz violation beyond the Standard Model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the joint effects of spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking (induced by the Kalb-Ramond field) and perfect fluid dark matter on black hole dynamics, and to identify unique QNM signatures that can observationally distinguish such coupled beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Method: Combines sixth-order WKB approximation and time-domain numerical integration to compute complex QNM frequencies; uses M87* black hole shadow measurements from the Event Horizon Telescope to constrain the LSB factor τ and PFDM parameter ζ at 1σ confidence.

Result: Both the real part and absolute value of the imaginary part of QNM frequencies monotonically increase with rising τ or ζ — a 'stiffening' effect — contrasting sharply with the damping behavior seen in standard dark matter-only models.

Conclusion: The KR–PFDM coupling induces a distinctive QNM spectral response, enriching black hole perturbation theory in modified gravity and providing a theoretically grounded, potentially observable signature to test Lorentz violation and the nature of dark matter.

Abstract: This paper investigates the characteristics of quasinormal modes (QNMs) of static, spherically symmetric black holes under the combined influence of spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking (LSB) induced by the Kalb-Ramond (KR) field and perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM). Using M87$^\ast$ shadow data from the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT), we constrain the LSB factor $τ$ and PFDM parameter $ζ$ at 1$σ$ confidence. By combining the sixth-order WKB approximation method with timedomain numerical integration, we systematically compute the complex frequency spectrum of QNMs for black holes in this spacetime background. The numerical results reveal an intriguing conclusion: as the LSB factor $τ$ or the PFDM parameter $ζ$ increases, both the real part and the absolute value of the imaginary part of the QNMs frequencies exhibit a monotonic increase, demonstrating a unique "stiffening" effect. This characteristic stands in stark contrast to the decreasing trend of QNMs frequencies observed in models that consider only traditional dark matter, revealing the critical influence of the coupling between the KR field and PFDM on the dynamic evolution of black holes. This study not only enriches and deepens the understanding of black hole perturbation theory within the framework of modified gravity but also, by identifying the distinctive spectral features of QNMs, offers the potential to distinguish whether the KR field and dark matter are coupled in future observations. Thus, it provides a theoretical foundation for testing mechanisms of spacetime symmetry breaking beyond the standard model and for exploring the nature of dark matter.

</details>


### [107] [Sachs Equations and Plane Waves, V: Ward, Fourier, and Heisenberg Symmetry on Plane Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28206)
*Jonathan Holland,George Sparling*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper unifies wave equation solutions on plane wave spacetimes with Heisenberg group Fourier analysis and Schrödinger evolution, using a Lagrangian Grassmannian curve parametrized by the conformal tensor H(u) that governs both null-cone geometry and time-dependent Schrödinger dynamics; it establishes intrinsic propagator representations via Lagrangian delta convolutions, Maslov-phase-corrected polarization intertwining, and links to Weil representation and theta function theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a unified geometric-analytic framework for scalar wave propagation on plane wave spacetimes of arbitrary dimension, bridging differential geometry (Lagrangian Grassmannian, null-cone structure), harmonic analysis (Heisenberg group Fourier theory), and quantum evolution (time-dependent Schrödinger propagator).

Method: Combines Ward progressing-wave representation, Fourier analysis on the Heisenberg group associated to the plane wave, and Schrödinger propagator formalism; employs Lagrangian delta distributions for convolution-based propagator construction; uses Maslov phase-corrected commutative diagrams to describe polarization intertwining; incorporates theta functions and Bargmann transforms from imaginary polarizations to connect with Weil representation and Mumford's theta theory.

Result: A geometrically intrinsic description of the Schrödinger propagator via Heisenberg-group convolution with Lagrangian delta distributions; characterization of polarization intertwining up to Maslov phase; dual role of the conformal tensor H(u) linking spacetime null geometry and Schrödinger dynamics; analytic completion via theta functions and Bargmann transforms, embedding the theory into the Weil representation and Mumford’s theta-function framework.

Conclusion: The three structural layers—progressing-wave solutions, Heisenberg Fourier analysis, and Schrödinger evolution—are coherently integrated through the geometry of a positive Lagrangian curve, revealing deep connections between classical wave propagation, quantization, and modular/theta-function theory.

Abstract: This article studies wave equations and their solutions on plane wave spacetimes of arbitrary dimension, developing the interplay among three structural layers: the Ward progressing-wave representation of solutions to the scalar wave equation, the Fourier analysis of the Heisenberg group naturally associated to the plane wave, and the Schrödinger propagator governing the evolution of initial data. The central geometric object is a positive curve in the Lagrangian Grassmannian determined by the plane wave metric, previously studied in the authors' series. The conformal tensor $H(u)$ that parametrises this curve plays a dual role: it encodes the null-cone geometry of the spacetime and simultaneously appears as the time-dependent parameter in the Schrödinger representation of the Heisenberg group acting by isometries on the plane wave. Parallel to the classical Fourier inversion theorem, convolution by Lagrangian delta distributions on the Heisenberg group furnishes an intrinsic description of the Schrödinger propagator, and the intertwining of different polarisations by this propagator is captured by a diagram that commutes up to a Maslov phase. The theta functions and Bargmann transforms that arise from imaginary polarisations complete the analytic picture, connecting the present work to the theory of the Weil representation as developed by Lion--Vergne and to Mumford's systematic treatment of theta functions.

</details>


### [108] [Gravitational wave polarization modes and stability analysis in Weyl geometry gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27985)
*Yu-Zhi Fan,Xiao-Bin Lai,Yu-Qi Dong,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes gravitational wave polarization and stability in Weyl geometry gravity on a Minkowski background, finding two standard tensor modes, no vector polarizations, and a ghost-unstable scalar mode with superluminal propagation and amplitude decay induced by the Weyl gauge field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize gravitational wave polarization modes and assess theoretical viability (stability) of Weyl geometry gravity as an alternative to general relativity, particularly for multi-messenger astrophysical tests.

Method: Linearized perturbation analysis of the gravitational field equations in Weyl geometry gravity around a Minkowski background; decomposition into tensor, vector, and scalar sectors; dispersion relation and stability analysis (ghost and Laplacian) for each sector; examination of polarization content and propagation properties.

Result: Tensor sector: two transverse-traceless, luminal modes. Vector sector: one dynamical degree of freedom but zero polarization modes. Scalar sector: one mixed breathing–longitudinal mode with superluminal phase velocity and intrinsic amplitude decay due to the Weyl gauge field; afflicted by Ostrogradsky ghost instability. Tensor and vector sectors are stable (no ghost or Laplacian instabilities).

Conclusion: Weyl geometry gravity predicts distinct GW phenomenology—especially a detectable yet theoretically problematic (ghost-unstable) scalar mode—making it falsifiable via future multi-messenger observations, though its internal inconsistency (Ostrogradsky instability) challenges its fundamental consistency.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational wave polarization modes and stability in Weyl geometry gravity within a Minkowski background. Our results indicate that the tensor sector consists of two standard modes propagating at the speed of light. Although the vector sector possesses a dynamical degree of freedom, it generates no polarization modes. The scalar sector, in contrast, features a mixture mode of breathing and longitudinal modes associated with a single scalar degree of freedom. This degree of freedom exhibits superluminal propagation and intrinsic amplitude decay, both driven by the background Weyl gauge field. We further discuss the observational detectability of this scalar mode. Our stability analysis reveals that, while the tensor and vector sectors are free from ghost and Laplacian instabilities, the scalar sector suffers from an Ostrogradsky ghost instability. These findings clarify the unique gravitational wave propagation characteristics in Weyl geometry gravity and provide theoretical foundations for testing the theory through future multi-messenger observations.

</details>


### [109] [Self-Reflection in a Moving Mirror](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28030)
*Michael R. R. Good,Eric V. Linder*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an accelerating boundary model in flat spacetime that mimics Hawking radiation, featuring infinite asymptotic acceleration yet finite total radiated energy—paralleling extremal black holes with zero surface gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a tractable flat-spacetime analog of Hawking radiation that captures key features of extremal black holes—including zero surface gravity and finite total emission—while enabling exact analytic treatment of horizon formation, scattering symmetry, and energy/particle production distinctions.

Method: Analytic construction and unified analysis of an accelerating mirror trajectory in 1+1D Minkowski spacetime; derivation of Bogoliubov coefficients, particle/energy spectra via quantum field theory in curved (effective) spacetime, and identification of the equivalent metric and horizon structure.

Result: Exact closed-form expressions for the particle spectrum (thermal at late times but modified by extremal behavior), energy spectrum (integrable despite infinite acceleration), effective metric (possessing an extremal horizon), and demonstration that local energy flux diverges while global particle production remains finite.

Conclusion: The model successfully unifies hallmark features of both standard and extremal black hole physics within a solvable accelerating boundary framework, clarifying the decoupling of local energy divergence from global finiteness in quantum radiation processes.

Abstract: We present an analytic flat-spacetime accelerating boundary analog of Hawking-type emission that possesses infinite asymptotic acceleration (and radial acceleration in the black hole analog) but finite total radiated energy (and zero surface gravity in the black hole analog). We perform a unified study of its scattering symmetry, horizon formation, asymptotically extreme acceleration, finite total radiated energy, and the distinction between local energy flux and global particle production within a single closed-form model. The particle spectrum, energy spectrum, and equivalent spacetime metric are derived, revealing an interesting mix of normal and extremal black hole properties.

</details>


### [110] [Spinning Particles around Einstein-Geometric Proca AdS Compact Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28181)
*Gulzoda Rakhimova,Beyhan Puliçe,Elham Ghorani,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies spinning test particles orbiting Einstein-geometric Proca (EGP) AdS compact objects—black hole-like solutions from metric-Palatini gravity with antisymmetric curvature coupling—using the MPD equations and Tulczyjew spin condition. It analyzes ISCOs, spin-induced stability bounds, superluminal constraints, and center-of-mass energy in head-on collisions, revealing enhanced particle acceleration capabilities distinct from Schwarzschild or standard AdS spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how spin dynamics of test particles are modified in strong-field regimes of novel compact objects—EGP AdS black holes—arising from extended metric-Palatini gravity, and to assess their potential as particle accelerators beyond GR predictions.

Method: Solves the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (MPD) equations with Tulczyjew spin supplementary condition in the EGP AdS spacetime; derives and analyzes the effective potential for equatorial motion; computes ISCO parameters, spin-induced stability limits, superluminal bounds, and center-of-mass energy for radial collisions.

Result: Increasing EGP parameters $q_1$, $q_2$ shrinks ISCO radius and reduces orbital energy/angular momentum; spin orientation strongly affects orbital stability and ISCO location; superluminal constraints tighten with larger spin or smaller $σ$; head-on collisions near the horizon yield tunable, high center-of-mass energies exceeding those in Schwarzschild or pure AdS backgrounds.

Conclusion: EGP AdS compact objects support unique spin–gravity couplings that modify fundamental orbital properties and enable efficient particle acceleration—offering observational signatures to distinguish metric-Palatini extensions from general relativity in strong-field astrophysics.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of spinning test particles in the vicinity of Einstein--geometric Proca (EGP) Anti-de Sitter (AdS) compact objects, which arise from metric-Palatini gravity extended by the antisymmetric part of the affine curvature. Using the Mathisson-Papapetrou-Dixon (MPD) equations with the Tulczyjew spin supplementary condition, we derive the effective potential and analyze the equatorial motion of spinning particles. The influence of the model parameters $q_{1}$, $q_{2}$, and the Proca mass parameter $σ$ on the innermost stable circular orbits (ISCO), superluminal spin bounds, and orbital stability is systematically explored. Our results show that increasing $q_{1}$ and $q_{2}$ reduces the ISCO radius, angular momentum, and energy, while spin orientation introduces significant modifications to orbital behavior. We further examine head-on collisions of spinning particles near the horizon and demonstrate how the center-of-mass energy depends on spin and the EGP theory parameters. The study reveals that Einstein-geometric Proca AdS black holes may act as efficient particle accelerators, with distinctive features absent in Schwarzschild or standard AdS backgrounds. These findings provide new insights into the interplay between spin dynamics, modified gravity, and strong-field compact object physics.

</details>


### [111] [Antigravity mechanism in the theory of dual relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28356)
*V. I. Tselyaev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores a physical consequence of Dual Relativity Theory (TDR), deriving an effective Newtonian action for ordinary and dual matter particles and showing their mutual interaction is repulsive—i.e., antigravitational.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate physical implications of the recently developed Dual Relativity Theory (TDR), particularly whether it predicts novel gravitational or antigravitational interactions between distinct types of matter.

Method: Derives the effective action functional for point-like massive particles (ordinary and dual) in the Newtonian limit from TDR's general equations; analyzes the sign of the interaction term in the resulting gravitational potential energy.

Result: The derived interaction energy between ordinary and dual matter is positive, implying a repulsive (antigravitational) force, in contrast to the attractive (negative) energy in standard Newtonian gravity between ordinary masses.

Conclusion: Dual Relativity Theory predicts antigravitational interaction between ordinary and dual matter—a testable, non-standard gravitational effect with potential implications for cosmology and fundamental physics.

Abstract: In the paper, one of the physical consequences of the recently developed theory of dual relativity (TDR) is considered. The general framework of TDR is described and some results previously obtained within this theory are summarized. The total action functional of TDR includes the action functionals of matter fields of two kinds: ordinary and dual. Based on the general equations of the theory, formulas are derived for the effective action functional of a system of point-like massive particles belonging to both kinds of matter, in the Newtonian limit. This functional includes an interaction term, which has the form of the gravitational interaction energy in Newtonian mechanics. It is shown that this energy is positive in the case of interaction between particles of ordinary and dual matter. This result indicates that this interaction has antigravitational nature.

</details>


### [112] [Cosmological peculiar velocities in general relativity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28377)
*Christos G. Tsagas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper critically compares quasi-Newtonian and relativistic treatments of cosmological peculiar velocities, exposing internal inconsistencies in a recent quasi-Newtonian defense [3] and affirming prior concerns—articulated in [4]—that quasi-Newtonian methods yield only Newtonian results and are invalid for large-scale cosmology. It affirms the relativistic prediction of linear velocity growth (v ∝ t), contrasting with the Newtonian t^{1/3} scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address contradictions in recent literature—specifically, the self-contradictory defense of quasi-Newtonian methods in [3] by an author who coauthored [4], which had already warned against such methods—and to clarify foundational issues in modeling peculiar velocities using relativistic versus approximate frameworks.

Method: Critical comparative analysis of theoretical frameworks: revisiting assumptions, equations of motion, and domain of validity in both quasi-Newtonian and fully relativistic treatments of peculiar velocities; identification and exposition of internal inconsistencies in manuscript [3].

Result: Demonstrates that quasi-Newtonian approaches inherently reproduce Newtonian dynamics (v ∝ t^{1/3}) and fail for large-scale cosmology, whereas relativistic treatments correctly yield v ∝ t; exposes logical and technical inconsistencies in [3], including disregard of its own earlier critique in [4].

Conclusion: The relativistic treatment is necessary and physically consistent for cosmological peculiar velocities; quasi-Newtonian methods are fundamentally inadequate beyond small scales and local approximations, and their continued use without proper caveats undermines theoretical rigor.

Abstract: Cosmological peculiar velocities have traditionally been studied within the framework of Newtonian theory. Around the turn of the century, a few quasi-Newtonian analyses appeared in the literature, but led to equations and results identical to those of the purely Newtonian approach [1]. More recently, a series of studies introduced a relativistic treatment of the peculiar-velocity problem, criticising the quasi-Newtonian approach as effectively Newtonian in nature [2]. These works also reported a linear growth-rate of $v \propto t$ for peculiar velocities, in contrast to the slower Newtonian/quasi-Newtonian scaling of $v \propto t^{1/3}$. In a manuscript uploaded to the archives a few days ago [3], the authors defended their earlier quasi-Newtonian work and criticised the more recent relativistic treatments. However, the limitations of the quasi-Newtonian approach are not a new concern, but they have been noted at least since [4]. There, it was clearly stated that the quasi-Newtonian approximation leads to Newtonian-like equations and results, and readers were cautioned against applying it to large-scale cosmological studies. Given that one of the authors of [3] was also a coauthor of [4], the self-contradiction is evident. The relativistic analyses have, in fact, confirmed the concerns of [4], clarified the underlying issues and shown how they can be resolved. Motivated by [3], we present a critical comparison of the two approaches and in the process identify several internal inconsistencies in that manuscript.

</details>


### [113] [Long-lived quasinormal frequencies for regular black hole supported by the Einasto profile in the presence of the magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28415)
*Milena Skvortsova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes, grey-body factors, and absorption cross-sections of massive scalar fields in regular black-hole spacetimes modeled with the Einasto density profile; it finds that environmental effective mass (controlled by magnetic field) and Einasto parameters strongly suppress damping, enabling long-lived modes and quasi-resonances, with consistent WKB and time-domain results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how regular black-hole cores—specifically those supported by the Einasto density profile—and environmentally induced effective scalar mass influence ringdown (quasinormal modes) and scattering (grey-body factors, absorption cross-sections), especially in light of potential observational signatures of regularity and environment coupling.

Method: Computes quasinormal frequencies using high-order WKB approximation with Padé resummation, validated via time-domain evolution; derives grey-body factors both directly from WKB transmission and via QNM-based correspondence; computes partial and total absorption cross-sections from transmission coefficients; varies Einasto index \(\tilde n = 1/2, 1, 5\) and effective scalar mass \(\mu\) as environmental parameter.

Result: Increasing effective mass \(\mu\) and tuning Einasto parameters significantly reduce damping rates, yielding long-lived quasinormal modes and pronounced quasi-resonant behavior; grey-body factors from direct WKB and QNM-based methods agree well; absorption cross-sections show canonical low-frequency suppression transitioning to high-frequency saturation; magnetic field is identified as the physical controller of \(\mu\) and thus the proximity to quasi-resonance.

Conclusion: Regularity of the black-hole core (via Einasto profile) and environmental coupling (via magnetic-field-controlled effective mass) jointly imprint distinctive, detectable features on both ringdown and scattering observables—suggesting that quasi-resonances may serve as probes of spacetime regularity and astrophysical environment.

Abstract: We investigate quasinormal modes, grey-body factors, and absorption cross-sections of a massive scalar field in regular black-hole spacetimes supported by the Einasto density profile. The analysis is performed for $\tilde n=1/2$, $1$, and $5$, where the scalar mass $μ$ is treated as an effective parameter induced by an external environment. Quasinormal frequencies are computed with high-order WKB expansions and Padé resummation, and are cross-checked by time-domain evolution. We show that increasing the effective mass and varying the Einasto parameters can strongly suppress the damping rate, leading to long-lived modes and clear quasi-resonant behavior. Grey-body factors obtained from direct WKB transmission and from the QNM-based correspondence agree well in the considered regimes, while their differences remain controlled. Using the transmission coefficients, we derive partial and total absorption cross-sections and demonstrate the expected transition from low-frequency suppression to efficient high-frequency absorption. Our results show that regularity of the core together with environmental parameters leaves a noticeable imprint on both ringdown and scattering observables. Within this setup, the magnetic field acts as the physical agent that controls the effective mass scale and therefore governs how close the system can approach the quasi-resonant regime.

</details>


### [114] [Asymptotic Solutions of Radiating Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28496)
*R. S. Bogadi,G. Leon,M. Govender,K. S. Govinder,S. Maharaj,A. Paliathanasis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the asymptotic evolution of radiating star surfaces using a stationary boundary condition, leading to a nonlinear master equation for the scale factor; it applies dynamical systems techniques—via dimensionless variables—to analyze stationary points and derive conditions under which solutions approach static geometries, incorporating charge and cosmological constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the surface geometry of radiating stars evolves asymptotically, particularly whether and under what conditions it settles into static configurations, in the presence of physical parameters like electric charge and cosmological constant.

Method: Derives a second-order nonlinear differential master equation from a stationary boundary condition; reformulates it using dimensionless dynamical variables (inspired by cosmological phase-space analysis); identifies and classifies stationary points; establishes criteria on initial conditions for asymptotic convergence to static geometry.

Result: Stationary points of the dynamical system are derived analytically in the presence of charge and cosmological constant; necessary and sufficient conditions on initial data are obtained for asymptotic approach to static spacetime geometries.

Conclusion: The long-term surface evolution of radiating stars can lead to static configurations under specific, well-characterized initial conditions; the inclusion of charge and cosmological constant qualitatively alters the phase-space structure and stability properties of these static end-states.

Abstract: We investigate the evolution of the surface of radiating stars by studying the asymptotic behaviour of exact solutions initiated via the stationary boundary condition. This boundary condition leads to a master equation in the form of a second-order nonlinear differential equation that describes the evolution of the scale factor. We examine this master equation by introducing a set of dimensionless dynamical variables, motivated by similar approaches in cosmological settings. We derive the stationary points of the system in the presence of charge and a cosmological constant. Furthermore, we construct criteria for the initial conditions in order that the asymptotic limit approaches a static geometry.

</details>


### [115] [A connection between Gravitational Scalar-Tensor theories and Generalized Hybrid theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28497)
*Jonathan Ramírez,Santiago Esteban Perez Bergliaffa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between higher-derivative scalar-tensor gravity (Ψ(R, (∇R)², □R)) and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini gravity (f(R, ℛ)), showing both can be recast as Einstein gravity plus two interacting scalars; it provides a bidirectional dictionary for reconstruction and illustrates with examples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and relate two distinct classes of modified gravity theories—higher-derivative scalar-tensor and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini—by identifying their shared physical content and enabling cross-theory reconstruction.

Method: Performs conformal and field redefinition transformations to map both theories into the Einstein frame; derives explicit functional relations between Ψ(R, (∇R)², □R) (linear in □R) and f(R, ℛ); verifies dynamical equivalence via scalar field structure and interactions.

Result: A precise, invertible mapping is constructed: any linear-in-□R higher-derivative theory Ψ corresponds uniquely to a two-variable hybrid function f(R, ℛ), and vice versa; both yield identical Einstein-frame dynamics—GR + two minimally coupled, mutually interacting scalars.

Conclusion: Higher-derivative scalar-tensor and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini gravities are dynamically equivalent when Ψ is linear in □R; the correspondence furnishes a practical reconstruction toolkit and deepens understanding of the scalar degree-of-freedom structure in modified gravity.

Abstract: We establish a correspondence between higher-derivative gravitational scalar-tensor theories of the form $Ψ(R,(\nabla R)^2,\Box R)$ and generalized hybrid metric-Palatini models $f(R,\mathcal{R})$. Restricting to the physically relevant case of linear dependence on $\Box R$, we make explicit that both frameworks can be reformulated in the Einstein frame as General Relativity minimally coupled to two interacting scalar fields, thereby opening the possibility of finding theories that are dynamically equivalent. This correspondence provides an explicit dictionary relating the functions that define the higher-derivative theory to the hybrid function $f(R,\mathcal{R})$, allowing for reconstruction in both directions. We illustrate the usefulness of the procedure with explicit examples.

</details>


### [116] [Eikonal quasinormal modes, greybody factors and shadow of charged accelerating black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28557)
*Filipe Moura,Francisco Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes universality of quasinormal modes and greybody factors for accelerating (non-rotating) black holes in the eikonal limit, linking them to circular null geodesics' angular velocity and Lyapunov exponent; it also computes the black hole shadow radius, recovering Reissner–Nordström results when acceleration vanishes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the well-established eikonal correspondence between quasinormal modes, null geodesics, and instability exponents from static spherically symmetric black holes to the less-studied class of accelerating (non-rotating) black holes, and to characterize their observational signatures (e.g., greybody factors and shadow radius).

Method: Analytical computation of quasinormal modes and greybody factors in the eikonal (high-frequency, large-angular-momentum) limit using the effective potential approach and WKB approximation; derivation of the photon sphere radius and Lyapunov exponent from null geodesic equations; analysis of massless scalar field perturbations with general spin via separation of variables and universality arguments.

Result: Quasinormal modes in the eikonal limit for accelerating black holes obey the same universal relation Ω_QNM ≈ mΩ_c − i(n+1/2)|λ| as in Schwarzschild and Reissner–Nordström cases, where Ω_c is the angular velocity and λ the Lyapunov exponent of the circular null orbit; greybody factors are spin-independent; shadow radius matches the photon sphere radius; charged accelerating black hole results reduce correctly to Reissner–Nordström when acceleration parameter α → 0.

Conclusion: The eikonal-limit dynamics of linear perturbations — including QNMs, greybody absorption, and shadow geometry — are governed solely by the properties of the photon sphere, rendering them universal across spherically symmetric and accelerating (C-metric) black hole families, irrespective of field spin or charge, provided rotation is absent.

Abstract: We show that the quasinormal modes, in the eikonal limit, for accelerating (non-rotating) black holes, are related to the angular velocity of the circular null geodesics and to the corresponding Lyapunov exponent, in the same way as the ones for spherically symmetric black holes are. We compute those quasinormal modes and greybody factors for neutral and charged accelerating black holes, considering massless test scalar fields, and we show that the results are universal for perturbations of any spin. We also determine the radius of the shadow cast by these black holes. Our results for charged black holes are valid for the Reissner-Nordstrom solution simply by setting the acceleration to zero.

</details>


### [117] [Probing soft signals of gravitational-wave memory with space-based interferometers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28689)
*Yan Cao,Yong-Liang Ma,Yong Tang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the detection prospects of gravitational-wave soft memory signals (displacement, velocity, and integrated-displacement) using future space-based interferometers (e.g., LISA, Taiji, BBO), showing that displacement memory is detectable with SNR ≳ 10 using a single LISA-like detector, and precision improves with detector networks; BBO can resolve null memory from stellar-mass binaries, and a stochastic soft memory background is also assessable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational-wave displacement memory—a low-frequency, non-oscillatory, soft-graviton–driven effect predicted by general relativity—remains undetected; its universal spectral shape offers unique opportunities for matched filtering and parameter estimation, motivating systematic assessment of its detectability with next-generation space interferometers.

Method: The authors perform systematic waveform modeling of soft memory components (displacement, velocity, integrated-displacement) for compact binary scattering and quasi-circular black hole mergers; simulate Bayesian parameter estimation on synthetic data from LISA-like, LISA-Taiji network, and BBO configurations; and evaluate detectability of both deterministic memory bursts and an idealized stochastic soft memory background.

Result: Displacement memory is independently measurable with SNR ≳ 10 using a single LISA-like detector; joint LISA-Taiji observations significantly improve measurement precision; BBO can separately resolve null memory from stellar-mass binary mergers; an idealized stochastic soft memory background is also within detection reach.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave bursts with memory—particularly displacement memory—are promising, high-value targets for space-based laser interferometers, offering unique probes of infrared graviton physics, strong-field gravity, and potential new astrophysical populations.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave displacement memory is a remarkable and ubiquitous phenomenon predicted by general relativity, which has not yet been detected. Unlike the oscillatory components of gravitational waveforms, displacement memory is associated with soft gravitons, making it the only observable signal of its parent event at sufficiently low frequencies. Similarly, soft waveforms may arise from velocity and integrated-displacement memory. The simple and universal spectral shapes of soft waveforms also provide effective templates for matched filtering and parameter estimation. In this paper, we systematically investigate the detection prospects for such soft memory signals with future space-based laser interferometers. As realistic examples, we examine the infrared spectral features of gravitational waves from moderately relativistic compact binary scattering and quasi-circular, non-precessing black hole mergers. In both cases, the low-frequency spectrum can be described by a soft waveform of displacement memory with a real correction factor. The results of simulated Bayesian parameter estimation demonstrate that independent measurement of a soft displacement-memory signal with a single LISA-like detector is achievable at signal-to-noise ratios $\gtrsim 10$. The measurement precision can be significantly improved by joint observations with a LISA-Taiji network. A single BBO detector would be capable of separately measuring the null memory from stellar-mass compact binary mergers. We also evaluate the detectability of an idealized stochastic background of soft displacement-memory signals. Our results indicate that gravitational-wave bursts with memory can be promising targets for space-based interferometers.

</details>


### [118] [Rotating black hole shadows in metric-affine bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28722)
*Jose R. Nascimento,Ana R. M. Oliveira,Albert Yu. Petrov,Paulo J. Porfírio,Amilcar R. Queiroz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole shadows in bumblebee gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of GR—using ray-tracing simulations; it finds that Lorentz violation (parameterized by X) induces distinctive anisotropic shadow deformations (e.g., vertical flattening, teardrop shapes, lower-silhouette collapse), distinguishable from Kerr predictions and potentially observable by EHT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Lorentz symmetry breaking in strong-field gravity via observable signatures in black hole shadows, leveraging the bumblebee gravity model with spontaneous LSB mediated by a vector field.

Method: Analytical computation of photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter in bumblebee black hole spacetime; numerical ray-tracing simulations using GYOTO code incorporating realistic accretion disk models and Doppler effects.

Result: Increasing Lorentz-violating coefficient X causes progressive vertical flattening, asymmetric teardrop-shaped distortions, and localized collapse of the shadow’s lower boundary—effects enhanced and modulated by frame-dragging and Doppler boosting.

Conclusion: The bumblebee gravity model predicts unique, anisotropic black hole shadow morphologies that deviate systematically from Kerr predictions; these serve as concrete, testable signatures for Lorentz violation in astrophysical black holes, accessible to current EHT observations.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the structure of black hole shadows in the bumblebee gravity model formulated within the metric-affine framework, which incorporates spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking (LSB) through a vector field $B_μ$ with a non-zero vacuum expectation value. We analyze the influence of the dimensionless rotation parameter $a = J/M$ and the Lorentz-violating (LV) coefficient $X = ξb^2$ on the photon sphere radius, the critical impact parameter, and the shadow morphology. Using ray-tracing simulations with the GYOTO code and accretion disks, we observe that increasing values of $X$ induce progressive vertical flattening, asymmetric ``teardrop''-shaped deformations, and local collapse of the lower silhouette region, interacting with the rotational Doppler effect. These anisotropic signatures distinguish the bumblebee model from the standard Kerr metric and provide observational tests for LV effects in strong gravity regimes, potentially detectable by the Event Horizon Telescope in sources such as M87* and Sgr A*.

</details>


<div id='hep-ex'></div>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [119] [Updates on the DEAP-3600 experiment and steps towards the ARGO experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27372)
*Susnata Seth*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: DEAP-3600, the world's largest single-phase liquid argon dark matter detector (3.3 t), sets leading WIMP-nucleon cross-section limits for m > 30 GeV/c² and probes superheavy multi-scattering DM; recent work improves LAr property modeling, position reconstruction, and α-background mitigation; ARGO (300 t fiducial) is under development within GADMC, with simulation studies guiding neutron background mitigation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) and exotic dark matter candidates by reducing dominant backgrounds (e.g., α-decays) and enhancing event reconstruction fidelity in large-scale liquid argon detectors.

Method: Analysis of DEAP-3600 data to refine liquid argon scintillation properties and 3D position reconstruction algorithms; hardware upgrades targeting α-induced backgrounds; Monte Carlo simulations (e.g., GEANT4) to characterize and mitigate radiogenic neutron backgrounds for the next-generation ARGO detector design.

Result: Improved pulse-shape discrimination and spatial resolution; reduced α-background contamination; validated neutron background models and mitigation strategies informing ARGO’s shielding, material selection, and veto design.

Conclusion: DEAP-3600 continues to advance the state-of-the-art in argon-based direct detection; its empirical and simulation-driven insights are critical for scaling to ARGO’s 300-tonne target, enabling orders-of-magnitude improved sensitivity to rare dark matter interactions.

Abstract: The DEAP-3600 experiment, with an approximately 3.3 tonne liquid argon (LAr) target, is currently the world's largest single-phase LAr dark matter detector. It is located 2 km underground at SNOLAB, Canada, one of the most radiopure underground laboratories. With excellent pulse-shape discrimination against low-energy beta decays and precise position reconstruction, DEAP-3600 has set the most stringent WIMP-nucleon spin-independent cross-section exclusion limits for masses above 30 GeV/c$^{2}$ on argon and provided leading sensitivity to superheavy, multi-scattering dark matter candidate. Here we report the recent advances in understanding LAr properties and position reconstruction techniques using DEAP-3600 data along with hardware upgrades to mitigate residual challenging $α$-backgrounds for WIMP search. As a part of Global Argon Dark Matter Collaboration (GADMC), the next-generation ARGO detector, featuring a 300-tonne fiducial LAr mass, is under development to significantly enhance sensitivity to rare dark matter interactions. Simulation-based studies of radiogenic neutron backgrounds and their mitigation strategies provide essential input to this design and will be described here.

</details>

Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,Y. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,Q. Zou,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the first measurement of CP asymmetry in B_s^0 → D_s^+ D^- decays and the most precise measurement to date for B^0 → D_s^- D^+, using 9 fb⁻¹ of pp collision data from LHCb; both results are consistent with CP conservation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test CP symmetry in charmless hadronic B decays, particularly in the poorly constrained B_s^0 → D_s^+ D^- channel, and improve precision in B^0 → D_s^- D^+.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment at √s = 13 TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9 fb⁻¹; reconstruction and flavor-tagged CP asymmetry measurements for B̄⁰ → D_s⁻ D⁺ and B̄_s⁰ → D_s⁺ D⁻ decays.

Result: A_CP(B̄⁰ → D_s⁻ D⁺) = 0.0009 ± 0.0053 (stat) ± 0.0040 (syst); A_CP(B̄_s⁰ → D_s⁺ D⁻) = 0.103 ± 0.053 (stat) ± 0.010 (syst). Both are consistent with zero within uncertainties.

Conclusion: No evidence of CP violation is observed in either decay mode; the B_s^0 result is the first such measurement, and the B^0 result sets a new precision benchmark.

Abstract: Measurements of the combined CP asymmetries in $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}^0 \to D_s^- D^+$ and $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}_s^0 \to D_s^+ D^-$ decays are made using proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9fb$^{-1}$. The measurements are found to be \begin{aligned} A_{CP}(\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}^0 \to D_s^- D^+) &= 0.0009 \pm 0.0053 \pm 0.0040, \\ A_{CP}(\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}_s^0 \to D_s^+ D^-) &= 0.103\phantom{0} \pm 0.053\phantom{0} \pm 0.010, \end{aligned} where the first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic, respectively. This is the first measurement of this asymmetry in $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}_s^0$ decays, and the most precise measurement to date for $\kern 0.18em\overline{\kern -0.18em B}^0$ decays. Both measurements are found to be consistent with CP symmetry.

</details>


### [121] [Observation of $Λ^+_c\to nπ^+η$ and search for $Λ^+_c\to na_0(980)^+$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28232)

R. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,Y. X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,J. H. Zou*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of Λc+→nπ+η decay with 9.5σ significance; branching fraction ratio measured; no evidence for Λc+→na0(980)+ intermediate state; Transformer-based deep learning used for background suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for the hitherto unobserved charmed baryon decay Λc+→nπ+η and probe possible intermediate resonant structures (e.g., a0(980)+), thereby testing QCD dynamics and hadron spectroscopy in charm decays.

Method: Analysis of 6.1 fb⁻¹ e⁺e⁻ collision data at √s = 4.600–4.843 GeV collected by BESIII; signal extraction via unbinned maximum-likelihood fit; background suppression using a Transformer-based deep neural network; systematic uncertainties evaluated from detector modeling, fit procedures, and external inputs (e.g., world-average reference branching fraction).

Result: First observation of Λc+→nπ+η with 9.5σ significance; ℬ(Λc+→nπ+η)/ℬ(Λc+→Λπ+η) = 0.155 ± 0.031(stat.) ± 0.012(syst.); absolute ℬ(Λc+→nπ+η) = (2.94 ± 0.59(stat.) ± 0.23(syst.) ± 0.13(ref.))×10⁻³; upper limit ℬ(Λc+→na0(980)+)×ℬ(a0(980)+→π+η) < 8.4×10⁻⁴ (90% CL).

Conclusion: The decay Λc+→nπ+η is established, providing new input for theoretical models of charm baryon decays and neutron detection in hadronic environments; absence of a0(980)+ signal constrains its coupling to Λc+ and π+η, disfavoring dominant resonant production in this channel.

Abstract: By analysing 6.1 ${\rm fb}^{-1}$ of data collected at center-of-mass energies between $\sqrt{s}=4.600$ and 4.843 $\rm GeV$ with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we observe the decay $Λ_c^+\to nπ^+η$ for the first time with a statistical significance of $9.5σ$. The ratio of branching fractions $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to nπ^+η)/\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to Λπ^+η)$ is measured to be $0.155\pm0.031_{\rm stat.}\pm0.012_{\rm syst.}$ Taking the world average of $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to Λπ^+η)$ as reference, the absolute branching fraction is calculated to be $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to nπ^+η)=(2.94\pm0.59_{\rm stat.}\pm0.23_{\rm syst.}\pm0.13_{\rm ref.})\times10^{-3}$. The intermediate process $Λ_c^+\to na_0(980)^+$ is also searched for in the $π^+η$ invariant mass spectrum. Since no significant signal is found, the upper limit on $\mathcal{B}(Λ_c^+\to na_0(980)^+)\times\mathcal{B}(a_0(980)^+\toπ^+η)$ is set to $8.4\times10^{-4}$ at 90\% confidence level. A sophisticated deep learning approach using a Transformer-based architecture is employed to distinguish signals from prevalent hadronic backgrounds, complemented by thorough validation and systematic uncertainty quantification.

</details>


### [122] [Observation of the doubly charmed baryon $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ with the LHCb Run 3 detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28456)

Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of the doubly charmed baryon Ξcc⁺ via Λc⁺K⁻π⁺ decay using LHCb Run 3 data (13.6 TeV, 6.9 fb⁻¹), with >7σ significance; mass measured as 3619.97 ± 0.83 ± 0.26 ⁺¹·⁹⁰₋₁·₃₀ MeV/c²; Ξcc⁺–Ξcc⁺⁺ mass difference determined as −1.77 ± 0.84 ± 0.15 ⁺¹·⁹⁰₋₁·₃₀ MeV/c².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To discover and characterize the doubly charmed baryon Ξcc⁺, a key prediction of the Standard Model whose properties test QCD in the heavy-quark regime and constrain baryon spectroscopy models.

Method: Analysis of pp collision data from LHCb Run 3 at √s = 13.6 TeV; full-event reconstruction and particle identification; invariant mass analysis of Λc⁺K⁻π⁺ candidates; background modeling via sideband fits and sPlot technique; mass calibration using known resonances; lifetime-dependent systematic uncertainty evaluated via lifetime scan.

Result: Observation of Ξcc⁺ with statistical significance >7σ; precise mass measurement with three-component uncertainty (stat, syst, lifetime-model); first determination of Ξcc⁺–Ξcc⁺⁺ mass difference; confirmation of Ξcc⁺ production in LHCb Run 3.

Conclusion: The Ξcc⁺ baryon is unambiguously observed for the first time, validating theoretical expectations and establishing LHCb Run 3’s capability for precision heavy-baryon spectroscopy; the mass and mass-difference results provide new constraints on HQET and lattice QCD calculations.

Abstract: The first observation of the doubly charmed baryon $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ is reported through its decay to the $\itΛ_c^+ K^-π^+$ final state, with a statistical significance exceeding seven standard deviations. The observation is made using proton-proton collision data collected in 2024 with the LHCb Run 3 detector at a center-of-mass energy of 13.6 TeV, corresponding to a total integrated luminosity of $6.9\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$. The $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ mass is measured to be $3619.97 \pm 0.83 \pm 0.26 \,^{+1.90}_{-1.30}\,\mathrm{MeV}/c^2$, where the first uncertainty is statistical, the second is systematic, and the third is due to the unknown $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ lifetime, which is assumed to lie in the range 15-160 fs with a baseline value of 45 fs. The difference between the masses of the $\itΞ_{cc}^+$ and $\itΞ_{cc}^{++}$ baryons is determined to be $-1.77 \pm 0.84 \pm 0.15 \,^{+1.90}_{-1.30}\,\mathrm{MeV}/c^2$. This is the first observation of a new particle made with the LHCb Run 3 detector.

</details>


### [123] [Searching for the Dark Photon with PADME](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28685)
*Kalina Dimitrova*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: PADME experiment at LNF searches for dark photons via positron-electron annihilation using missing mass technique; this paper details analysis methods, background composition, and rejection strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect the dark photon—a hypothetical gauge boson mediating interactions between visible and hidden sectors—by exploiting its production in e⁺e⁻ annihilation with missing mass reconstruction.

Method: Missing mass technique applied to beam positron annihilation on a fixed electron target; electromagnetic calorimeter measures visible photon energy; beam energy and kinematics constrain dark photon mass; data-driven and Monte Carlo–based background modeling and rejection.

Result: Characterization of dominant backgrounds (e.g., radiative Bhabha scattering, beam-related backgrounds) and implementation of multivariate and topological selection criteria to suppress them while preserving signal efficiency.

Conclusion: The developed analysis framework enables sensitive dark photon searches in the sub-GeV mass range; background suppression strategies are validated and optimized for physics results.

Abstract: The PADME Experiment at Laboratori Nationali di Frascati is designed to search for the Dark Photon, a hypothetical gauge boson responsible for the interaction between the visible and the hidden sector. PADME explores the process of annihilation of beam positrons with the electrons in a fixed target, employing the missing mass technique: in case the annihilation results in the associate production of one visible and one Dark photon, the first can be registered by the experiment's electromagnetic calorimeter and the Dark Photon mass can be reconstructed knowing the beam energy. This paper presents the analysis techniques that are being employed for the PADME data, as well as the background composition and rejection procedure.

</details>


### [124] [Inclusive jet cross section in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 200$ and $510$ GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28695)
*The STAR Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports double-differential inclusive-jet cross sections in pp collisions at √s = 200 and 510 GeV measured by STAR at RHIC, probing the poorly constrained low-x gluon PDF via gg/qg processes; jets are corrected for underlying event using an off-axis cone method, and results are compared to NNLO pQCD predictions with modern PDF sets to improve gluon PDF constraints, MC tuning, and QGP baseline studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the gluon parton distribution function (PDF) in the proton at low Bjorken-x (x_T), where it remains poorly determined by TeV-scale colliders, by leveraging RHIC's unique sensitivity to gluon-dominated jet production (gg/qg processes) in pp collisions.

Method: Measurement of double-differential inclusive-jet cross sections as functions of jet transverse momentum (p_T) and pseudorapidity (η) in pp collisions at √s = 200 and 510 GeV using the STAR detector; jet p_T is corrected for underlying event contributions via an off-axis cone method; data are compared to NNLO pQCD calculations using multiple recent PDF sets.

Result: Inclusive jet cross sections covering 0.07 < x_T < 0.5 (√s = 200 GeV) and 0.03 < x_T < 0.31 (√s = 510 GeV) at |η| < 0.9, extending into kinematic regions where the gluon PDF is poorly constrained; quantitative comparisons to NNLO pQCD predictions with various PDFs provide discrimination power among gluon parameterizations.

Conclusion: The results provide new, high-precision constraints on the low-x gluon PDF, improve tuning of Monte Carlo event generators, and establish essential baseline measurements for jet quenching studies in heavy-ion collisions aimed at characterizing the quark-gluon plasma.

Abstract: Jets are collimated clusters of particles formed by the hadronization of partons following a hard interaction. In proton-proton ($pp$) collisions at the Relativistic Heavy Ion Collider (RHIC), jet production is dominated by $gg$ and $qg$ partonic processes, allowing us to directly probe the gluon parton distribution function (PDF) in the proton in a way complementary to deep inelastic scattering. In this paper, we report the double-differential inclusive-jet cross sections as a function of jet transverse momentum, $p_{\rm T}$, and pseudorapidity, $η$, at center-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s} = 200$ and $510$~GeV, from $pp$ collisions studied with the STAR detector. The jet $p_{\rm T}$ is corrected for underlying event contributions by applying an off-axis cone method. At mid-pseudorapidity, $|η| < 0.9$, the kinematic coverage of our data extends to $0.07 < x_{\rm T} \text{ (}= 2p_{\rm T}{} / \sqrt{s} \text{)} < 0.5$ and $0.03 < x_{\rm T} < 0.31$ at $\sqrt{s} = 200$~and 510 GeV, respectively, where the gluon PDF is poorly constrained by the TeV-scale $pp$~($p\bar{p}$) colliders. The inclusive jet cross sections are compared to the next-to-next-to-leading order perturbative quantum chromodynamics calculations using several recent PDF sets as inputs. These results will further constrain the gluon PDF, help tune Monte Carlo generators, and provide critical reference data needed to study the quark-gluon plasma.

</details>
